<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-level9-necessary-word-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## family_b_level9_necessary_word_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/family_b_level9_necessary_word_2026-09-22.md`.

### Family B has a forced lower level-nine word

22 September 2026. This is a local necessary condition, not an exclusion of Family B or the `(1,-3)` orbit.

In the September 13 Family B wake table,

```text
W=6m+4,
O={0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W},
2<=p<r<=m-1,  p+r odd.
```

Hence `6p+2>=14`, `6r+4>=22`, and `W-2>=26`, so `omega_9=0`. The lower cap has level six `ER,EL,ER` and two possible level-eight words, as proved in `odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_six_2026-09-12.md`:

```text
8A: ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER
8B: WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,WL,ER.
```

Both level-eight words have exactly four north departures (`EL` once, `WR` three times) and no south arrivals. Level six has no east departures and no west arrivals. Directional balance into odd level nine therefore gives four `NR`, zero `NL`, zero `SR`, and four `SL` after its non-wake right/left balance. Alternating turns starting with right force the unique word

```text
9: NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL.
```

The checker `code/python/verify_family_b_level9_word.py` runs `p2_research.row_options` and exhaustive `stable_words` separately for both level-eight words and gets exactly one count row and this one word in each branch. It also checks that this extension satisfies the local physical first-return condition. At level ten, the same raw count enumeration finds 32 words per branch; this note makes no claim about their later continuation or global tour connectivity.

<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-lower-level10-restriction-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## family_b_lower_level10_restriction_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/family_b_lower_level10_restriction_2026-09-22.md`.

### A row-ten restriction in lower `(1,2,2)` Family B

22 September 2026. This is an all-period **necessary prefix restriction** at
drift `(1,-3)`. It does not exclude Family B or close the drift orbit.

Family B has `W=6m+4` and wakes

```text
{0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W},
2<=p<r<=m-1,  p+r odd.
```

The first later wake is at least level fourteen. In particular, levels nine
and ten are non-wake. The lower-cap return argument fixes rows zero through
seven, and gives two row-eight words:

```text
8A: ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER
8B: WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,WL,ER.
```

Both force `9: (NR,SL)^4`, as checked separately in
`code/python/verify_family_b_level9_word.py`. Flow counts and R-first
alternation then allow exactly 32 row-ten words after either row-eight word.
All 64 pass the local physical first-return test. Exact lifted-clock
feasibility eliminates 24 of the 32 after 8A and 15 of the 32 after 8B:

| Row eight | Raw row-ten words | Bad physical return | Bad lifted clock | Retained |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 8A | 32 | 0 | 24 | 8 |
| 8B | 32 | 0 | 15 | 17 |

The retained words are listed in `work_family_b_global/lower_level10_restriction.json`. For each
finite word, the checker solves integer equations on every known successor
edge. In lower-cap coordinates an east or west edge of voltage `v=+1` or
`-1` imposes `tau(target)=tau(source)+1-N*v`; vertical edges have `v=0`.
Successive stable visits on a row have time gap at least four. It also imposes
`N>=max(6, number of displayed events)`, since those events have distinct
phases in any full period. These are necessary conditions for any period,
with no upper bound on `N` or `W`. They are not sufficient for a full tour.

Run `python work_family_b_global/verify_lower10_restriction.py` from the
repository root. The script uses exhaustive `row_options` and
`stable_words` from `code/python/p2_research.py`, verifies the unique row-nine
word again, checks every row-ten first return, and writes the result JSON.
The row-eight normal form and completeness of the stable-word framework are
the hand prerequisites; the checker is a finite certificate, not a formal
proof of those prerequisites.

This does not create a cross-cap contradiction. In particular, the upper
`210` branch still requires `r=m-1` from its separate offset-six
restriction. Then `p+m` is even and the closest wake beyond upper offset six
is at least inward offset fourteen. Both paired upper row-five/six patterns
retain prefixes with exact integer clocks even when `N>=1,000,000`, as
`work_family_b_global/check_upper_large_period.py` confirms on saved row-ten states. This tests
only prefix equations; it rules out a small upper period bound from those
particular prefixes, not every later-period argument.

<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-lower-row12-er1-restriction-2026-09-26-md"></a>

## family_b_lower_row12_er1_restriction_2026-09-26

Source: `research-notes/family_b_lower_row12_er1_restriction_2026-09-26.md`.

### Family B lower row twelve with one `ER`

26 September 2026. This is an all-period necessary-prefix restriction for
Family B at drift `(1,-3)`. It does not exclude Family B or assert that any
retained prefix extends to a complete highway.

Family B has `W=6m+4` and wakes
`{0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W}` with `2<=p<r<=m-1` and `p+r` odd.
Consequently rows nine through thirteen are non-wake. The established lower
normal form gives the two row-eight words A and B and the unique row-nine
word `(NR,SL)^4`. Exact lifted clocks retain 8 row-ten words after A and 17
after B.

Suppose lower row twelve has **exactly one `ER`**. Then its port counts
`(ER,EL,WR,WL)` must be `(1,7,9,3)`. The row-ten word is one of only four:

| Row eight | Row-ten index | Row-ten word | Remaining row-eleven/twelve prefixes |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| A | 3 | `WR EL WR WL WR WL WR WL WR EL WR WL` | 6 |
| A | 4 | `WR EL WR WL WR WL WR WL WR WL WR EL` | 7 |
| A | 7 | `WR EL WR WL WR WL ER WL` | 6 |
| B | 7 | `WR WL WR EL WR WL WR WL WR WL WR EL` | 2 |

The verifier `code/python/verify_family_b_row12_er1_all.py` regenerates the 25 row-ten
words from the checked normal form, then enumerates all 110 directional count
rows on row eleven. Ordinal matching and one-excursion same-cell returns
reduce the row-eleven words to 1,150; 112 have an explicit finite wrong first
return, and 473 of the rest fail the exact lifted integer clock. Thus 565
row-eleven prefixes remain. At row twelve, the `ER=1` count vector is unique
above each prefix. One-excursion adjacency leaves 532 words; explicit finite
wrong returns reject 272, and exact clocks reject 239 of the rest. Exactly 21
necessary prefixes remain, distributed as in the table.

Each clock uses an unbounded integer period `N`, the edge equation
`tau(v)-tau(u)=1-N*voltage`, and stable-row time gaps at least four. No period
or width cutoff, wake property beyond row twelve, or quotient-cycle return
extrapolation is used. The finite return checks reject only when an explicit
walk reaches the same physical cell at the wrong next stable event. The
verifier depends on the established lower-cap normal form and on the
`p2_research.py` ordinal successor and adjacency routines. It regenerates its
row-ten and row-eleven prerequisites without loading scratch data.

The 21 retained prefixes are enumerated in
`results/family_b_row12_er1_all_2026-09-26.json`. They pass these necessary checks; their
existence rules out a claim that `ER>=2` follows from the present prefix
argument.

<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-lower-row12-er-positive-2026-09-24-md"></a>

## family_b_lower_row12_er_positive_2026-09-24

Source: `research-notes/family_b_lower_row12_er_positive_2026-09-24.md`.

### Family B requires an east-right event on lower row twelve

24 September 2026. This is an all-period necessary prefix restriction at
drift `(1,-3)`. Family B and the full drift orbit remain open.

Family B has width `W=6m+4` and wakes

```text
{0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W},
2<=p<r<=m-1,  p+r odd.
```

The first later wake is at least level fourteen, so lower rows nine through
twelve are non-wake. The established lower-cap return analysis gives two row
eight words, a unique row nine, and 25 row-ten words after exact lifted-clock
filtering: eight after row-eight A and seventeen after B. One of the latter
has no row-eleven continuation by the separate fixed-return argument, leaving
24 viable row-ten words.

**Claim.** Every Family B tour has at least one `ER` event on lower row twelve.

For each of the 25 row-ten candidates, the verifier derives every non-wake
row-eleven count row and enumerates R-first alternating words satisfying
one-excursion, zero-voltage first-return adjacency. There are 339,024 raw
alternating row-eleven words, 1,150 after that adjacency condition, and 1,038
after the full physical first-return check. Exact integer clocks, checked
separately in the preceding row-eleven analysis, leave 565 of those 1,038;
the present exclusion does not need that row-eleven clock filter.

If row twelve contains no `ER`, directional flow and non-wake balance force
one of only three count vectors, shown as `(ER,EL,WR,WL)`:

```text
(0,8,12,4), (0,8,16,8), (0,8,20,12).
```

Assume `ER=0`. Then every south departure from row twelve is `WL`.
For **every** one of the 1,150 row-eleven adjacency candidates, one of those
`WL` departures has a four-step path of the form

```text
12:WL_i -> 11:SR -> 8:WR -> 9:NR -> 12:E_j,
       i in {0,1,2}, j in {0,2,4}.
```

Here `i` is an outgoing-south ordinal on row twelve and `j` is an incoming-east
ordinal. The verifier checks the actual ordinal successor path separately
for every candidate; it does not assert that every displayed `(i,j)` pair
occurs. The path has net horizontal voltage zero and no intermediate row-twelve
event, so it is the first return to the same physical cell after `WL_i`.
Consequently the `E_j` event must immediately follow `WL_i` in stable order.
Yet `ER=0` makes every east-arrival event on row twelve `EL`. Both `WL_i` and
`EL_j` are left turns, impossible as
consecutive events in the alternating word. The checker independently
enumerates the zero-`ER` row-twelve count
rows and finds zero adjacency words. Thus none of the 1,038 fully physical or
565 clock-feasible row-eleven prefixes can lead to a zero-`ER` row twelve.
No period or width cutoff is used.

Run `python code/python/verify_family_b_row12_er.py` from the repository
root. It recomputes the 25 row-ten words with
`code/python/verify_family_b_row10_words.py`, independently rebuilds
the row-eleven adjacency and physical frontiers, and writes
`results/family_b_row12_er_positive_2026-09-24.json`. The checker uses the
stable-word and return machinery in `code/python/p2_research.py`; the
earlier hand lower-cap normal form and the row-ten word certificate remain
its prerequisites.

This does not force a unique row-twelve word or exclude Family B. A separate
necessary-condition scan still retains row-twelve candidates above each of
the 24 previously viable row-ten words; those candidates are not certified
full tours.

<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-row10-pattern-exclusion-2026-09-23-md"></a>

## family_b_row10_pattern_exclusion_2026-09-23

Source: `research-notes/family_b_row10_pattern_exclusion_2026-09-23.md`.

### One Family B lower row-ten word has no continuation

23 September 2026. This is an all-period exclusion of **one word**, not of
Family B or the `(1,-3)` drift orbit.

Take lower row eight B from the proved two-word normal form and the forced
non-wake row nine:

```text
8:  WR EL WR WL ER WL WR WL ER
9:  NR SL NR SL NR SL NR SL
10: ER EL WR WL WR WL WR WL
```

The row-ten word is one of the seventeen integer-clock-feasible B words in
`family_b_lower_level10_restriction_2026-09-22.md`. Family B has no wake at
row eleven. Directional balance and R-first alternation allow four possible
row-eleven count rows:

| NR | NL | SR | SL |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 4 | 0 | 6 | 10 |
| 3 | 1 | 5 | 7 |
| 2 | 2 | 4 | 4 |
| 1 | 3 | 3 | 1 |

Write `W6` for the sixth row-eleven west departure in stable order and `N1`
for its first north arrival. Ordinal successor matching fixes the following
path, independently of the still unknown row-eleven word:

```text
11:W6 -> 8:WL8 -> 7:SR5 -> 4:WR5 -> 5:NL4 -> 2:WL2
      -> 1:SL2 -> 4:EL6 -> 5:NR5 -> 8:ER9 -> 7:SL6
      -> 10:EL2 -> 11:N1.
```

Here `WL8` means the eighth event in row eight, not the eighth WL port.
The path has twelve steps and net horizontal voltage zero. It does not visit
row eleven earlier, so `N1` is the first same-physical-cell return after
`W6`. The two visits must therefore be consecutive in stable row-eleven
order.

In the first count row, all six west departures before `N1` have to be
`SR`. Thus `W6` is a right turn, while `N1` is `NR`, also a right turn.
They cannot be consecutive in an alternating word. In each of the other
three count rows, at most five `SR` ports are available; the sixth west
departure requires an `NL` port, which is itself a north arrival. Thus `N1`
is no later than `W6`, again contradicting the forced first return.

The standalone `code/python/verify_family_b_row11_return.py` checks the fixed path,
its zero voltage, all four count rows, and all 899 R-first alternating
count-compatible words against physical first returns; none survives. The
generic adjacency checker in `work_family_b_next/lower11_physical.py` independently finds zero.
The other
24 previously retained row-ten words have row-eleven continuations. Exact
lifted clocks leave 189 such prefixes after row-eight A and 376 after
row-eight B; all 565 remain feasible with `N>=1,000,000` and neither
period residue `N=2,6 (mod 8)` removes any. Those computations show why this
local argument does not presently extend to a Family B exclusion.

<a id="source-research-notes-family-b-row13-er1-witnesses-2026-09-27-md"></a>

## family_b_row13_er1_witnesses_2026-09-27

Source: `research-notes/family_b_row13_er1_witnesses_2026-09-27.md`.

### Family B's 21 one-`ER` row-twelve prefixes continue through row thirteen

27 September 2026. This is a negative finding for the present local-exclusion
strategy, not a construction of a highway or a proof that Family B exists.

Family B has `W=6m+4` and wakes
`{0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W}` with `2<=p<r<=m-1` and `p+r` odd.
Thus row thirteen is non-wake at every allowed width. The exact row-twelve
restriction in `family_b_lower_row12_er1_restriction_2026-09-26.md` leaves
21 necessary prefixes when row twelve has exactly one `ER`.

**Verified claim.** Each of those 21 prefixes admits at least one
non-wake row-thirteen word that satisfies directional counts, alternating
turns, ordinal edge matching, one-excursion same-cell adjacency, every
witnessed finite first return, and the exact unbounded lifted integer clock.
The witness lengths are 32 for six prefixes and 36 for fifteen prefixes.
This check gives no all-width exclusion; it also leaves the `ER>=2` branch
of row twelve untouched.

`code/python/verify_family_b_row13_er1_witnesses_2026_09_27.py` reconstructs
the established rows zero through ten, loads the 21 row-eleven/twelve
prefixes from `results/family_b_row12_er1_all_2026-09-26.json`, checks each
loaded prefix, and searches row-thirteen count rows and adjacency words until
it finds a valid witness. It tests 473 candidate words, of which 452 fail
the exact clock; the other 21 are explicit witnesses written to
`results/family_b_row13_er1_witnesses_2026-09-27.json`. The clock solver
has an integer period `N>=max(6, number of represented events)` with no
upper bound. It imposes each ordinal edge equation and a gap of at least
four between successive same-row events. The return check rejects only an
explicit finite walk returning to the wrong next event; it does not
extrapolate a quotient cycle.

Run from the repository root:

```text
python code/python/verify_family_b_row13_er1_witnesses_2026_09_27.py
```

The script consumes the earlier verified row-twelve result file, so its
claim is conditional on that prerequisite. Its row-thirteen output is a
set of necessary prefixes, not a complete frontier or a realized tour.

<a id="source-research-notes-lower-guard-upper-fat123-wake-families-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## lower_guard_upper_fat123_wake_families_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/lower_guard_upper_fat123_wake_families_2026-09-22.md`.

### Wake placements for a lower guard cap and an upper `(1,2,3)` cap

22 September 2026

#### Result and scope

Take drift `(1,-3)` and growth eight.  Suppose the lower cap has profile
`(1,1,1)` (guard) and the upper cap has profile `(1,2,3)` (fat).  Normalize the
transverse levels to `0<=t<=W`, with the lower cap at level zero.  Then the
wake set `O` is one of the placements in the following eight parameter
families.  In every family `O` contains the five cap wakes `0,1,2,W-2,W`, and
`x,y,z` (or `u,v,w`) denote the three remaining wakes.

| Family | Width | Extra wakes | Integer restrictions |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | `W=6m` | `z=6k+2`, `x=6i+1`, `y=6j+1` | `1<=i<j<=m-1`, `1<=k<=m-1`, `i+j+k` even, `k<i` or `k>=j` |
| A2 | `W=6m` | `z=6k+2`, `x=6i+4`, `y=6j+4` | `0<=i<j<=m-2`, `1<=k<=m-1`, `i+j+k` odd, `k<=i` or `k>j` |
| C1 | `W=6m+4` | `z=6k`, `x=6i+1`, `y=6j+1` | `1<=i<j<=m`, `1<=k<=m`, `i+j+k` odd, `k<=i` or `k>j` |
| C2 | `W=6m+4` | `z=6k`, `x=6i+4`, `y=6j+4` | `0<=i<j<=m-1`, `1<=k<=m`, `i+j+k` even, `k<=i` or `k>j` |
| B0 | `W=6m+2` | `6i+4`, `6j+4`, `6l+4` | `0<=i<j<l<=m-1`, `i+j+l` even |
| B1 | `W=6m+2` | `6i+1`, `6j+1`, `6l+4` | `1<=i<j<=m-1`, `0<=l<=m-1`, `i+j+l` odd |
| B2 | `W=6m+2` | `u=6i`, `v=6j+3`, `w=6l+1` | `1<=i<=m-1`, `0<=j<=m-1`, `1<=l<=m-1`, `i+j+l` even, `[i<=l]=[j<l]` |
| B3 | `W=6m+2` | `u=6i+2`, `v=6j+5`, `w=6l+1` | `1<=i<=m-1`, `0<=j<=m-1`, `1<=l<=m-1`, `i+j+l` even, `[l<=i]=[l<=j]` |

The Iverson conditions in the last column are the rank parity; the parity
conditions on the parameter sums are the additive moment.  No other width is
possible for this cap pair apart from the three residues `W=0,2,4 mod 6`, and
each residue class has only the listed shapes.

The families are pairwise disjoint.  Their smallest widths and first
placements are

| Family | first `W` | a first placement | number at that width |
|---|---|---|---|
| B2 | 14 | `{0,1,2,3,6,7,12,14}` | 1 |
| A2 | 18 | `{0,1,2,4,10,14,16,18}` | 1 |
| B1 | 20 | `{0,1,2,4,7,13,18,20}` | 2 |
| B3 | 20 | `{0,1,2,8,11,13,18,20}` | 4 |
| C1 | 22 | `{0,1,2,6,7,19,20,22}` | 2 |
| C2 | 22 | `{0,1,2,6,10,16,20,22}` | 3 |
| A1 | 24 | `{0,1,2,8,13,19,22,24}` | 3 |
| B0 | 26 | `{0,1,2,4,10,22,24,26}` | 2 |

The number of placements at widths `W<=44` is `686`, and at widths `W<=80` it
is `9802`, per width

```text
W : 14 18 20 22 24 26 28 30 32 34 36 38  40 42  44 ...  80
#  :  1  1 10  5  5 30 12 14 72 30 30 135 52 55 234 ... 1800
```

These are necessary conditions valid at every period.  They are not
constructions, and by themselves they exclude no width.  The derivation is by
hand and uses only the cap wakes, P22, the rank parity and the additive
moment.  An exhaustive enumeration over all eight-element wake sets confirms
the classification exactly, placement for placement, for every even
`W<=80`.

#### Premises

1. **Caps.**  The lower guard cap has rows `0:WR`, `1:NR`, `2:WR`; levels
   `0,1,2` are wakes.  The upper `(1,2,3)` cap has one, two and three visits on
   levels `W`, `W-1`, `W-2`, so `W` and `W-2` are wakes and `W-1` is not.  Both
   extreme arrivals are horizontal, so `W` is even.  Eight distinct wakes need
   `W>=8`, so the five cap wakes are distinct.
2. **P22.**  There are exactly eight wake levels.  In each residue fibre
   `O_r={t in O : t=r mod 3}` the signed sum of the checker signs `(-1)^t` is
   `2 mod 4`.  With eight wakes this is equivalent to: fibre sizes `4,2,2`, each
   two-element fibre has equal parities, and the four-element fibre splits
   `3+1` by parity.  (Each fibre sum must be even and non-zero mod 4, so every
   fibre has even size at least two.)
3. **Rank** (`growth8_odd_skew_additive_2026-08-23.md`).  With
   `I_ij=#{(a,b) in O_i x O_j : a<b}`, the sum `I=I_01+I_02+I_12` is even.  As
   noted in `odd_skew_missing_guard_exclusion_2026-09-03.md`, labelling the
   fibres by `t mod 3` instead of the normalized labels does not change the
   parity, because every fibre has even size.
4. **Moment** (same note, `s=-1`), lower cap at level zero:

   ```text
   M = sum_(t in O) f(t) = 3 (mod 4),      f(t) = (-1)^(t+1) floor((t+1)/3).
   ```

   Values used below: `f(0)=f(1)=0`, `f(2)=-1`,
   `f(6a)=-2a`, `f(6a+1)=2a`, `f(6a+2)=-(2a+1)`, `f(6a+3)=2a+1`,
   `f(6a+4)=-(2a+1)`, `f(6a+5)=2a+2`.

#### Derivation

Write the fixed wakes by residue and parity: `0` (res 0, even), `1` (res 1,
odd), `2` (res 2, even), and the upper pair `W`, `W-2` (both even).  Three
extra wakes remain.  A fixed fibre containing two opposite parities must be
the four-element fibre.

##### Width `W=6m` (families A)

Here `W` has residue 0 and `W-2` residue 1.  The fibre of residue 1 contains
`1` (odd) and `W-2` (even), so it is the four-element fibre
`O_1={1,W-2,x,y}`; its `3+1` split forces `x,y` of equal parity.  The other
fibres are `O_0={0,W}` and `O_2={2,z}` with `z` even, so `z=6k+2`.  Since
`8<=z<=W-4`, `1<=k<=m-1`.  If `x,y` are odd then `x=6i+1`, `y=6j+1` with
`7<=x<y<=W-5`, so `1<=i<j<=m-1`.  If they are even then `x=6i+4`,
`y=6j+4` with `4<=x<y<=W-8`, so `0<=i<j<=m-2`.

Rank: `I_01=4`, `I_02=2`, `I_12=2+[x<z]+[y<z]`.  So `[x<z]=[y<z]`.  For odd
`x`, `x<z` iff `i<=k`, giving "`k<i` or `k>=j`"; for even `x`, `x<z` iff
`i<k`, giving "`k<=i` or `k>j`".

Moment: the cap wakes give `f(0)+f(1)+f(2)+f(6m)+f(6m-2) = -1-2m-(2m-1) =
-4m`.  With odd `x,y`: `M=-4m+2i+2j-(2k+1)`, and `M=3 mod 4` iff `i+j+k` is
even (A1).  With even `x,y`: `M=-4m-(2i+1)-(2j+1)-(2k+1)`, and `M=3 mod 4` iff
`i+j+k` is odd (A2).

##### Width `W=6m+4` (families C)

Now `W` has residue 1 and `W-2` residue 2.  The fibre of residue 1 contains
`1` and `W` of opposite parity: `O_1={1,W,x,y}` with `x,y` of equal parity.
Then `O_2={2,W-2}` and `O_0={0,z}` with `z` even, `z=6k`, `6<=z<=W-4`, so
`1<=k<=m`.  Odd `x=6i+1<y=6j+1<=W-3` gives `1<=i<j<=m`; even
`x=6i+4<y=6j+4<=W-6` gives `0<=i<j<=m-1`.

Rank: `I_01=5+[z<x]+[z<y]`, `I_02=3`, `I_12=4`.  So `[z<x]=[z<y]`, and in both
parities `z<x` iff `k<=i`, giving "`k<=i` or `k>j`".

Moment: cap wakes give `-1+f(6m+4)+f(6m+2)=-1-2(2m+1)=-4m-3`, and `f(z)=-2k`.
Odd `x,y`: `M=-4m-3-2k+2i+2j`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+k` odd (C1).  Even `x,y`:
`M=-4m-3-2k-(2i+1)-(2j+1)`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+k` even (C2).

##### Width `W=6m+2` (families B)

Now `W` has residue 2 and `W-2` residue 0, so the fixed fibres are
`{0,W-2}` (even, even), `{1}`, and `{2,W}` (even, even).  Residue 1 has one
fixed wake and needs at least one more.  The three extra wakes therefore
split in one of three ways.

* **(a) `O_1` is the four-element fibre**, `O_1={1,x,y,z}`.  The `3+1` split
  with `1` odd means either all three extras are even (`=4 mod 6`, B0) or
  exactly two are odd (`=1 mod 6`) and one even (B1).  Level `W-1=6m+1` is
  non-wake, so odd extras satisfy `7<=6i+1<=W-7`, i.e. `1<=i<=m-1`; even
  extras satisfy `4<=6l+4<=W-4`, i.e. `0<=l<=m-1`.
* **(b) `O_0` is the four-element fibre**, `O_0={0,W-2,u,v}` with one even
  `u=6i` and one odd `v=6j+3` (`6<=u<=W-8`, `3<=v<=W-5`), and `O_1={1,w}` with
  `w=6l+1<=W-7` odd (B2).
* **(c) `O_2` is the four-element fibre**, `O_2={2,W,u,v}` with even
  `u=6i+2` (`8<=u<=W-6`) and odd `v=6j+5` (`5<=v<=W-3`), and `O_1={1,w}` as in
  (b) (B3).

Rank.  (a): `I_01=4`, `I_02=3`, `I_12=5`; `I=12` is even for every placement,
so the rank parity gives nothing.  (b): `I_01=2+[u<w]+[v<w]`, `I_02=5`,
`I_12=3`, so `[u<w]=[v<w]`, i.e. `[i<=l]=[j<l]`.  (c): `I_01=2`, `I_02=5`,
`I_12=5+[w<u]+[w<v]`, so `[w<u]=[w<v]`, i.e. `[l<=i]=[l<=j]`.

Moment.  The cap wakes give `-1+f(6m+2)+f(6m)=-1-(2m+1)-2m=-4m-2`.
B0: `M=-4m-2-(2i+1)-(2j+1)-(2l+1)`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+l` even.
B1: `M=-4m-2+2i+2j-(2l+1)`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+l` odd.
B2: `M=-4m-2-2i+(2j+1)+2l`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+l` even.
B3: `M=-4m-2-(2i+1)+(2j+2)+2l`, so `M=3` iff `i+j+l` even.

This completes the classification.  Disjointness is clear: the families differ
in `W mod 6`, in the fibre that carries four wakes, or in the parity pattern
of the extras.

#### Near-cap wake patterns

Only the wakes near either cap enter the local chronology.  In lower levels
the extra wakes lie in the following residues mod 6, and in upper offsets
`u=W-t` in the listed residues:

| Family | lower levels of extras (mod 6) | upper offsets of extras (mod 6) |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | `x,y=1`, `z=2` | `x,y=5`, `z=4` |
| A2 | `x,y=4`, `z=2` | `x,y=2` (`>=8`), `z=4` |
| C1 | `x,y=1`, `z=0` | `x,y=3`, `z=4` |
| C2 | `x,y=4`, `z=0` | `x,y=0`, `z=4` |
| B0 | all `=4` | all `=4` |
| B1 | two `=1`, one `=4` | two `=1`, one `=4` |
| B2 | `u=0`, `v=3`, `w=1` | `u=2` (`>=8`), `v=5`, `w=1` (`>=7`) |
| B3 | `u=2`, `v=5`, `w=1` | `u=0`, `v=3`, `w=1` (`>=7`) |

In particular upper offset three is a wake only in C1 (`x` or `y=W-3`) and B3
(`v=W-3`), and upper offset four is a wake only in A (`z=W-4`), C (`z=W-4`),
B0 and B1 (an even extra at `W-4`).  In all other placements the upper
`(1,2,3)` four-band reduction
(`odd_skew_upper_fat123_four_band_reduction_2026-09-21.md`) applies.  Lower
level three is a wake only in B2 (`j=0`), lower level four only in A2, C2
(`i=0`), B0 (`i=0`) and B1 (`l=0`), lower level five only in B3 (`j=0`).

Every family also contains placements with all three extra wakes arbitrarily
far from both caps (take `m` large and the parameters near `m/2` with the
required parity and order).  Hence a complete exclusion of this cap pair by
local chronology must handle the schedule in which lower levels `3,...,D` and
upper offsets `3,...,D` are all non-wake.

#### Verification

From `code/python/`:

```text
python lower_guard_upper_fat123_families.py 80
```

prints

```text
brute 9802 closed 9802 closed distinct 9802 equal True
per W: {14: 1, 18: 1, 20: 10, 22: 5, 24: 5, 26: 30, 28: 12, 30: 14, 32: 72, ...}
through W=44: 686
per family: {'A1': 855, 'A2': 861, 'B0': 490, 'B1': 1512, 'B2': 2205, 'B3': 2184, 'C1': 840, 'C2': 855}
```

The brute force enumerates, for every even `W<=80`, all three-element sets of
extra wakes from `{3,...,W-3}` added to `{0,1,2,W-2,W}`, and keeps those passing
the three tests literally (fibre signed sums `=2 mod 4`, rank sum even, moment
`=3 mod 4`).  The closed form generates the eight families.  The two lists
coincide.

`python lower_guard_upper_fat123_check_formulas.py` recomputes, for all 28,340 placements of the eight
shapes with the rank and moment conditions dropped (`W<=80`), the pair counts
`I_01,I_02,I_12` and the moment `M`, and compares them with the expressions in
the derivation above.  It prints `checked 28340 relaxed placements, mismatches 0`.

<a id="source-research-notes-lower-missing-upper-fat123-wake-families-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## lower_missing_upper_fat123_wake_families_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/lower_missing_upper_fat123_wake_families_2026-09-22.md`.

### Wake placements for a lower missing cap and an upper `(1,2,3)` cap

Later results: [the missing-cap offset-six theorem](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-missing-cap-offset6-2026-09-23-md) excludes M-II. The M-I width-22 bands are excluded separately for [long 111](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-missing-mi-w22-upper111-2026-09-24-md), [long 120](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-missing-mi-w22-upper120-2026-09-26-md), and [short](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-missing-mi-w22-upper-short-2026-09-27-md). The classification below records the preceding arithmetic stage; M-I at larger widths remains open.

22 September 2026

#### Result and scope

Take drift `(1,-3)` and growth eight.  Suppose the lower cap has profile
`(1,1,0)` (missing) and the upper cap has profile `(1,2,3)` (fat).  Normalize
the transverse levels to `0<=t<=W`, with the lower cap at level zero.  Then
every such trace has wake set `O` in one of the following two families.

| Family | Width | Wake set | Integer restrictions |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-I | `W=6m+4` | `{0,1,4,6,6i+4,6k+2,6m+2,6m+4}` | `1<=i<k<=m-1`, `i+k` odd |
| M-II | `W=6m+2` | `{0,1,4,7,6k+2,6j+1,6m,6m+2}` | `1<=k<j<=m-1`, `j-k` even |

No width divisible by six is possible.  Family M-I first appears at `W=22`,
with the single placement `{0,1,4,6,10,14,20,22}`.  Family M-II first appears at
`W=26`, with the single placement `{0,1,4,7,8,19,24,26}`.  In either family
exactly one of levels six and seven is a wake.  At width `6m+4` there are
`floor((m-1)^2/4)` placements, and at width `6m+2` there are
`floor((m-2)^2/4)`.

These are necessary conditions valid at every period.  They are not
constructions, and they exclude neither family at any width.  The proof is by
hand.  It uses only the cap wake levels, P22, the odd-skew rank parity, the
additive moment, and the lower-missing lemma `omega_6+omega_7>=1`.  An
exhaustive enumeration then confirms the final classification, and each
intermediate step, through `W=80`.

The derivation independently confirms the classification recorded in the
22 September working notes.  Its two convention checks also pass.  The moment
condition may be evaluated with the lower cap at level zero; in fact, given
P22, it does not depend on the level translation at all.  Its orientation is
fixed, however: the lower cap must be the minimum of `3x+y`.  The rank parity is
unchanged by every relabelling of the residues and by reversing the level
order.

#### Premises

1. **Caps.**  The lower missing cap has fixed rows `0:WR`, `1:NR`, `2:` empty,
   `3:SR,SL`, `4:WR,WL,ER`.  Thus levels `0,1,4` are wakes and levels `2,3` are
   non-wake.  The upper `(1,2,3)` cap has one, two, and three visits on levels
   `W`, `W-1`, and `W-2`.  Since a stable word has odd length exactly at a wake,
   levels `W` and `W-2` are wakes and level `W-1` is not.  Both extreme
   arrivals are horizontal, so `W` is even.
2. **P22.**  There are exactly eight wake levels.  In each fibre `O_r` (wake
   levels with a fixed residue mod 3), the signed sum of the checker signs is
   `2 mod 4`.  With eight wakes this is equivalent to the following: the fibre
   sizes are `4,2,2`, each two-element fibre has two wakes of equal parity, and
   the four-element fibre splits 3+1 by parity.
3. **Rank** (`growth8_odd_skew_additive_2026-08-23.md`, equation (2)).  Put
   `I_ij=#{(u,v) in O_i x O_j : u<v}`.  Then `I_01+I_02+I_12` is even.
4. **Moment** (same note, equation (1), for `s=-1`).  In cap-normalized levels:

   ```text
   M = sum_(t in O) (-1)^(t+1) floor((t+1)/3) = 3  (mod 4).
   ```

5. **Lower-missing lemma** (`odd_skew_excursions_and_domino_transfer_2026-08-31.md`,
   section "Level six or level seven must be wake").  At a lower `(1,1,0)` cap
   and growth eight, `omega_6+omega_7>=1`.  Its proof uses exact P3 at the lower
   cap, the lower-cap clock sign, and P22.  It assumes nothing about the upper
   cap or the period.  It must not be transferred to an upper missing cap.

#### Conventions

##### Residues and checker signs

With `t_raw=3x+y`, a translation class with base `(x,y)` has
`y = t_raw (mod 3)`, and its checker sign is `(-1)^(x+y) = (-1)^(t_raw)`,
because `x+y = t_raw-2x`.  The normalized level is `t=t_raw-t_min`.  Hence the
normalized residues `t mod 3` are a cyclic relabelling of the `y mod 3`
fibres, and `(-1)^t` equals the checker sign up to one common factor.  Neither
change affects P22: the condition "signed sum `=2 mod 4` in every fibre" is
invariant under a global sign and under any relabelling of fibres.

##### The rank parity is labelling-invariant

For `d=(1,-3)`, the additive note orders fibre `j` by `U_j={3n+j}` with
`n=x+floor(y/3)` and `j=y mod 3`.  Since `3n+j=3x+y=t_raw`, the rank ordering
is the ordering of raw levels.  Normalization preserves that order.

Changing the residue labels changes only which of each pair of fibres is
called the smaller one.  For two fibres `A,B` with distinct levels,

```text
#{(u,v) in B x A : u<v} = |A||B| - #{(u,v) in A x B : u<v}.
```

The fibre sizes are `4,2,2`, so every product `|A||B|` is even.  Hence the
parity of `I_01+I_02+I_12` is the same under all six labellings.  Reversing the
level order replaces each term by `|A||B|` minus that term, which again
preserves the parity.  The rank condition can therefore be evaluated with
`t mod 3` labels in normalized coordinates, measured from either cap.

##### The moment is translation-invariant but orientation-sensitive

In the additive note's coordinates, `3n+j=t_raw`, so `n=floor(t_raw/3)`,
`j=t_raw mod 3`, and `(-1)^(n+j)=(-1)^(t_raw)`.  The moment is therefore

```text
sum_(t in O) (-1)^(t_raw) floor(t_raw/3) = 3  (mod 4).
```

For an integer `c`, put `M_c = sum_O (-1)^(t+c) floor((t+c)/3)`.  Write
`s=t+c`.  Since `floor((s+1)/3)=floor(s/3)+[s = 2 mod 3]`,

```text
M_(c+1) = -M_c - S,
```

where `S` is the signed sum over one residue fibre.  By P22, `S=2 mod 4`, so
`M_(c+1) = 2-M_c (mod 4)`.  Thus `M_c=3` if and only if `M_(c+1)=3`.
**Given P22, the moment condition does not depend on the level translation.**
The normalized formula with `t+1`, which corresponds to `c=1`, is therefore
correct with the lower cap at level zero.  So are `c=0` and every other shift.

The shift by one has an explanation.  The rank section of the additive note
gives even raw vertices horizontal *departures*, whereas even normalized levels
have horizontal *arrivals*.  So `t_raw = t+1 (mod 2)`.  By the shift
invariance, this bookkeeping does not affect the moment condition.

The orientation cannot be reversed.  Since
`floor(-s/3) = -floor(s/3) - [s != 0 mod 3]`, and two fibre sums of `2 mod 4`
cancel mod four, reflection `t -> -t` negates the moment modulo four.  In
upper inward coordinates `u=W-t`, the same condition therefore reads

```text
sum_(u) (-1)^(u+1) floor((u+1)/3) = 1  (mod 4).
```

The level shifts `E +3`, `N +1` coincide with the additive note's
`t=3x-sy=3x+y` (for `s=-1`, `N -> t+1`, `E -> t+3`).  The lower cap is the
minimum of this `t`.  So the families below use the correct orientation.

All three statements were checked on the 15,158 P22 placements with these caps
and `W<=80`.  The checks were all 36 rank labellings and orders, the moment
shifts `c=-6,...,6`, and reflection (see Verification).

#### Derivation

##### Fibre sizes

The wakes `1` (odd) and `4` (even) lie in `O_1` and have opposite checker signs.
Their contributions cancel, so `O_1` needs two more wakes of equal parity.
Hence `|O_1|=4` and `|O_0|=|O_2|=2`.  Since `0 in O_0`, the other member of
`O_0` is even and divisible by three, so it is `0 mod 6`.

The upper wakes `W` and `W-2` are both even, with residues `W` and `W+1 mod 3`.
They therefore lie in different fibres.

Small widths fail directly.  For `W=0,2,4` the caps overlap inconsistently:
level `2` or `1` would have to be both wake and non-wake, or would not exist.
For `W=6` or `W=8`, at most six levels can be wakes.  The general cases below
also cover these widths.

##### Width `0 mod 6` is impossible

Put `W=6m`.  Then `W` lies in `O_0`, so `O_0={0,W}`.  The level `W-2` is
`1 mod 3`, so `O_1={1,4,W-2,a}`.  Three of the signs are fixed: level 1 is odd,
and levels 4 and `W-2` are even.  A 3+1 split forces `a` to be even.
Consequently:

* level 7 (odd, `1 mod 3`) cannot be a wake;
* level 6 is a wake only if `6=W`, and width six has at most four wake levels.

This contradicts `omega_6+omega_7>=1`.  Without the lemma, width `0 mod 6` is
**not** excluded: 1,430 placements with `W<=80` pass P22, rank, and moment.

##### Width `6m+4`

`W` is `1 mod 3` and even, so `O_1={1,4,a,W}` with `a` even.  Thus `a=6i+4` with
`4<a<W`, so `1<=i<=m-1`.  The level `W-2=6m+2` lies in `O_2`, so
`O_2={c,W-2}` with `c=2 mod 6`.  Level two is empty, and the next level after
`W-2` in that class is `W+4`, so `c=6k+2` with `1<=k<=m-1`.  Finally,
`O_0={0,b}` with `b=0 mod 6`.  Level 7 would be an odd member of `O_1`, so the
lemma forces `b=6`.  Level `W-1=6m+3` is odd, so it is automatically excluded.
Result:

```text
O={0,1,4,6,6i+4,6k+2,6m+2,6m+4},    1<=i,k<=m-1.
```

##### Width `6m+2`

`W` is `2 mod 3`, so `O_2={c,W}` with `c=6k+2` and `1<=k<=m-1`.  Level
`W-2=6m` gives `O_0={0,6m}`.  Level 6 is a wake only if `6m=6`.  Then `W=8`,
and no `c=2 mod 6` with `2<c<8` exists.  By the lemma, level 7 is therefore a
wake.  It is odd, so the fourth member of `O_1` is odd as well: `6j+1`, with
`6j+1` not equal to `1`, `7`, or the non-wake level `W-1=6m+1`.  Result:

```text
O={0,1,4,7,6j+1,6k+2,6m,6m+2},      2<=j<=m-1,  1<=k<=m-1.
```

##### Rank

Width `6m+4`.  Here `O_0={0,6}`, `O_1={1,4,A,W}`, and `O_2={C,W-2}`, with
`A=6i+4>=10` and `C=6k+2>=8`.  Counting pairs gives

```text
I_01 = 4+2 = 6,
I_02 = 2+2 = 4,
I_12 = 2+2+(1+[C>A])+0 = 5+[C>A].
```

So `I=15+[C>A]`.  It is even exactly when `C>A`, which holds exactly when
`k>i`.

Width `6m+2`.  Here `O_0={0,6m}`, `O_1={1,4,7,J}`, and `O_2={K,W}`, with
`J=6j+1<=6m-5` and `K=6k+2>=8`.  Then

```text
I_01 = 4+0 = 4,
I_02 = 2+1 = 3,
I_12 = 2+2+2+(1+[K>J]) = 7+[K>J].
```

So `I=14+[K>J]`.  It is even exactly when `K<J`, which holds exactly when
`k<j`.

##### Moment

The terms `(-1)^(t+1) floor((t+1)/3)` are as follows.

```text
M-I : t   = 0  1  4  6  6i+4     6k+2     6m+2     6m+4
      f   = 0  0 -1 -2 -(2i+1)  -(2k+1)  -(2m+1)  -(2m+1)
      M   = -7-2(i+k)-4m          = 3 (mod 4)  <=>  i+k odd

M-II: t   = 0  1  4  7  6j+1  6k+2     6m    6m+2
      f   = 0  0 -1  2  2j   -(2k+1)  -2m   -(2m+1)
      M   = -1+2(j-k)-4m          = 3 (mod 4)  <=>  j-k even
```

As a cross-check, the `c=0` form gives `5+2(i+k)+4m` and `-1-2(j-k)+4m`.  These
lead to the same parity conditions.

##### Width bounds and counts

M-I needs `1<=i<k<=m-1` with `i+k` odd.  The first case is `m=3`, `i=1`,
`k=2`, so `W=22`.  M-II needs `1<=k<j<=m-1` with `j-k` even.  The first case is
`m=4`, `k=1`, `j=3`, so `W=26`.  The number of pairs is
`ceil(n/2)floor(n/2)=floor(n^2/4)` in M-I, with `n=m-1`, and
`C(ceil(n/2),2)+C(floor(n/2),2)=floor((n-1)^2/4)` in M-II.

#### Consequences for the cap bands

In upper inward coordinates `u=W-t`, the two families are as follows.

```text
M-I : {0,2,6p+2,6q,6m-2,6m,6m+3,6m+4},   p=m-k, q=m-i, 1<=p<q<=m-1, p+q odd
M-II: {0,2,6a+1,6b,6m-5,6m-2,6m+1,6m+2}, a=m-j, b=m-k, 1<=a<b<=m-1, b-a even
```

* Upper offsets `1,3,4,5,6` are non-wake in both families.  Offset 7 is a wake
  only in M-II with `j=m-1`, and offset 8 only in M-I with `k=m-1`.  Since
  upper offsets 3 and 4 are non-wake, the four-band theorem
  (`odd_skew_upper_fat123_four_band_reduction_2026-09-21.md`) applies.  The
  upper initial band is therefore the short row, `(1,1,1)`, `(1,2,0)`, or
  `(2,1,0)`.
* On the lower side, M-I has levels `5,7,8,9` non-wake, with next wake
  `6i+4>=10`; level 10 is a wake exactly when `i=1`.  M-II has levels `5,6`
  non-wake; level 8 is a wake exactly when `k=1`, and otherwise the next wake is
  at least 14.

#### Verification

The scripts are in `code/python/` (run from that directory).  Python
3.13.14, standard library only.

```text
python lower_missing_upper_fat123_families.py 80
```

This enumerates every 8-subset of `[0,W]` containing `0,1,4,W-2,W` and avoiding
`2,3,W-1`, for every even `W<=80`.  It filters by P22, rank, moment, and the
lemma, in that order.  The result is compared with the closed forms.  Output
(excerpt):

```text
even W<=80: total placements 250; all W agree with M-I u M-II: True
surviving counts after P22 / +rank / +moment / +lemma: {'p22': 15158, 'rank': 7007, 'moment': 3490, 'lemma': 250}
P22+rank+moment survivors WITHOUT the lemma, by W mod 6: {0: 1430, 2: 980, 4: 1080}
convention audit over 15158 P22 placements:
  rank parity changes under residue shift/permutation/order reversal: 0
  moment "=3 mod 4" changes under level shift c in [-6,6]: 0
  upper-inward (reflected) moment not equal to 1 exactly when lower moment is 3: 0
ALL_CHECKS_PASSED=True
```

Per-width counts, as brute force / M-I / M-II: 22:1/1/0, 26:1/0/1, 28:2/2/0,
32:2/0/2, 34:4/4/0, 38:4/0/4, 40:6, 44:6, 46:9, 50:9, 52:12, 56:12, 58:16,
62:16, 64:20, 68:20, 70:25, 74:25, 76:30, 80:30, with every width agreeing.

```text
python lower_missing_upper_fat123_derivation_steps.py 80
```

This checks each hand step against brute force: width `0 mod 6` is empty after
P22 and the lemma; the pre-rank parametrizations for widths `6m+4` and `6m+2`
are exact; the integer values of `I_01,I_02,I_12` match the displayed counts;
and the moments equal exactly `-7-2(i+k)-4m` and `-1+2(j-k)-4m`.  Output:
`STEPS_OK=True (W<=80)`.

```text
python lower_missing_upper_fat123_upper_offsets.py 140
```

This confirms the upper-inward forms and the offset table for `W<=140`; its
output ends with `UPPER_FORMS_OK=True`.

```text
lower_missing_upper_fat123_families.py  fb5d1b040f149c69d3078432d7566b2b27a5199a77345857d89b750745e4a3e0
lower_missing_upper_fat123_derivation_steps.py  9b170fa4e960bf82f21e36c1a58fe2c618eb5397f66374eec9173ba77b159667
lower_missing_upper_fat123_upper_offsets.py  22ae0bd43d7718a808e71121157586f23d7a2565c73398707124737cb2dadf97
```

#### Status

**Proved at every period** (by hand, from premises 1-5): the lower-missing /
upper-`(1,2,3)` wake set lies in M-I or M-II, and width `0 mod 6` is
impossible.  The enumeration up to `W=80` is a check of the hand argument, not
part of it.

**Not proved:** that any placement in M-I or M-II is realized.  Also not proved
is any exclusion of the upper band choices listed above.  The lemma
`omega_6+omega_7>=1` is essential: without it, all three width classes survive
the static conditions.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-excursions-and-domino-transfer-2026-08-31-md"></a>

## odd_skew_excursions_and_domino_transfer_2026-08-31

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_excursions_and_domino_transfer_2026-08-31.md`.

### Exact excursions and the odd-skew domino transfer

This note gives an exact algebraic description of a quarter-turn lattice
excursion and applies same-cell return arguments at odd-skew caps.  The
`(1,3)` profile `(1,2,2)` admits a local domino transfer and remains open.  For
the lower `(1,1,0)` cap at drift `(1,-3)`, the return arguments and P22 prove
that level six or level seven is wake at growth eight, in addition to the
known wake levels `0,1,4`.  This excludes the lower-missing/upper-guard cap
pair, but not the drift orbit.

#### An exact flow description of an excursion

Write the four directions in clockwise order as

```text
E, S, W, N.
```

A direction word of length `s` is a sequence

```text
q_0,q_1,...,q_(s-1)
```

in which every transition is a right or left quarter-turn.  For each direction
`q`, let `r_q` count transitions from `q` to its right successor and let `l_q`
count transitions from `q` to its left successor.  Put `a=q_0` and
`b=q_(s-1)`.

The following conditions characterize the possible direction words exactly.

1. All eight transition counts are nonnegative integers, and their sum is
   `s-1`.
2. The prescribed turn balance is

   ```text
   sum_q (r_q-l_q).
   ```

3. In the directed graph on the four directions, the transition counts have
   degree imbalance `+1` at `a`, degree imbalance `-1` at `b`, and zero
   imbalance elsewhere.  When `a=b`, every degree is balanced.
4. Every used direction lies in the same component of the underlying
   undirected support as `a`.
5. If

   ```text
   n_q = out(q) + [q=b],
   ```

   then the displacement is `sum_q n_q v_q`, where `v_q` is the unit vector in
   direction `q`.

Necessity follows by counting the transitions of a direction word.  For
sufficiency, regard the transition counts as a directed multigraph.  If
`a!=b`, add one artificial edge from `b` to `a`.  The resulting multigraph is
balanced and its used vertices are weakly connected.  A balanced weakly
connected directed multigraph is strongly connected on its used edges: in the
condensation graph, a source component has no incoming edge, and degree balance
then gives it no outgoing edge.  Weak connectivity leaves only one component.
The directed Euler theorem supplies a circuit.  Removing the artificial edge
gives a trail from `a` to `b`.  If `a=b`, the same argument applies without the
extra edge.  Reading the vertices of the trail gives the required direction
word, and condition 5 gives its displacement.

This theorem works at arbitrary excursion length.  It does not assert that the
excursion stays inside a transverse strip, and it does not impose alternation
between separate visits to the same physical cell.  An infeasible flow is a
valid local obstruction; a feasible flow is not a highway certificate.

#### Consecutive visits of either colour

The completed-lifetime argument in the detailed reference's "Collision chains"
section treats an `R` visit followed by the next `L` visit to the same physical
cell. Its unsigned incidence conclusion also holds in the other direction.

Let `r<l` be any two consecutive visits to a cell `z`.  The turns at the two
visits are opposite.  The cell `z` occurs once in the departure interval
`[r,l)`.  The two endpoint ant states are at the same cell and have the same
horizontal-or-vertical heading type. The two-endpoint collision-chain parity
theorem therefore gives

```text
partial(B_r symmetric_difference B_l)=0.
```

The symmetric difference contains `z`, so its row-column incidence component
through `z` contains a cycle.  It has at least four cells.  This applies to a
black `R`-to-`L` lifetime and to a white `L`-to-`R` lifetime.  The extension is
only the unsigned completed-lifetime conclusion; signed endpoint conventions must still be chosen
according to the aligned directed poses.

#### The `(1,2,2)` cap chronology

Take drift `d=(1,3)` and transverse coordinate

```text
t=3x-y.
```

At the lower cap, name the level-zero singleton `A`, the level-one pair `B`,
and the level-two pair `C`.  In lifted physical time, normalize

```text
B_R=-1,  A=0,  B_L=u,  C_L=u+1.
```

If `C_R=v`, stable order gives exactly two possibilities.  Either `v<-1`, in
which case the `C` lifetime contains the `B` lifetime, or
`-1<v<u`, in which case the two lifetimes interlace.  There is no integer time
strictly between `B_L=u` and `C_L=u+1`.

The forced path leaving `B_R` is

```text
B_R -> A_R -> P_R -> Q,
```

where `P` is the unique level-three `ER` event and `Q` is the level-four corner
cell.  The predecessor of `B_L` is the same physical cell `Q`.

If the displayed visit to `Q` is `R`, the path returns immediately to `B_L`.
The earlier visit to `Q` is then `L`, and the central chronology is

```text
Q_L -> B_R -> A_R -> P_R -> Q_R -> B_L -> C_L.
```

With

```text
A=(0,0), B=(0,-1), C=(0,-2),
P=(1,0), Q=(1,-1), D=(1,-2),
```

their transverse levels are `0,1,2,3,4,5`, respectively.  The immediate-return
branch puts `C_R` before `B_R`, so the `C` lifetime contains both visits to `B`
and both visits to `Q`.  Those cells cancel from its odd-visit discrepancy.  In
the sharp completion the remaining signed rectangle is

```text
-C -D +A +P.
```

Every exact row and column has signed sum zero.

#### Why the local bridge does not iterate

The level-four `Q` bridge is enclosed by consecutive visits to `D` at level
five.  It is tempting to declare this a strictly inward successor and repeat
the argument.  The following compatible completion disproves that step:

```text
D_L -> Q_L -> B_R -> A_R -> P_R -> Q_R -> B_L -> C_L -> D_R.
```

Between the two directed poses at `D`, the turn word is

```text
LLRRRRLL.
```

Starting at `D` with eastward arrival, the successive moves are

```text
N,W,N,E,S,W,S,E.
```

The displacement and turn balance are zero, and the eastward arrival at `D` is
restored.  The repeated cells `B,Q` cancel.  The four odd cells are exactly
`C,D,A,P`, with signs `-,-,+,+`.

Geometrically, the block is the clockwise all-`R` square on `B,A,P,Q` together
with the adjacent counterclockwise all-`L` square on `C,D,Q,B`.  Its scalar
rotation is zero: it transfers one unit-square winding from the outer face to
the next face instead of forcing a new wake.

Adding white lifetimes does not strengthen this sharp local system.  The black
`B_R`-to-`B_L` lifetime and the white `Q_L`-to-`Q_R` lifetime both have odd
support `{A,B,P,Q}`.  When `C_R` goes directly to the displayed `D_L`, the black
`C_R`-to-`C_L` lifetime and the white `D_L`-to-`D_R` lifetime both have support
`{A,C,D,P}`.  Thus the two new white rows duplicate the two black rows over
`F_2`; they do not recover a missing rank direction at the cap.

This duplication is not an accident of the six named cells.  In a stable odd
class

```text
R_1,L_1,R_2,L_2,...,R_k,L_k,R_(k+1),
```

the black intervals are `(R_i,L_i)` and the white intervals are
`(L_i,R_(i+1))`.  Their endpoint pairs form one open path, not a cycle.  Over
`F_2`, the sum of all its interval boundaries is

```text
R_1 + R_(k+1).
```

For an even class the corresponding boundary is `R_1+L_k`.  White pairing
therefore transports the unmatched defect to the first or terminal event; it
does not fill the black wake cycle.  A white version of the touch-graph theorem
is valid only after building a new touch graph with these seed and terminal
switches.  Its boundary size is not controlled by growth.

There is also a small graph-theoretic obstruction to a universal two-colour
lifting claim.  Take boundary vertices `H_1,H_2,V_1,V_2`, wake edges

```text
u_1=H_1V_1, u_2=H_2V_1, u_3=H_2V_2, u_4=H_1V_2,
```

and one ordinary edge `a=H_1V_2`.  The black row
`a+u_1+u_2+u_3` is a cycle, while the wake cycle
`u_1+u_2+u_3+u_4` is independent.  The alternating class system

```text
A_R,U1_R,U2_R,U3_R,A_L,U4_R
```

has no white interval at all, so adding every white row leaves the missing wake
direction unchanged.  This example concerns the algebra of the touch graph; it
is not asserted to be a Langton trace.

The same paired `C` structure occurs at the start of profile `(1,2,3)`.  Its
extra level-two `R` supplies another endpoint, but it does not invalidate this
zero-voltage block.

The block cannot be forbidden as a turn subword.  In the normalized standard
period-104 highway recorded in `results/standard_highway.json`, the cyclic word
contains `LLRRRRLL` at zero-based phases `7,13,82,102`.  Any exclusion must use
the growth-eight wake budget and the cap placement, not the eight letters alone.

The present local tools therefore do not close profiles `(1,2,2)` or `(1,2,3)`.
A valid next lemma has to retain where the two opposite
unit-square windings lie.  Equivalently, it must prove that a chain of these
domino transfers cannot be spliced into the single voltage-one Euler tour
without ending at further wake classes or violating a stable class order.  No
such global splice lemma has been proved here.

#### A last-exit voltage lemma

There is a chronological constraint which is absent from the row spaces above.
Let a periodic trace have period `N` and primitive drift `d`.  Choose one cell
`c_v` in each visited translation class `v`.  If a phase occurrence in that
class is at `c_v+a_i d`, give it lifted time

```text
tau_i = i-N a_i.
```

The copy at the physical cell `c_v+n d` then occurs at actual time
`nN+tau_i`.  Let `tau_v` be the largest lifted time among the occurrences of
class `v`, and let the outgoing step from this last occurrence enter class
`f(v)`.  Define the integer edge voltage `lambda_v` by

```text
c_v + outgoing_step = c_(f(v)) + lambda_v d.
```

The arrival occurs one step after the last visit to the source.  It is one of
the visits to the target, whose last visit can only be later.  Therefore

```text
tau_(f(v)) + N lambda_v >= tau_v + 1.                 (3)
```

Every vertex has one selected outgoing edge, so each component of the finite
quotient contains a directed cycle.  Summing (3) around a cycle `C` gives

```text
N sum_(v in C) lambda_v >= |C|.
```

In particular, every last-exit cycle has strictly positive integral voltage.
Equivalently, the periodic lift of the selected last exits has no directed
cycle in the plane; every quotient cycle advances in the drift direction.

The cylinder argument in `cylinder_exit_cycles_2026-09-03.md` strengthens this
to voltage exactly one.  It also recovers one complete period as the selected
cycle edges interspersed with actual zero-displacement return excursions.
Every such excursion has length divisible by four and strictly less than the
period.  This keeps the omitted visit order available for a global argument.

The first entries give the reverse statement.  Let `epsilon_v` be the smallest
lifted visit time, and write the physical predecessor of `c_v+n d` as
`c_(p(v))+(n+mu_v)d`.  Its first visit is no later than this predecessor event,
so

```text
epsilon_(p(v)) + N mu_v <= epsilon_v - 1.
```

Every cycle of the reverse predecessor map has strictly negative total
`mu`-voltage.  These two inequalities use chronological extremality, rather
than only alternation or aggregate port flow.

For `d=(1,3)` and transverse level `t=3x-y`, take `c_t=(0,-t)`.  East and west
edges have voltages `1,-1`; north and south edges have voltage zero.  Thus every
last-exit cycle has more east than west edges.  The forced lower-cap final exits
in both profiles under study are

```text
0 -> 3,          1 -> 2 -> 5.
```

The arrows `0->3` and `2->5` have voltage one; `1->2` has voltage zero.  The
next exit from level three is not fixed.  In the odd level-three branch of the
sharp `(1,2,2)` completion, the stable port order is `ER,WL,WR`, so the final
`WR` event exits north and `f(3)=2`.  The even branch ends in `WL` and has
`f(3)=4`.  The aggregate port table for profile `(1,2,3)` also permits both
inward endings; no global realisability claim is intended here.  This
distinction is easy to miss because the predecessor of `C_R` lies at the
translated cell `P-d`: its physical time in the displayed block is earlier,
but its lifted time in the class represented by `P` is one period later.

The displayed arrows are a genuine new restriction on a global splice.
Positivity alone does not show where their orbits merge or which quotient
cycles they enter, so it is not an exclusion of either cap profile.

#### Why the fifth and sixth missing-cap levels are not local consequences

For `d=(1,-3)`, use `t=3x+y` and consider the lower `(1,1,0)` cap.  The
proved port rows through level four are

```text
0: WR
1: NR
2: empty
3: SR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER
```

The stable words on levels three and four are `RL` and `RLR`.  On level five,
write the ports as `(NR,NL,SR,SL)`.  The level-four `WR` supplies one north
arrival, while the empty level two forbids west departures.  Hence

```text
NR=1,       NL=SR=0,       SL in {0,1}.
```

Thus level five can be either the wake row `NR` or the even row `NR,SL`.
In the even case, level six has one east arrival from the level-three `SL`
and one south departure to the level-five `SL`.  The even port row

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(0,1,2,1)
```

has stable order `WR,EL,WR,WL` and satisfies every aggregate equation.

The even/even branch also survives the cap chronology.  The following data
give one exact lifted ordering for the named classes.  Take bookkeeping period
`N=202`, representatives `c_t=(0,t)`, and write an occurrence as `(phase,x)`,
meaning the physical cell `c_t+x(1,-3)`.  Its lifted time is `phase-202x`.

```text
t=0: WR (22,-1)
t=1: NR (23,-1)
t=3: SR (21,0), SL (25,0)
t=4: WR (102,1), WL (20,0), ER (24,0)
t=5: NR (103,1), SL (61,0)
t=6: WR (180,2), EL (26,1), WR (142,1), WL (60,0)
```

Sorting by lifted time gives `R`, `R`, `RL`, `RLR`, `RL`, and `RLRL` on
the six occupied levels.  The phases `20` through `26` form the forced block

```text
(0,4) WL -> (0,3) SR -> (-1,3) WR -> (-1,4) NR
         -> (0,4) ER -> (0,3) SL -> (1,3) EL.
```

There are also the required immediate edges from the level-six `WL` at phase
`60` to the level-five `SL` at phase `61`, and from the level-four `WR` at
phase `102` to the level-five `NR` at phase `103`.  At a fixed physical copy,
the visits occur in the following orders:

```text
t=3:  21 R, 25 L
t=4: -100 R, 20 L, 24 R
t=5:  -99 R, 61 L
t=6: -224 R, -176 L, -60 R, 60 L.
```

The named cells therefore obey ordinary alternation as well as stable P3
order.  Here are literal direction words for the five open gaps; the last gap
ends at the translate of phase `20` in the next period.

```text
26 ->  60: NENENENENENENENENWNWSWSWSWSWSWSWSW
61 -> 102: ENENENENENENENENESESESWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSW
103 -> 142: ENENENENENENENENENENWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSW
142 -> 180: NENENENENENENENESESESWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSW
180 -> 222: NENENENENENENENENENENWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSWSW
```

Successive letters are perpendicular, so each word is a quarter-turn walk.
Every proper intermediate position has level at least seven.  No further cap
event is forced.  These fillers need not obey P3 on their own translation
classes, so this construction is not a periodic highway.  Its point is
narrower.  Even when exact motion and same-cell chronology are added to local
port flow, neither level five nor level six is forced to be wake.  Ruling out
this branch requires the stable first-turn condition in the inward excursions,
or an equivalent global splice or lifetime invariant.

#### Orientation of first-departure cycles

There is a useful global restriction on those inward excursions.  Let
`epsilon_v` be the first lifted visit time of a class `v`, and let `g(v)` be
the class reached by the edge leaving that visit.  If the edge voltage is
`lambda_v`, its arrival has lifted time

```text
epsilon_v+1-N lambda_v.
```

The first visit to the target is no later, so

```text
epsilon_(g(v))+N lambda_v <= epsilon_v+1.             (4)
```

For a `g`-cycle of length `k`, summing (4) gives `N Lambda<=k`.  A positive
integer voltage is therefore possible only when `k=N`; in that exceptional
case the cycle contains every period event.  Every proper first-departure
cycle has nonpositive voltage.

Suppose a zero-voltage cycle lifts to a simple lattice Jordan curve.  It
cannot be clockwise.  At a selected first visit, write `e` for the outgoing
unit edge.  P3 makes the turn `R`, so the predecessor cell is the unit right
normal from the current cell.  On a clockwise grid polygon this cell lies in
the closed interior.  The statement remains true at a reflex corner: the
short right-normal segment starts on the interior side and cannot cross a
grid edge transversely.

Now take a sufficiently far future translate of the lifted curve.  The ant
starts outside this bounded region, and the selected visits are the genuine
first visits to its boundary cells.  Choose the earliest of them.  If its
predecessor is another boundary cell, that cell was visited earlier.  If the
predecessor is strictly inside, the ant must already have crossed the Jordan
curve.  Either alternative contradicts the choice of the earliest boundary
visit.  Thus every simple zero-voltage first-departure cycle is
counterclockwise.  A doubled two-edge cycle is not a Jordan curve and is not
covered by this argument.

For the odd-skew drifts this gives a four-level test.  With `d=(1,3)` and
`t=3x-y`, let `a` be even.  The first-port pattern

```text
a:NR,       a+1:WR,       a+3:ER,       a+4:SR
```

would give the clockwise square with directions `E,S,W,N` and edge voltages
`1,0,-1,0`.  It is impossible.  For `d=(1,-3)`, `t=3x+y`, and odd `a`, the
corresponding forbidden pattern is

```text
a-1:WR,     a:NR,         a+2:SR,       a+3:ER.
```

At the lower `(1,1,0)` cap this second test applies to levels `0,1,3,4`.
The first ports on the first three of those levels are `WR,NR,SR`, so level
four cannot begin with `ER`.  Its proved order `WR,WL,ER` has the required
orientation.

#### Order-preserving returns at the missing cap

The local countermodel through level six does not settle its inward extension.
Exact edge matching supplies a stronger restriction one level later.  Assume
that levels five through seven are all even.  Level five is `NR,SL`.  On level
six, write `(a,b,c,d)=(ER,EL,WR,WL)`.  Its equations are

```text
a+b=1,       a+d=1,       a+c=b+d.
```

Nonnegativity forces `(a,b,c,d)=(0,1,2,1)`.  There are two possible stable
orders, and both must be retained at this stage:

```text
A: WR,EL,WR,WL
B: WR,WL,WR,EL.
```

For a zero-excess level-seven row, the three northbound departures from level
six and the two west arrivals on level four give

```text
NR+NL=3,       NL+SR=2,       NR+SR=NL+SL.
```

Thus its count row is either `(2,1,1,2)` or `(3,0,2,5)`, in the order
`(NR,NL,SR,SL)`.

Let `W0<W1` be its two westbound departures in stable order.  They feed the
level-four ports `WR,WL` in that order.  Indeed, along any fixed directed edge
block, target lifted time is source lifted time plus the constant
`1-N lambda`, so matching preserves order.  The later departure has the forced
successor path

```text
W1 -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR
   -> 4:ER -> 3:SL -> 6:EL -> Q,
```

where `Q` is a north arrival on level seven.  Its direction word is
`WSWNESEN`.  The eight steps have zero displacement, and no intermediate event
lies on level seven.  Thus `W1` and `Q` are consecutive visits to the same
physical cell.  Their turns must be opposite, and they must be adjacent in the
stable level-seven word.

In the larger count row `(3,0,2,5)`, both west departures are `SR` and every
north arrival is `NR`.  The two endpoints would both turn right.  This row is
therefore impossible.  In the smaller row, if `W1` were `SR`, its opposite-turn
target would have to be the unique `NL`.  But that `NL` is the earlier west
departure `W0`, contrary to the forward chronology of the return.  Consequently

```text
W0=SR,       W1=NL,       Q=NR,
```

and `NL,Q` are consecutive stable events.

In order A, `6:EL` is the middle northbound departure, so `Q` is the middle
north arrival on level seven.  In order B it is the last northbound departure,
so `Q` is the last north arrival.  Placing the unique `SR` before `NL` in the
alternating six-event word leaves exactly

```text
level 6 A:  level 7 SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
level 6 B:  level 7 SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL
         or level 7 NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

Two of these possibilities are impossible even if level eight is allowed to
be wake.  In each, let `r` be the unique `7:SR` event.  It is the earlier
westbound departure, so the three-step path

```text
r -> 4:WR -> 5:NR -> U
```

ends at the first east arrival `U` on level eight.  Its net edge voltage is
zero, giving `tau(U)=tau(r)+3`.  Let `V` be the southbound departure from level
eight that feeds `r`.  Then
`tau(V)=tau(r)-1`.  Since `V` precedes the first east arrival, it cannot be
`ER`; it must be `WL`.

The path `V->r->4:WR->5:NR->U` is a four-step return to the same physical
level-eight cell, with no intermediate level-eight event.  Opposite turns
therefore force `U=ER`.  Its southward step then completes a four-step return
from `r` to the same level-seven cell, now with a south arrival.  These are
consecutive visits there, so the `SR` must be immediately followed by `SL` in
the stable word.  Only the middle row of the displayed list has that property.
We have therefore forced

```text
level 6: WR,WL,WR,EL
level 7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL.
```

If level eight is also even, its count equations leave only

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(0,2,5,3) or (1,1,2,2).
```

The first row has no `ER`, so it is impossible.  In the second, the first east
arrival is `ER`, and the four-step return makes `WL,ER` consecutive stable
events.  This `WL` is also the first southbound departure, because `r` is now
the first south arrival on level seven.  The alternating word then has only
two possibilities:

```text
WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL
WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL.
```

The second order contradicts a lifted-time inequality.  Put
`a=tau(6:WL)` and `b=tau(second 6:WR)`, so `a<b`.  The northbound step from
the latter feeds `7:NL`, and the eight-step return gives
`tau(last 7:NR)=b+9`.  If `h=tau(last 7:SL)`, then `h>b+9`.  On level eight,
the second east arrival is its sole `EL`; it is fed by
`6:WL->5:SL->8:EL`, giving

```text
tau(8:EL)=a+2-N.
```

The last `8:WL` feeds the last `7:SL`, so its time is `h-1>b+8`.  Since the
period is positive, the `EL` is earlier than this `WL`, excluding the second
order.

Thus there is a unique stable band through level eight when levels five
through eight are all even:

```text
level 5: NR,SL
level 6: WR,WL,WR,EL
level 7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL
level 8: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL.
```

The next level cannot also be even.

#### A fourth lower-cap wake by level nine

At a lower `(1,1,0)` cap with drift `(1,-3)`, at least one of levels five
through nine is a wake level.  Together with `0,1,4`, this gives at least four
wake classes in the first ten transverse levels.  The proof is independent of
the period and uses no hypothesis on level ten.

Suppose, to the contrary, that levels five through nine are all even.  The
preceding argument fixes the stable rows through level eight.  The level-nine
port equations are

```text
NR+NL=3,       NL+SR=3,       NR+SR=NL+SL.
```

Their nonnegative solutions are

```text
(NR,NL,SR,SL)=(3,0,3,6), (2,1,2,3), or (1,2,1,0).
```

Let `W1,W2,W3` be the westbound departures on level nine in stable order, and
let `N1,N2,N3` be its ordered north arrivals.  The second west departure feeds
`6:WL`; the middle north departure on level eight is `EL`.  Hence edge matching
forces

```text
W2 -> 6:WL -> 5:SL -> 8:EL -> N2.
```

This is a four-step same-cell return with direction word `WSEN` and no
intermediate level-nine event.  The count row `(3,0,3,6)` makes both endpoints
right turns and is impossible.

For `(1,2,1,0)`, the only possible stable order is `SR,NL,NR,NL`: the other
alternating order makes the return run backward in stable time.  Here
`W2=N1=NL`.  This forces another return,

```text
8:first WR -> 9:NL -> 6:WL -> 5:SL -> 8:EL,
```

whose four steps have zero displacement and no intermediate level-eight
event.  But the fixed stable row on level eight has `WL,ER` between this first
`WR` and its `EL`.  That is impossible.

In the remaining count row `(2,1,2,3)`, if `W2=NL`, the first four-step return
makes it the first north arrival.  The same level-eight contradiction applies.
Thus `W2=SR` and `N2=NL=W3`.  These are adjacent stable events.  Placing the
other ports in the alternating word leaves only

```text
NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL
SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

Write the two `SR` events as `SR1,SR2`.  Their west-departure order is
`SR1,SR2,NL`.  Let `E1,E2,E3,E4` be the east arrivals on level ten; the ordered
east departures on level seven that feed them are `SL1,NR1,NR2,SL2`.

The third arrival `E3` must be `EL`.  If it were `ER`, the path

```text
9:NL -> 6:WR2 -> 7:NL -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR
     -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL -> 6:EL -> 7:NR2 -> E3 -> 9
```

would be a twelve-step return to the same level-nine cell, with direction
word `WNWSWNESENES` and no intermediate level-nine event.  Its target is a
south arrival, whereas the next stable event after `9:NL` is `NR`.  Therefore
`E3=EL`.

Now let `J` be the level-ten southbound departure feeding `9:SR2`.  Its
successors are forced all the way to `E3`:

```text
J -> 9:SR2 -> 6:WL -> 5:SL -> 8:EL -> 9:NL
  -> 6:WR2 -> 7:NL -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR
  -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL -> 6:EL -> 7:NR2 -> E3.
```

The direction word `SWSENWNWSWNESENE` has zero displacement, and none of its
intermediate events lies on level ten.  Its endpoints must be consecutive
same-cell visits with opposite turns.  Since `E3` is `EL`, this forces
`J=ER`.  It is an east arrival earlier than `E3`, so it is either `E1` or
`E2`.  It cannot be `E1`, since then the stable visit `E2` would intervene.
Thus `J=E2`.

But this gives the four-step return

```text
9:SR1 -> 6:WR1 -> 7:NR1 -> J -> 9:SR2.
```

Its direction word is `WNES`, and it has no intermediate level-nine event.
Both endpoints turn right, contradicting ordinary same-cell alternation.
This eliminates the last even level-nine possibility and proves the wake
claim.

The conclusion is a disjunction, not five separate forced offsets:
`omega_5+omega_6+omega_7+omega_8+omega_9>=1`.  In particular, it does not prove
the previously conjectured individual wake offsets five and six, nor does it
close growth eight.

At growth eight, P22 strengthens the disjunction to

```text
omega_5+omega_6+omega_7+omega_8 >= 1.
```

To see this, partition the wake classes by level modulo three, which is the
same as the `y mod 3` partition up to a common residue shift.  Each fibre has
signed sum `2 mod 4`, so it contains an even number of classes, at least two.
The known wake levels one and four share a fibre and have opposite checker
signs.  They cancel, so that fibre needs at least two further classes.  The
total of eight therefore forces fibre cardinalities `2,4,2`, with the middle
fibre containing levels one and four.

The fibre containing level zero has only one other wake class.  Its checker
sign must agree with that of level zero, so its level is an even multiple of
three.  Level nine cannot be wake.  Combining this with the preceding theorem
proves the four-level disjunction.  Thus a growth-eight lower missing cap
contributes at least four wake classes among levels zero through eight.

#### Allowing a wake on level eight

The level-nine contradiction can be strengthened.  If levels five through
seven are even, level nine must be wake regardless of level eight's parity.
Only the wake-level-eight branch remains to check.

The argument through level seven did not assume level eight was even, so its
stable rows remain

```text
6: WR,WL,WR,EL
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL.
```

For wake level eight, the equations `ER+EL=2`, `ER+WL=3`, and excess one give
the count rows

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(0,2,6,3), (1,1,3,2), or (2,0,0,1).
```

The four-step return already proved that the first south departure is `WL`,
immediately followed by the first east arrival `ER`.  The first count row has
no `ER`.  The last has stable word `ER,WL,ER`, whose first south departure is
`ER`.  Thus only the middle count row remains.  Its stable order must be

```text
WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,WR
or WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR.
```

The earlier inequality `tau(8:EL)<tau(last 8:WL)` still holds: the extra final
`WR` changes neither of the two paths used to derive it.  Therefore level
eight has the first displayed order.

Suppose level nine is even.  It now has four north arrivals and three west
departures.  Its possible count rows are

```text
(NR,NL,SR,SL)=(4,0,3,7), (3,1,2,4), or (2,2,1,1).
```

Let `W2` again be the second west departure, and let `N2` be the second of the
four north arrivals.  The same four-step return is

```text
W2 -> 6:WL -> 5:SL -> 8:EL -> N2.
```

The first count row makes both endpoints right turns.  In either remaining
row, if `W2=NL`, its opposite-turn target is `NR`, so this `NL` is the first
north arrival.  Then the four-step return from the first `8:WR` to `8:EL`
skips the fixed intervening `WL,ER` visits.  Thus `W2` must be `SR`.

For the middle count row, the target is its unique `NL`, which is also the
last west departure.  The possible stable words are

```text
NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL.
```

These differ from the preceding even-level-eight branch only by a final
`NR,SL` pair.  The twelve-step return still forces the third east arrival on
level ten to be `EL`, since `9:NL` is immediately followed by `NR`.  The
sixteen-step return then identifies its predecessor as the second east
arrival.  The resulting four-step `SR1`-to-`SR2` return has two right-turn
endpoints.  All events in those paths are unchanged by the extra pair.

In the last count row, the two west departures other than `SR` are both
`NL`.  Since `W2=SR` and its target `N2` comes later, that target must be
`W3=NL`; the other `NL` is `W1=N1`.  No right turn can precede this first
`NL`: the only `SR` comes later, while either `NR` would already be a north
arrival before `N1`.  This contradicts the R-first stable order.

All even level-nine rows are impossible.  We have proved

```text
omega_5+omega_6+omega_7+omega_9 >= 1.
```

At growth eight, the P22 argument above gives `omega_9=0`, so at least one of
levels five through seven is wake.

#### Level six or level seven must be wake

There is a short complementary argument when level five is wake.  Its entire
row is then `NR`.  If level six is even, its east-arrival count is one and
its south-departure count is zero.  Its unique stable row is `WR,EL`.

If level seven were also even, its equations would be

```text
NR+NL=2,       NL+SR=2,       NR+SR=NL+SL,
```

leaving count rows `(2,0,2,4)` and `(1,1,1,1)`.  The eight-step return from
the later west departure on level seven still goes through
`4:WL,3:SR,0:WR,1:NR,4:ER,3:SL,6:EL` and returns to the last north arrival.
The larger row has two right-turn endpoints and is impossible.  In the
smaller row the later west departure cannot be `SR`, because its target
would be the earlier `NL`.  Hence the consecutive endpoint pair is `NL,NR`,
forcing the complete stable row

```text
SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

The earlier west departure `SR` feeds `4:WR->5:NR` and then the first east
arrival on level eight.  The same four-step square used above forces this
`SR` to be immediately followed by `SL`, contradicting the displayed `NL`.
Thus level five cannot be wake while both levels six and seven are even.

Combining this with the preceding disjunction proves the growth-eight bound

```text
omega_6+omega_7 >= 1.
```

This uses exact P3 at the lower missing cap, together with P22 at growth eight.
No period bound or inward cap assumption is needed.  The
consequence for a guard at the opposite cap is proved in
`odd_skew_missing_guard_exclusion_2026-09-03.md`.

#### Last-exit cycles need not be unique

The standard period-104 trace already has two last-exit cycles.  In its
`d=(2,-2)` normalization, use quotient coordinates

```text
(t,r)=(x+y,x mod 2),       ell=(x-r)/2.
```

The latest lifted occurrences listed below give the two cycles

```text
(-1,1) -> (-2,1) -> (-1,0) -> (-2,0) -> (-1,1),
(-3,0) -> (-4,0) -> (-3,1) -> (-4,1) -> (-3,0).
```

Their phase indices are respectively `(97,98,99,16)` and `(19,20,21,26)` in
normalization A of `results/standard_highway.json`.  In each cycle exactly one
selected edge raises `ell`; the other three preserve it.  Both cycles therefore
have voltage one, exactly as the lemma requires.  This finite check is a direct
counterexample to uniqueness even under exact P3 alternation.  Any useful cap
argument must control how the two forced cap chains attach to positive-voltage
cycles; it cannot assume that the last-exit forest has only one such cycle.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-fat122-adjacent-wake-band-exclusion-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_fat122_adjacent_wake_band_exclusion_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_fat122_adjacent_wake_band_exclusion_2026-09-14.md`.

### Excluding the lower band with wakes at four, five, and six

At a lower `(1,2,2)` cap in the `(1,-3)` orbit,

```text
(omega_4,omega_5,omega_6)=(1,1,1)
```

is impossible. There are two positions for the remaining
north right turn on level seven. The first contradicts
lifted-time order; the second forces a third west departure
on level nine, where only two are available.

Combined with the preceding exclusions, this leaves only
`(omega_4,omega_5,omega_6)=(0,1,1)` at a lower `(1,2,2)`
cap. Its two wake families A and B remain open, as do the
other cap profiles in the `(1,-3)` orbit.

#### The fixed rows

Write `f=omega_7` and `g=omega_8`. The lower-band normal
form gives

```text
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR
5: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR
6: ER,EL,ER,WL,WR.
```

The level-seven theorem leaves

```text
P: SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,[SL]^(1-f);
Q: SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,SR,SL,NR,[SL]^(1-f).
```

Label the west departures on level seven by `W1,...,W5`.
There are five east arrivals `E1,...,E5` on level eight;
`E3=ER` is the fifth south departure. The two west
departures `D1,D2` on level nine feed `6:WL,WR`. Let
`Fi` denote the east arrivals on level ten.

#### The north right turn cannot precede W5

For P, the level-seven note already forces `f=0,g=1`
and the following tail on level eight:

```text
E3=ER,I=WL,WR,E4=EL,WR,WL,E5=ER.
```

Here `I`, the sixth south departure, feeds the `SL`
between `7:NR` and `7:W5`. It therefore supplies `F4`.
The preceding east arrival `F3` is supplied by the `NR`
whose source is `6:WR`. Thus

```text
tau(F4) = tau(I)+2-N < tau(E4),
tau(F3) = tau(6:WR)+2-N
        > tau(6:WL)+2-N = tau(E4).
```

This contradicts `F3<F4`. Hence P is impossible.

#### The tail forced by Q

In Q, `W5` is the sixth south arrival on level seven.
If `E5=EL`, its return through `W5` would force `E4=ER`
as the sixth south departure on level eight. This is
impossible after `E3=ER` at ordinal five: alternation
between successive east right turns requires an intervening
`WL`. Therefore `E5=ER`, at south ordinal seven.

Let `J` feed `W5`. The square

```text
8:J -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR -> 5:last NR -> 8:E5=ER
```

forces `J=WL`, immediately before `E5`. The south
ordinals and east order now force

```text
E3=ER,E4=EL,X=WR,J=WL,E5=ER.                         (1)
```

Indeed, `E4=ER` would put `E5` at south ordinal at least
nine; there is no other south departure between `E3`
and `J`, and no stable event between `J` and `E5`.

There are `8-f` south departures in total. If `f=1`,
`E5` is both the last east arrival and the last south
departure; it must end the word. If `f=0`, the remaining
south departure is `K=WL` and the tail is

```text
E3=ER,E4=EL,X=WR,J=WL,E5=ER,K=WL,[WR]^g.             (2)
```

#### The two west departures on level nine

The square from `D1` through `6:WL,5:SL2,E4=EL`
cannot have an `NL` source. Such a source would require
an incoming `WR` immediately preceding `E4`, whereas
its predecessor in (1) is `E3=ER`. Hence `D1=SR`,
immediately followed by `D2=NL`.

The three-step path from `D2` through `6:WR,7:NR` ends
at `F4`. If `F4=ER`, its south step would complete a
square return from `D2=NL` to a third west departure
`SR`. Therefore `F4=EL`.

The south predecessor of `D1` on level ten follows the
eight-step first return

```text
10:Y -> 9:D1=SR -> 6:WL -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=EL
     -> 9:D2=NL -> 6:WR -> 7:NR -> 10:F4=EL.
```

Consequently `Y=F3=ER`, immediately preceding `F4`.
The arrival `F3` is supplied by `7:SL3`, whose incoming
predecessor is `8:E5=ER`. The preceding east arrival
`F2` is supplied by `7:SL2`, from `8:E3=ER`.

#### The west departure feeding J must turn right

Let `P_J` be the west departure on level eleven feeding
`8:J`. It has the sixteen-step first return

```text
11:P_J -> 8:J=WL -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR -> 5:last NR
       -> 8:E5=ER -> 7:SL3 -> 10:F3=ER -> 9:D1=SR
       -> 6:WL -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=EL -> 9:D2=NL
       -> 6:WR -> 7:NR -> 10:F4=EL -> north arrival on 11.
```

If `P_J=NL`, its incoming north predecessor `Z` on level
ten precedes `F3`, since `F3=ER,F4=EL` are consecutive.
The eight-step first return

```text
10:Z -> 11:P_J=NL -> 8:J=WL -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR
     -> 5:last NR -> 8:E5=ER -> 7:SL3 -> 10:F3=ER
```

forces `Z=F2=EL`. The square

```text
8:E3=ER -> 7:SL2 -> 10:F2=EL -> 11:P_J=NL -> 8:J=WL
```

would then skip `E4,X` in (1). Thus `P_J=SR`.

Its sixteen-step return ends at the next west departure
`NL`. In particular, `J` cannot be the last west arrival
on level eight. This excludes `f=1`. In (2), the next
west arrival after `J` is `K`, so its matching departure
`P_K` on level eleven is `NL`, supplied by `10:F4=EL`.

#### A third west departure is unavoidable

Now `f=0`, and the last `SL` on level seven is supplied
by `8:K`. The square

```text
10:F4=EL -> 11:P_K=NL -> 8:K=WL -> 7:last SL -> 10:F5
```

forces `F5=ER`, immediately after `F4`. But then

```text
9:D2=NL -> 6:WR -> 7:NR -> 10:F4=EL -> 11:P_K=NL
        -> 8:K=WL -> 7:last SL -> 10:F5=ER -> south arrival on 9
```

is an eight-step first return to the cell of `D2`.
Its direction word is `W N E N W S E S`; no earlier
step revisits the starting cell. Since its source turns
