`odd_skew_fat122_five_lower_bands_2026-09-13.md`. Let `Wi` be the
west departures on level seven, and `Ni` its north arrivals. The
initial triple on level six gives

```text
W1=SR,       W2=SR,       W3=NL=N1.
```

The preceding note also proves `W4=SR`, immediately followed by
`SL`. Let `J` be the first `WL` on level six, and suppose a north
arrival `N2` exists on level seven. Its source `X` is the first
`WR` on level six and satisfies `tau(J)<tau(X)`.

Label the east arrivals on level eight as `Ei`. The fixed path
from `W4` gives `tau(E3)=tau(W4)+7`, whereas
`6:J -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4` gives `tau(E4)=tau(J)+2-N`.
The order `E3<E4` implies

```text
tau(W4) < tau(J)-N-5 < tau(X)+1 = tau(N2).
```

Thus `W4` occurs before any remaining north arrival. The word on
level seven starts `SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL`.

If `W5=NL=N2`, there would be no north right turn between
`N1=NL` and `N2=NL`. The intervening `W4=SR` is already followed
by `SL`. A right turn is still needed before `W5`, but it could
be neither a north arrival nor another west departure. This is
impossible.

The only other possible north ordinal for `W5=NL` is `N3`, when
`(c,e)=(0,1)`. Its source is the final `WR` on level six, after
the final `WL`. The path from `W5` through
`4:last WR,5:last NR` gives

```text
tau(E5)=tau(6:last WR)+4.
```

But `6:last WL -> 5:last SL -> 8:E6` gives
`tau(E6)=tau(6:last WL)+2-N`, contradicting `E5<E6`.
Hence `W5=SR` as well.

All remaining north arrivals are `NR`. Inserting their `k` copies
before or after `W5`, with alternation, proves the stated word.
For `(c,e)=(0,1)`, if both north arrivals preceded `W5`, the same
comparison would give `tau(E5)>tau(6:last WR)` and
`tau(E6)<tau(6:last WR)`. Thus `j=2` is excluded.

#### The band with `(c,e,j)=(1,1,1)`

Here the relevant fixed rows are

```text
5: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR
6: ER,EL,ER,WL,WR
7: SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,[SL]^(1-f).
```

On level eight, `E3=ER` is already forced by the return from
`W4=SR`; it is the fifth south departure. Let `D1,D2` be the two
west departures on level nine, feeding `6:WL,WR`. The three-step
path from `D2` through `6:WR,7:NR` ends at the third east arrival
`F` on level ten. The preceding east arrival `F'` there is supplied
by the `SL` immediately after `7:W4`.

Suppose first that `E5=EL`. The square from the south predecessor
of `W5` forces the consecutive pair `E4=ER,E5=EL`. The return

```text
D1 -> 6:WL -> 5:SL2 -> E4=ER -> 7:W5=SR
   -> 4:last WR -> 5:last NR -> E5=EL -> north arrival on 9
```

has length eight and is a first same-cell return on level nine.
The source cannot be `NL`: its incoming predecessor `X` on level
eight would be the north departure immediately preceding `E5`,
and `X -> D1 -> 6:WL -> 5:SL2 -> E4` would be a four-step first
return. It would force `X=EL=E3`, contradicting `E3=ER`.

Therefore `D1=SR`, with consecutive target `NL=D2`. If `F=ER`,
its south step would complete a four-step return from `D2=NL`
to a further `SR`, contradicting that `D2` is the last west
departure on level nine. Hence `F=EL`.

The south predecessor of `D1` on level ten follows the eight-step
return to `D2`, then the three-step path to `F`. This is a
twelve-step first same-cell return, so its source is the preceding
east arrival `F'=ER`. The resulting path is

```text
8:E3=ER -> 7:SL -> 10:F'=ER -> 9:D1=SR -> 6:WL
        -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=ER.
```

It has direction word `SESWSE`, length six, and displacement
`(1,-3)`. It would give `tau(E4)=tau(E3)+6-N`, contrary to
`E3<E4` and `N>=6`. Thus `E5=EL` is impossible.

It follows that `E5=ER`. Its south step returns from the last
right turn `W5=SR` to a following `SL`, forcing `f=0`. This is
the eighth and last south departure on level eight, and `E5` is
also its last east arrival. No left turn can follow it: an `EL`
would be another east arrival and a `WL` another south departure.
Thus level eight is wake.

#### The two words on level eight

Between the fifth south departure `E3=ER` and the eighth `E5=ER`
there are only two south departures. If `E4=ER`, alternation
between each successive pair of east right turns would require
an intervening `WL`, putting `E5` at south ordinal at least nine.
Therefore `E4=EL`.

Now `D1` has the four-step first return through
`6:WL,5:SL2,E4=EL` to a north arrival on level nine. If it were
`SR`, its target would again be `NL=D2`. The same argument with
`F,F'` would give the six-step translated path from `E3` to `E4`
and the same time contradiction. Hence `D1=NL`.

Its incoming predecessor on level eight must consequently be
`WR`, immediately preceding `E4=EL`. The seventh south departure
on level eight feeds `W5=SR`, so it is `WL`, immediately followed
by `E5=ER`. These facts fix the final block as

```text
E3=ER,WL,WR,E4=EL,WR,WL,E5=ER.
```

For the first two east arrivals, the square through `W1` allows
either initial `E1=ER,E2=EL`, or prefix
`WR,E1=EL,WR,WL,E2=ER`. The third and fourth south departures
before `E3` must be `WL`. Alternation then fixes the two complete
fifteen-visit words in the statement.

These are necessary orders, not traces. Both the `j=0`
continuation of this band and the listed `j=1` words are
subsequently excluded in
`odd_skew_fat122_adjacent_wake_band_exclusion_2026-09-14.md`.
The remaining lower-cap work is recorded in the case ledger.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-fat122-three-visit-six-2026-09-12-md"></a>

## odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_six_2026-09-12

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_six_2026-09-12.md`.

### The three-visit level-six branch requires two fat caps

Consider growth eight and drift `(1,-3)`, with a lower `(1,2,2)` cap.
Suppose level four is non-wake, level five is wake, and level six has
two `ER` ports. Then the wake and non-wake pattern through level eight is
forced:

```text
wake:      0,5,6,8
non-wake:  1,2,3,4,7.
```

Moreover, the upper cap is neither missing nor guard. It must be fat.
An upper `(1,2,3)` cap is possible only at widths `W=4 mod 6`.
These are all-period restrictions; the two-fat-cap continuation remains
unresolved.

#### Three visits on level six

The lower-cap normal form gives

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL
3: SR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL
5 A: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR
  B: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR.
```

With `e=omega_6`, the level-six counts are

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(r,3-r,5+e-3r,2-r).
```

For `r=2`, nonnegativity forces `e=1` and the entire stable word is
`ER,EL,ER`. The south departure feeding `5:SR` has the four-step return
through `5:SR,2:WR,3:NR` to the second east arrival on level six.
Its source is the first `ER` and its target is `EL`. The first south
arrival on level five is therefore `SR`, so word A is forced.

#### Levels seven and eight

There are four west departures and one north arrival on level seven.
The second west departure has the twelve-step return

```text
W2 -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER
   -> 3:first SL -> 6:first ER -> 5:SR -> 2:WR
   -> 3:NR -> 6:EL -> only north arrival on 7.
```

Its endpoints have opposite turns and are consecutive stable events.
The source must be `SR`, the target `NL`, giving

```text
7: SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,[SL]^(1-f),       f=omega_7.
```

Call the three right-turn south arrivals `SR1,SR2,SR3`. Level eight has
three east arrivals `E1,E2,E3`, supplied by `SL,NR1,NR2` on level five.
There are fixed paths

```text
7:SR1 -> 4:first WR -> 5:first NR -> E2,
7:SR3 -> 4:last WR -> 5:NL -> 2:WL -> 1:SL
      -> 4:EL -> 5:last NR -> E3.
```

The second path has length seven and ends at the physical cell immediately
north of its starting level-seven cell. If `E3=EL`, the south departure
feeding `SR3` would have an eight-step return to this `EL`, forcing its
opposite-turn source to be the immediately preceding east arrival `E2=ER`.
The first path would then give a four-step `SR1`-to-`SR3` return with
two right-turn endpoints. Thus `E3=ER`.

Its south step returns to the next stable visit after `SR3`, forcing that
visit to be `SL`. Hence `f=0`. The source of the eight-step level-eight
return is `WL`, immediately followed by `E3=ER`. These are the fourth
and fifth south departures there, and the fifth is the last.

The south predecessor of `SR1` has a four-step return to `E2`. If
`E2=EL`, that source is `E1=ER`. If `E2=ER`, the source is `WL`, and
`E1=EL` since it precedes the first south departure. Thus level eight
has two `ER` ports in either case. Its counts, with `b=omega_8`, are

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(2,1,2+b,3).
```

The visit `E3=ER` is both the last east arrival and the last south
departure. Every `EL` and `WL` consequently precedes it. No event can
follow it in an alternating stable word, since the next event would
have to turn left. The word ends with this right turn, so `b=1`.
The two possible stable words are

```text
8: ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER,
or WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,WL,ER.
```

This proves the claimed wake pattern without any assumption on level nine.

#### P22 removes both thin upper caps

The opposite checker signs at levels five and eight lie in residue two
modulo three, so that fibre must be the four-wake fibre. The two other
fibres each contain exactly two wakes. In particular,

```text
O_0={0,6},               |O_1|=2,               |O_2|=4.
```

There are five fixed non-wake levels through level eight. Eight distinct
wakes therefore require `W>=12`.

An upper guard supplies the three consecutive wakes `W-2,W-1,W`. One
of them lies in residue zero and is at least ten, contradicting
`O_0={0,6}`.

An upper missing cap supplies an opposite-sign pair at `W-4,W-1`.
That pair must lie in the four-wake fibre, residue two; otherwise a
second fibre would need four wakes. This forces `W=0 mod 3`, but the
upper endpoint `W` would then be an additional wake in `O_0`, again
impossible.

Both thin upper profiles are excluded. If the upper profile is `(1,2,3)`,
both `W` and `W-2` are wake. Neither can lie in `O_0`, so `W=1 mod 3`.
Since the width is even, this is `W=4 mod 6`.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-fat122-three-visit-wake-families-2026-09-13-md"></a>

## odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_wake_families_2026-09-13

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_wake_families_2026-09-13.md`.

### Wake families above the lower `0,5,6,8` band

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap theorem](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) excludes family A. Family B remains open. The local derivation and earlier family status below are retained.

Consider growth eight and drift `(1,-3)`. Assume a lower `(1,2,2)` cap,
with `omega_4=0`, `omega_5=1`, and two `ER` ports on level six. The
return argument in `odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_six_2026-09-12.md` forces

```text
O intersect [0,8] = {0,5,6,8},
O_0={0,6},                 |O_1|=2,                 |O_2|=4,
```

where `O_j` is the wake fibre modulo three. The upper cap is fat. The
two-sided cap bound from `odd_skew_fat122_two_sided_wake_2026-09-13.md`
reduces the remaining wake placements to the families below.

| Family | Width and wake set | Restrictions | Upper cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `W=6m+2`, `O={0,5,6,8,6r+4,6p+2,W-4,W}` | `1<=r<p<=m-1`, `r+p` even | `(1,2,2)` |
| B | `W=6m+4`, `O={0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W}` | `2<=p<r<=m-1`, `p+r` odd | `(1,2,3)` |
| C | `W=6m+4`, `O={0,5,6,8,6p+5,6r+4,W-5,W}` | `1<=p<r<=m-1`, `p+r` odd | `(1,2,2)` |

These are necessary conditions. They do not construct a trace. The further
return argument in `odd_skew_fat122_rigid_five_wake_2026-09-13.md` excludes
family C; families A and B remain open. In particular, the table is a reduction
of this lower-band branch, not a classification of the entire drift orbit.

The subsequent exclusions of the lower bands with `omega_4=1`
now make A and B exhaustive for a lower `(1,2,2)` cap. See
`odd_skew_fat122_adjacent_wake_band_exclusion_2026-09-14.md` and
the case ledger for that extension of scope. This still does not
classify the other cap profiles in the drift orbit.

The minimum widths allowed by the table are respectively `26`, `28`, and
`22`. No bounded search is used in the derivation.

#### The rank condition fixes the order of the extra wakes

The width is even and at least twelve. Since `W` is wake and `O_0={0,6}`,
it is not divisible by three. The four-wake fibre `O_2` already contains
the opposite-parity pair `5,8`. Its other two wakes must have equal
parity: the checker split in this fibre is `3+1`. Each two-wake fibre
also has equal checker signs.

Use the odd-skew rank condition

```text
I_01+I_02+I_12 = 0 mod 2,
I_ij = #{(u,v) in O_i x O_j : u<v}.
```

The usual cyclic shift of residue labels does not affect this parity,
since every fibre has even cardinality.

If `W=6m+2`, write

```text
O_1={b,c},       b<c,
O_2={5,8,a,W}.
```

All the extra wakes exceed eight; P22 makes `a` even. Direct pair counts
give

```text
I_01=4,       I_02=7,       I_12=2+[b<a]+[c<a].
```

The rank condition is therefore equivalent to `b<a<c`.

If `W=6m+4`, instead write

```text
O_1={b,W},
O_2={5,8,u,v},       u<v.
```

Here `b` is even, and

```text
I_01=4,       I_02=7,       I_12=[b<u]+[b<v].
```

Thus `u<b<v`. These interlacings will prevent a wake at upper offset
six from replacing the wake forced at offset four or five.

#### Width `W=6m+2`

The upper cap must have profile `(1,2,2)`: a `(1,2,3)` cap would put
an additional wake at `W-2` in `O_0`. Write `a=6p+2`, with `p>=2`.

Suppose first that the two wakes `b,c` are odd. Set
`b=6r+1`, `c=6s+1`. The interlacing gives `r<=p<s`. Upper offset one
is non-wake, so `s<=m-1`. Upper offset four has even parity in the
odd-only fibre `O_1`, and offset five belongs to the already full fibre
`O_0`. Neither is wake. Offset six could be wake only if `a=W-6`, or
`p=m-1`; that contradicts `p<s<=m-1`. Hence all of upper offsets four
through six are non-wake, contrary to the two-sided `(1,2,2)` cap bound.
This excludes the odd-`O_1` family at every width.

Consequently `b=6r+4`, `c=6s+4`, with

```text
1<=r<p<=s<=m-1.
```

Upper offset five is still non-wake. Offset four is wake exactly when
`s=m-1`. If offset six is wake, then `p=m-1`, which itself implies
`s=m-1`. The cap bound therefore forces `c=W-4`. This gives family A
apart from its moment parity.

#### Width `W=6m+4`

Write `b=6r+4`. If the extra wakes in `O_2` are even, put
`u=6p+2`, `v=6q+2`. The rank interlacing gives

```text
2<=p<=r<q<=m.
```

An upper `(1,2,2)` cap would make offset two non-wake, so `q<=m-1`.
Offset four is in `O_0`, and offset five is odd in a fibre whose only
odd wake is the lower level five. Both offsets are non-wake. Offset six
could be wake only for `b=W-6`, or `r=m-1`, contrary to
`r<q<=m-1`. The two-sided bound again excludes this entire cap pairing.

Thus the upper cap is `(1,2,3)`, and its wake at offset two gives
`v=W-2`, or `q=m`. This is family B before the moment condition.

If the extra wakes in `O_2` are odd, instead put
`u=6p+5`, `v=6q+5`. Interlacing gives

```text
1<=p<r<=q<=m-1.
```

The upper cap must now be `(1,2,2)`, since its offset two is even and
cannot equal the only even wake eight in `O_2`. Upper offset four is
non-wake. Offset five is wake exactly when `q=m-1`; offset six can be
wake only when `r=m-1`, which implies `q=m-1` as well. The cap bound
forces `v=W-5`, giving family C.

#### The moment parity

In cap-normalized levels the remaining additive condition is

```text
M = sum_(t in O) (-1)^(t+1) floor((t+1)/3) = 3 mod 4.
```

This is the same normalization used in
`odd_skew_guard_missing_families_2026-09-03.md`. The fixed lower wakes
`0,5,6,8` contribute `-3`.

For family A, substitution gives

```text
M = -5-2(r+p+2m).
```

Hence `r+p` is even. In family B the result is

```text
M = -7-2(p+r+2m),
```

so `p+r` is odd. This also rules out the earlier endpoint `p=r` and
strengthens `p<=r` to `p<r`. Family C gives

```text
M = -3+2(p-r),
```

again requiring `p+r` odd. The least possible parameter choices are
`(r,p,m)=(1,3,4)` in A, `(p,r,m)=(2,3,4)` in B, and `(p,r,m)=(1,2,3)`
in C, proving the stated width bounds.

#### A further restriction on family C

In upper inward coordinates family C has `omega_4=0`, `omega_5=1`.
Its offset six is wake exactly when `r=m-1`. Its offset eight is always
non-wake: `W-8` is even in residue two, but the only even wake of that
fibre is eight, and `W>=22`.

Let `e=omega_6` in these upper coordinates and let `z` count the `ER`
ports there after rotating the heading labels. The level-six count row is

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(z,3-z,5+e-3z,2-z).
```

The square through `5:SR,2:WR,3:NR` excludes `z=0`. If `z=2`,
nonnegativity forces the three-visit row `ER,EL,ER`, with `e=1`.
The level-seven and level-eight return argument in
`odd_skew_fat122_three_visit_six_2026-09-12.md` then forces a wake at
offset eight. That part of the argument uses only zero-displacement
returns and applies at the upper cap unchanged. It contradicts the
placement just established.

Therefore family C requires `z=1`: upper offset six would have exactly
`6+e` visits, with count row `(1,2,2+e,1)`. The subsequent return argument
in `odd_skew_fat122_rigid_five_wake_2026-09-13.md` excludes this last
possibility and hence family C. Families A and B remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-fat122-two-sided-wake-2026-09-13-md"></a>

## odd_skew_fat122_two_sided_wake_2026-09-13

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_fat122_two_sided_wake_2026-09-13.md`.

### A wake by inward offset six at either `(1,2,2)` cap

At either cap in the `(1,-3)` drift orbit, a profile `(1,2,2)` satisfies

```text
omega_4 + omega_5 + omega_6 >= 1,
```

where offsets are measured inward from that cap. Every return in this proof
has zero displacement. Thus the argument applies at the upper cap after
rotating the heading labels by 180 degrees, without a longitudinal time
inequality. The stronger lower-cap bound `omega_4+omega_5>=1` remains
specific to that orientation.

#### An ordinal test for adjacent west departures

Suppose two adjacent west departures `Wi,Wi+1` from a class have forced
first same-cell returns to north arrivals `Na,Nb`. These returns must
join consecutive stable events. If `Wi=SR`, its target is `Na=NL`;
being the next stable west departure, this target is `Wi+1`. The return
from `Wi+1` must then end at the next north arrival, `N(a+1)`.

Consequently, if `b!=a+1`, then `Wi` must instead be `NL`, with target
`Na=NR`. Consecutive order makes its source `N(a-1)`. In particular,
if `a=1` and `b!=2`, the two returns are impossible.

#### The six-offset band

Suppose offsets four, five, and six are non-wake. The cap normal form,
valid at either end, is

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL
3: SR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL
5 A: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL
  B: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

Level six has counts `(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(r,3-r,6-3r,3-r)` for `r=0,1,2`.
The four-step return through `5:SR,2:WR,3:NR` to the second east arrival
excludes `r=0`. For `r=2`, it leaves only word `ER,EL,ER,WL` with word
A on level five. Its exclusion by level nine in
`odd_skew_fat122_even_band_2026-09-12.md` uses only zero-displacement
returns and applies at either cap.

For `r=1`, there are seven possibilities after imposing that same square
return. Four end with `WL`. They are `U1,...,U4` in
`odd_skew_fat122_early_wake_2026-09-12.md`; their exclusions in the
sections on the two adjacent west departures and the remaining `U`
branches also use only zero-displacement returns.

For clarity, the seven possibilities here are obtained without the
lower-cap restriction on the last south departure. If the second east
arrival is `EL`, its consecutive opposite-turn source is the first east
arrival `ER`. For word A on level five these are the initial `ER,EL`;
for word B they have prefix `WR,WL,ER,EL`. If the second east arrival is
`ER`, the source is `WL` and word A is required, since B does not have
a south arrival after its `SR`. Placing the remaining `WR,WL,EL` ports
in alternating slots gives the four `U` words and precisely these three
additional words:

| Name | Level five | Level six |
|---|---|---|
| `V1` | A | `ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,WR,EL` |
| `V2` | B | `WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,EL` |
| `V3` | A | `WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL` |

These three end with `EL`; their exclusion is the part still needed for
the upper-cap statement.

#### Removing `V1` and `V2`

Number the four west departures on level seven as `W1,...,W4`. They
feed `WR,WL,WL,WR` on level four. The same return paths used for the
`U` branches now give

| Branch | First return | Second return |
|---|---|---|
| `V1` | `W2 -> N1` | `W3 -> N5` |
| `V2` | `W2 -> N2` | `W3 -> N5` |
| `V3` | `W2 -> N2` | `W3 -> N5` |

The second return is the four-step path through
`4:second WL,3:last SL,6:last EL`. The first has length twelve for
`V1,V2` and eight for `V3`.

The ordinal test excludes `V1`. In `V2`, it gives `W2=NL=N1`. The
resulting path

```text
6:first WR -> 7:W2 -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR
           -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:first SL -> 6:first ER
```

returns to level six in eight steps with two right-turn endpoints and
no intermediate visit there. Thus `V2` is impossible.

#### The two level-seven words for `V3`

The ordinal test again gives `W2=NL=N1`, immediately followed by `N2=NR`.
If `W3=NL`, its return to `N5=NR` makes it `N4`. Then `W1=SR` and
`W4=SR`: another `NL` after `N4` would have to be the already specified
`N5=NR`. If `W3=SR`, its consecutive target `N5=NL` is `W4`.
R-first order and the five north arrivals give exactly

```text
A7: SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,SR,[SL]^(1-f),
B7: SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,
```

where `f=omega_7`; word B7 requires `f=0` because its last north arrival
is the final left-turn west departure.

Let `E1,...,E4` be the east arrivals on level eight, supplied by
`SL1,NR1,NR2,SL2` on level five.

In B7, the path from `W4=NL` through
`4:last WR,5:NL,2:WL,1:SL,4:EL,5:last NR` ends at `E3`.
It cannot end at `ER`: the following south step would return to the
same level-seven cell after its last stable visit. Thus `E3=EL`.
The south predecessor of `W3=SR` has a twelve-step return to this
`EL`, first following the four-step return to `W4`, then the displayed
path. Its source is consequently `E2=ER`. But the three-step path
`W1=SR -> 4:first WR -> 5:first NR -> E2` then completes a four-step
return to `W3=SR` with two right-turn endpoints. This excludes B7.

In A7, the same three-step path from `W1=SR` and seven-step path from
`W4=SR` imply `E3=ER`: otherwise its opposite-turn south predecessor
would again be `E2=ER`, producing the same right-to-right return.
The south step from `E3` must lead to `SL` after `W4`, so `f=0`.

Here `W1=SR` is immediately followed by `NL`. Thus `E2` cannot be
`ER`, since that would complete a four-step return from `W1` to a
south arrival instead. Consequently `E2=EL` and its south-departure
predecessor is `E1=ER`. These are the initial two stable visits on
level eight. Its eight-step return through `W4` forces a consecutive
`WL,E3=ER` pair, which supplies the fourth and fifth south departures.
The fifth is last, so `E4=EL`. Flow and alternation leave

```text
8: ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER,EL,[WR]^e,
   e=omega_8.
```

#### The final pair of returns

There are five west departures on level nine, feeding the west arrivals
`WR,WR,WL,WL,WR` on level six. Write them as `D1,...,D5`. The third has
the twelve-step return

```text
D3 -> 6:first WL -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR -> 6:ER
   -> 5:first SL -> 8:first ER -> 7:first SR -> 4:first WR
   -> 5:first NR -> 8:first EL -> first north arrival on 9.
```

The fourth has the four-step return

```text
D4 -> 6:last WL -> 5:last SL -> 8:last EL
   -> fifth north arrival on 9.
```

They have no intermediate level-nine visits. Their targets are `N1,N5`.
The ordinal test for adjacent west departures makes these returns
incompatible. This excludes A7 and finishes the proof.

All paths used here have zero net displacement. Rotating the labels at the
upper cap preserves both their motion and their stable successor relations.
No estimate involving `N` was transferred between the two cap orientations.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-a7-exclusion-2026-09-08-md"></a>

## odd_skew_guard_missing_a7_exclusion_2026-09-08

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_guard_missing_a7_exclusion_2026-09-08.md`.

### All-period exclusion of the `a=7` guard/missing branch at drift `(1,-3)`

Later result (9 September 2026): [the complete guard/missing exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-complete-exclusion-2026-09-09-md) closes both lower-guard / upper-missing families. The remaining-family statements below describe the earlier stage; the full drift orbit remains open.

#### Statement

Consider a finite-support periodic Langton trace of growth eight in the odd-skew drift orbit represented by `d=(1,-3)`. Normalize the transverse coordinate so a lower guard cap is at levels `0,1,2` and an upper missing cap is at the far end.

The previously proved guard/missing classification leaves Family II

```text
W=6m+2,
a=6p+1,
b=6q,
1<=p<q<=m-1,
m+p+q even,
```

with wake set

```text
O={0,1,2,a,b,W-4,W-1,W}.
```

The subfamily `p=1`, equivalently `a=7`, is impossible at every period and every width.

Consequently Family II has `p>=2`. Its old lower width bound `W>=20` improves to `W>=32`: with `p>=2`, the first arithmetic possibility is `m=5,p=2,q=3`.

#### Local reduction

For `p=1`, `b=6q>=12` and `W>=20`, so the wake pattern on levels zero through nine is exactly

```text
1,1,1,0,0,0,0,1,0,0.
```

The existing lower-guard return argument fixes the first five stable rows to

```text
0: WR
1: NR
2: WR
3: SR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,WL
```

where even transverse levels have horizontal arrivals and odd levels have vertical arrivals.

From here there are no width or period parameters left in the local problem. For each next level, directed edge matching with the already fixed outer levels determines a finite list of port-count rows. P3 requires the stable class word to alternate `R,L,R,L,...` beginning with `R`. Parallel unit edges match in this stable order.

Whenever the resulting deterministic successor path returns to the same physical cell, the endpoint must be the immediately next event in that cell's stable word. A return to an earlier stable event or one that skips an intervening stable event is impossible, since the traced path contains every ant step between its endpoints.

#### Exact finite certificate

The accompanying audit `verify_odd_skew_guard_missing_a7.py` exhausts only these exact local possibilities. It contains no period cutoff, width cutoff, or visit-count cutoff.

Layer counts are:

```text
through level 5 (even):       2 survivors
through level 6 (even):      11 survivors
force level 7 wake:           1 survivor
force level 8 even:           2 survivors
force level 9 even:           0 survivors
```

After level seven, the unique surviving stable rows are

```text
5: SR,SL,NR,SL
6: WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL
7: SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR
```

At even level eight exactly two stable words survive:

```text
ER,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL
ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,EL
```

For either level-eight word, an even level nine has one of the four port-count rows

```text
(NR,NL,SR,SL) =
(4,0,5,9),
(3,1,4,6),
(2,2,3,3),
(1,3,2,0).
```

Across both level-eight states these rows have exactly 948 alternating stable words. Every one is rejected by an exact same-cell return of length 4, 8, or 12 that either returns backward in stable order or skips at least one required visit. No longer excursion is used.

Therefore a lower guard cannot have its first additional wake at level seven while levels eight and nine are even. The `a=7` Family-II branch has exactly that local pattern, so it is excluded for all widths and periods.

#### Scope

This closes an infinite parameter subfamily, not the entire `(1,-3)` drift orbit. Family I (`W=6m`, first extra wake `a=6p+2`) remains open, and Family II remains open for `p>=2`. The fat cap profiles `(1,2,2)` and `(1,2,3)` also remain outside this argument.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-complete-exclusion-2026-09-09-md"></a>

## odd_skew_guard_missing_complete_exclusion_2026-09-09

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_guard_missing_complete_exclusion_2026-09-09.md`.

### Complete exclusion of the lower-guard / upper-missing orientation for drift `(1,-3)`

At growth eight, the surviving guard/missing orientation was previously reduced to two wake-placement families:

```text
Family I:  W=6m,   a=6p+2, b=6q+1, 1<=p<q<=m-2.
Family II: W=6m+2, a=6p+1, b=6q,   1<=p<q<=m-1.
```

Both families are impossible at every period. The proof is entirely from the upper missing cap using exact stable chronology.

#### Universal upper-cap input

Use inward coordinate `u=W-t` from the upper missing cap and rotate all heading labels by 180 degrees. The fixed missing-cap rows are

```text
0: WR
1: NR
2: empty
3: SR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER.
```

Every extension below is checked by exact order-preserving edge matching and the same-cell successor rule: the first physical return to a class copy must land at the next stabilized visit.

If offsets 5 through 12 are all non-wake, direct stable-word enumeration gives

```text
u     tried stable words     exact survivors
5              1                    1
6              2                    2
7             38                    3
8             57                    2
9             92                    8
10          3326                    4
11          1896                    3
12          1156                    0
```

Thus an upper missing cap cannot have all offsets 5,...,12 non-wake.

#### Family II

Here

```text
W=6m+2,
u_b=W-b=6(m-q)+2,
u_a=W-a=6(m-p)+1,
u_b<u_a.
```

If `q<=m-2`, then `u_b>=14` and `u_a>=19`, so offsets 5,...,12 are non-wake. The universal table excludes the case.

If `q=m-1`, then `u_b=8` and `u_a>=13`. Exact propagation with wake schedule

```text
5,6,7 non-wake; 8 wake; 9,10,11,12 non-wake
```

gives survivor counts

```text
1,2,3,2,12,16,16,5.
```

At offset 13, both possible parities are impossible: wake if `u_a=13`, non-wake if `u_a>13`. A necessary-return transfer solver gives zero rows in either case. A standalone direct C++ enumerator independently exhausts all raw stable words and finds zero survivors for both parities.

Therefore Family II is impossible.

#### Family I

Here

```text
W=6m,
u_b=W-b=6(m-q)-1,
u_a=W-a=6(m-p)-2,
u_b<u_a.
```

If `q<=m-3`, then `u_b>=17` and `u_a>=22`, so offsets 5,...,12 are all non-wake. The universal table excludes the case.

If `q=m-2`, then `u_b=11`. Since `p<q`, `u_a>=16`, so offset 12 is non-wake. Direct exact enumeration with wake schedule

```text
5,6,7,8,9,10 non-wake; 11 wake; 12 non-wake
```

gives

```text
u      5   6   7   8   9   10   11   12
surv   1   2   3   2   8    4    1    0
```

The direct word counts at the last two steps are 1584 candidates at wake offset 11, leaving one exact state, then only 8 candidates at non-wake offset 12, leaving zero.

Therefore Family I is impossible.

#### Theorem

No growth-eight periodic trace with drift `(1,-3)` can have a lower guard cap and an upper missing cap.

Combined with the earlier all-period exclusions of missing/missing, missing/guard, and guard/guard, every remaining `(1,-3)` growth-eight trace must involve at least one fat cap profile `(1,2,2)` or `(1,2,3)`.

This does not yet close the drift orbit because the fat-cap cases remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-families-2026-09-03-md"></a>

## odd_skew_guard_missing_families_2026-09-03

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_guard_missing_families_2026-09-03.md`.

### Wake placements for a lower guard and an upper missing cap

Later result (9 September 2026): [the complete guard/missing exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-complete-exclusion-2026-09-09-md) closes both lower-guard / upper-missing families. The remaining-family statements below describe the earlier stage; the full drift orbit remains open.

For drift `(1,-3)` and growth eight, suppose the lower cap has profile
`(1,1,1)` and the upper cap has profile `(1,1,0)`.  Normalize the transverse
levels to `0<=t<=W`.  Every such trace has wake set

```text
O={0,1,2,a,b,W-4,W-1,W},       a<b,
```

in one of the following families:

| Family | Width and extra wake levels | Integer restrictions |
|---|---|---|
| I | `W=6m`, `a=6p+2`, `b=6q+1` | `1<=p<q<=m-2`, `m+p+q` even |
| II | `W=6m+2`, `a=6p+1`, `b=6q` | `1<=p<q<=m-1`, `m+p+q` even |

Family I requires `W>=30`.  Family II requires `W>=20`.  These are necessary
conditions, not constructions.  They do not exclude either family at arbitrary
width, and they do not address the cap profiles `(1,2,2)` or `(1,2,3)`.

The proof uses exact same-cell returns, followed by the previously proved
residue and rank conditions.  The additive moment supplies the final parameter
parity.  No enumeration is used.

#### A return lemma valid at either missing cap

Measure offsets inward from a missing cap.  The fixed wake offsets are
`0,1,4`.  If offsets six and seven are non-wake, offset five is also non-wake.

At the lower cap, use the usual port labels, consisting of incoming heading
and turn.  At the upper cap, rotate every heading label by 180 degrees.  This
keeps right and left turns unchanged and puts the local port equations into
the same form.  All returns below have zero displacement, so the argument
does not use the sign of the longitudinal voltage.

In these labels the fixed rows are

```text
0: WR
1: NR
2: empty
3: SR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER.
```

Suppose offset five were wake and offsets six and seven non-wake.  Port flow
and alternating stable order force

```text
5: NR
6: WR,EL.
```

The level-seven counts, in the order `(NR,NL,SR,SL)`, can only be
`(2,0,2,4)` or `(1,1,1,1)`.  Write `W1<W2` for its two west departures.
Order-preserving edge matching forces the return

```text
W2 -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR
   -> 4:ER -> 3:SL -> 6:EL -> last north arrival on 7.
```

Its direction word is `WSWNESEN`.  There is no intermediate level-seven
event.  The endpoints are therefore consecutive visits to the same physical
cell and must have opposite turns.  This rules out `(2,0,2,4)`, whose
endpoints would both turn right.

In `(1,1,1,1)`, `W2` cannot be `SR`: its opposite-turn target would be the
earlier west departure `NL`.  Thus `W2=NL`, followed immediately by `NR`.
The only alternating stable word with the other west departure earlier is

```text
7: SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

The first departure `SR` follows the path `4:WR -> 5:NR` to the first east
arrival `U` on level eight.  Let `V` be the level-eight south departure
feeding that `SR`.  The path from `V` to `U` is a four-step same-cell return.
It puts `V` before the first east arrival, so `V=WL`.  Alternation forces
`U=ER`.  Its southward step completes a four-step same-cell return starting
at `7:SR`, which must consequently be followed immediately by `SL`.  This
contradicts the displayed level-seven word.

The upper-cap version follows by the stated rotation of labels.  In contrast,
the stronger lower-cap bound `omega_6+omega_7>=1` uses a lifted-time inequality
whose sign changes at the upper cap; that stronger bound is not being
transferred here.

#### Residue and rank classification

The width `W` is even.  The cap wakes are distinct once `W>=8`, which is
necessary for eight distinct wake levels.  The upper level `W-2` is empty.
Let `O_j` contain the wake levels congruent to `j mod 3`.

P22 requires each signed fibre sum to be `2 mod 4`.  Thus every fibre has an
even number of wakes, at least two.  The opposite signs at `W-4,W-1` force
their fibre to contain four wakes; the other fibres contain two each.  Each
two-wake fibre has equal checker signs.  We may use `(-1)^t` for these signs,
since changing the common sign does not change `2 mod 4`.

Write

```text
I_ij = #{(u,v) in O_i x O_j : u<v},
I = I_01+I_02+I_12.
```

The rank condition is `I=0 mod 2`.  Replacing the normalized residue labels
`(t+1) mod 3` by `t mod 3` preserves this parity because every fibre has even
cardinality.

If `W=6m+4`, the opposite-sign pair `W-4,W-1` lies in residue zero.  The
opposite-sign pair `1,W` lies in residue one.  Both fibres would need at least
four wakes, and residue two needs at least two.  That exceeds growth eight.

##### Width divisible by six

For `W=6m`, the fibres have the form

```text
O_0={0,W},
O_1={1,b},
O_2={2,a,W-4,W-1}.
```

P22 gives `a=2 mod 6` and `b=1 mod 6`.  Since the cap levels are already
occupied, `a>=8`, `b>=7`, and both extra wakes lie below `W-4`.  Direct pair
counts give

```text
I_01=2,       I_02=4,       I_12=6+[b<a].
```

The rank condition forces `a<b`.  Put `a=6p+2` and `b=6q+1`.  Then
`1<=p<q<=m-1` before using chronology.

In upper inward coordinates `u=W-t`, offsets six and seven cannot be wake.
Offset six would be an additional member of `O_0={0,W}`.  Offset seven has
residue two and odd parity, whereas the only non-cap member of that fibre,
`a`, is even.  The return lemma therefore makes upper offset five non-wake
too.  That offset is `b=W-5` precisely when `q=m-1`.  Removing it gives

```text
1<=p<q<=m-2.
```

##### Width equal to two modulo six

For `W=6m+2`, P22 gives

```text
O_0={0,b},
O_1={1,a,W-4,W-1},
O_2={2,W},
```

with `a=1 mod 6` and `b=0 mod 6`.  Thus `a>=7` and `b>=6`.  The extra wakes
again lie below `W-4`; in particular `b` cannot be the empty level `W-2`.
The pair counts are

```text
I_01=6+[b<a],       I_02=3,       I_12=5.
```

Hence `a<b`.  Writing `a=6p+1` and `b=6q` gives

```text
1<=p<q<=m-1.
```

Upper offset six is excluded by `O_2={2,W}`.  A wake at upper offset seven
would mean `a=W-7`, or `p=m-1`, contrary to `p<q<=m-1`.  Upper offset five
already has the wrong parity for `b`.  Thus upper offsets five through seven
are non-wake in both families.

#### The additive moment and the width bounds

In cap-normalized levels, the odd-skew moment condition is

```text
M = sum_(t in O) (-1)^(t+1) floor((t+1)/3) = 3 mod 4.
```

This is the `(1,-3)` specialization proved in
`growth8_odd_skew_additive_2026-08-23.md`.  Substitution of the wake levels gives

```text
Family I:  M=2(q-p-m)-1,
Family II: M=2(p-q-m)-1.
```

In either case, `M=3 mod 4` is equivalent to `m+p+q` being even.

In Family I, `1<=p<q<=m-2` first permits `m=4`, with `p=1,q=2`.  Their sum
with `m` is odd, so `m=4` fails the moment condition.  Thus `m>=5` and
`W>=30`.  The parameters `m=5,p=1,q=2` pass the displayed conditions.

In Family II, `1<=p<q<=m-1` first permits `m=3,p=1,q=2`, whose sum is even.
Thus `W>=20`.  At that width, the only possible wake placement is

```text
O={0,1,2,7,12,16,19,20}.
```

Its normalized moment is `-9=3 mod 4`.  Passing these conditions does not
settle its full chronology.

The earlier width-18 placement

```text
O={0,1,2,8,13,14,17,18}
```

passes the static tests, but has an upper wake at
offset five while offsets six and seven are non-wake.  The short return lemma
therefore rules it out at every period.

#### The first even band above the lower guard

Both parameter families make lower levels three through six non-wake.  This
also imposes a short local restriction on their visit multiplicities.  In the
port-count order `(ER,EL,WR,WL)`, level six must have either

```text
(1,2,3,2) or (2,1,0,1).
```

The second case forces the stable rows

```text
5: SR,SL,NR,SL
6: ER,EL,ER,WL
7: SR,SL,SR,NL.
```

In particular, a wake at lower level seven requires the first count row on
level six, with eight visits.  This applies to the width-20 candidate above.

To prove the restriction, begin with the singleton lower-guard rows
`0:WR`, `1:NR`, and `2:WR`.  Even level three has port counts
`(NR,NL,SR,SL)=(1,0,1,2)`.  Its unique `SR` follows `0:WR -> 1:NR` to the sole
east arrival on level four.  The south departure feeding that `SR` and the
returning east arrival form a four-step same-cell return.  The source cannot
itself be the sole east arrival, so it is `WL` and the target is `ER`.

The even level-four count equations now force `WR,WL,ER,WL`.  Its first
south departure is the `WL` feeding `3:SR`, so `SR` is the first south arrival
on level three.  Hence

```text
3: SR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,WL.
```

Even level five has the same counts as level three and one of two words:

```text
A: NR,SL,SR,SL
B: SR,SL,NR,SL.
```

Level six has three east arrivals and three south departures.  If it has
`h` copies of `ER`, its complete count row is

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(h,3-h,6-3h,3-h),       h=0,1,2.
```

The unique `5:SR` follows `2:WR -> 3:NR` to the second east arrival `E2` on
level six.  Its south predecessor `D` on level six satisfies the four-step
same-cell return

```text
D -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR -> E2.
```

When `h=0`, the target is `EL` but no right-turn south source exists.  Thus
`h=0` is impossible.

For `h=2`, the only alternating words are

```text
X: ER,WL,ER,EL
Y: ER,EL,ER,WL.
```

In X, the displayed return has `D=WL`, the second south departure.  Thus
`5:SR` is the second south arrival, which gives word A on level five.
In Y, `D` is the first `ER`, hence the first south departure, giving word B.

Word X is impossible without any assumption on level seven.  Its last west
departure has the forced four-step return

```text
last W on 7 -> 4:last WL -> 3:last SL -> 6:EL -> only N arrival on 7.
```

If the target were `NL`, it would be a west departure after the last west
departure.  If the target were `NR`, there would be no `NL` on level seven,
so the source would be `SR` and both endpoints would turn right.

It remains to consider Y with word B.  Let `W2` be the second of the three
west departures on level seven.  Its successor path is

```text
W2 -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER
   -> 3:first SL -> 6:first ER -> 5:SR -> 2:WR
   -> 3:NR -> 6:EL -> only N arrival on 7.
```

This has direction word `WSWNESESWNEN`, zero displacement, and no intermediate
level-seven event.  A target `NR` again gives two right turns.  Therefore the
only north arrival is `NL`.  It follows `W2=SR` and is the last west departure.

The level-seven count equations now leave `(NR,NL,SR,SL)=(0,1,2,0)` if wake,
or `(0,1,2,1)` if non-wake.  In the wake case the `NL` cannot be last among
the west departures in an alternating `RLR` word.  In the non-wake case the
only compatible word is `SR,SL,SR,NL`, as claimed.  The middle level-six row
`(1,2,3,2)` has not been excluded.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-102-exclusion-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion_2026-09-22.md`.

### The lower fat `(1,2,3)` bands with `omega_3=0` and `(j,k,l)=(1,0,2)` are impossible

22 September 2026

For drift `(1,-3)`, the two lower-cap bands

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL,WR
3: SR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,[WR]^b          b = omega_4 in {0,1}
```

cannot occur at any period. These were two of the four `j=1,k=0` bands that
escape the long black-lifetime bounds of the September 12 normal form, and
two of the five lower `(1,2,3)` bands still open after the earlier exclusions
of 22 September. The remaining open lower `(1,2,3)` bands are now
`(1,1,1)` with `omega_3=0` and either `omega_4`, and `(1,1,0)` with
`omega_3=omega_4=1`.

#### Method and scope

Starting from the displayed rows, each later row is enumerated exhaustively.
For both wake values at every level, the flow and excess equations give every
possible count row, and every right/left alternating stable word with those
counts is considered. A word is discarded only if one of the following
necessary conditions fails:

1. a zero-displacement walk from a new event through already built rows
   returns to the new level, and the event it reaches is not the next stable
   event (applied while the word is built);
2. a witnessed finite same-cell first return lands on an event other than the
   next stable visit (walks are followed only through built rows; no quotient
   cycle is extrapolated);
3. the exact lifted clock is infeasible over the integers:

   ```text
   tau(target) = tau(source) + 1 - N*lambda,   lambda(E)=1, lambda(W)=-1,
   N >= 6,     tau(t,i+1) - tau(t,i) >= 4.
   ```

No P22 wake restriction, rank or moment condition, period upper bound, width bound,
row-length bound or black-lifetime bound is used. Rows above the upper cap are
empty and occur in the enumeration as the empty word, so an empty frontier at
level nine excludes the band for every width, including widths below nine.

| `omega_4` | Level | Count rows | Words | Rejected by return | Rejected by clock | Survivors |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 5 | 6 | 13 | 9 | 0 | 4 |
| 0 | 6 | 34 | 120 | 78 | 0 | 42 |
| 0 | 7 | 405 | 450 | 378 | 12 | 60 |
| 0 | 8 | 627 | 2,999 | 2,431 | 116 | 452 |
| 0 | 9 | 5,208 | 1,088 | 1,088 | 0 | **0** |
| 1 | 5 | 6 | 17 | 13 | 0 | 4 |
| 1 | 6 | 38 | 209 | 143 | 9 | 57 |
| 1 | 7 | 615 | 2,983 | 2,557 | 132 | 294 |
| 1 | 8 | 3,831 | 34,861 | 29,421 | 2,328 | 3,112 |
| 1 | 9 | 46,424 | 26,435 | 26,435 | 0 | **0** |

"Words" counts the words that pass condition 1 during construction. At level
nine every such word has a witnessed wrong first return; the clock is needed
only at levels six through eight.

#### Reproduction and independent checks

```bash
python code/python/verify_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion.py
```

The standalone checker uses Z3 for the clock and writes
`results/lower_fat123_a0_102_checks_2026-09-22.json` with
`all_checks_passed=true`. A separately written Java explorer, which decides
the clock with an exact parametric Bellman-Ford iteration instead of Z3,
reproduces every entry of the table, including the split between return and
clock rejections. With P22 wake pruning switched on, both bands still die at
level nine (4, 42, 60, 452, 0 and 4, 57, 240, 2936, 0 survivors). The same
enumeration gives exactly the published counts for the seven lower `(1,2,3)`
bands excluded earlier on 22 September, and matches the independent probe
counts through level eight recorded in the parallel working copy.

This is a computer-checked all-period exclusion under the stable-word
framework of `odd_skew_lower_fat_cap_normal_form_2026-09-12.md`. It is not a
Lean formalization, and it does not close the `(1,-3)` drift orbit.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-102-hand-proof-2026-09-23-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_hand_proof_2026-09-23

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_hand_proof_2026-09-23.md`.

### A first-return proof that the lower fat `(1,2,3)` bands `(0,b,1,0,2)` are impossible

23 September 2026

#### Result and scope

For drift `(1,-3)`, in lower-cap coordinates `t=3x+y`, the two lower
`(1,2,3)` bands with `omega_3=0` and `(j,k,l)=(1,0,2)`,

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR1,WL,WR2
3: SR,SL1,NR1,SL2,NR2,SL3
4: WR1,WL1,ER,EL,WR2,WL2,WR3,WL3,[WR4]^b          b = omega_4 in {0,1},
```

cannot occur at any period. The computer certificate
`odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion_2026-09-22.md` found that every
level-nine continuation fails a witnessed first return. This note gives a
hand proof. It explains the failure by one mechanism that works for both
values of `b`.

The proof uses only four facts: right/left alternation, ordinal edge
matching, the count of west departures on row five, and first returns
along explicit closed walks. It uses **no** lifted clock, no lower bound on
`N`, no wake parity at levels five to nine, and no P22, rank or moment
condition. Every case split below is shown to be exhaustive.

Idea. Short closed walks force the start of each row from five to eight.
The first four W-arrivals on row six are then fixed, and a twelve-step
return makes the fourth west departure on row nine the first north arrival
there. From that event, the walk through row six and row five reaches the
third east arrival `F3` on row eight. If `F3` turns left, the walk comes back to
row nine and skips a north arrival. If `F3` turns right, the walk goes down
into row seven. Prefix it with the forced four-step path
`7:W1 -> 4:WR1 -> 5:n1 -> 8:F2 -> 9:p1`. The result is a closed walk from
the first event of row seven that lands on a south arrival, while the next
stable event there is a north arrival.

#### Conventions

A port is an arrival heading followed by a turn. Departures are

```text
ER->S, EL->N, WR->N, WL->S,   NR->E, NL->W, SR->W, SL->E,
level shift E +3, W -3, N +1, S -1.
```

At an even level every event is an E-arrival (`ER`, `EL`), a `WR`
(W-arrival, N-departure) or a `WL` (W-arrival, S-departure). At an odd
level every R-port is an N-arrival (`NR`) or a W-departure (`SR`), and every
L-port is `NL` (both an N-arrival and a W-departure) or `SL`. The framework
facts used are those of `odd_skew_lower_fat_cap_normal_form_2026-09-12.md`:

* **(A) Alternation.** A nonempty stable word begins with R and alternates,
  so consecutive stable events have opposite turns.
* **(M) Ordinal matching.** The r-th departure with heading `h` from row `t`
  feeds the r-th arrival with heading `h` on row `t+shift(h)`. Events of a
  fixed arrival (or departure) class occur in the stable word in rank order.
* **(F) First return.** Consider a walk along successors that starts at
  event `e` on row `t`, has net displacement `(0,0)` (equal numbers of E
  and W steps, and of N and S steps), and meets row `t` at no intermediate
  event. Its endpoint is then the stable successor of `e`: it is the event
  immediately after `e` (so `e` is not the last event of its row), and it
  has the opposite turn. Every closed walk
  below meets its start row at no intermediate event, and each has zero
  net voltage.

"Closed walk" below means a walk with displacement `(0,0)` that satisfies
(F). The script `code/python/verify_lower_fat123_a0_102_hand_walks.py` checks each one step by step.

Edges inside rows 0-4 that are used (all by (M)):
`0:WR->1:NR`, `1:NR->4:ER`, `1:SL->4:EL`, `2:WR1->3:NR1`, `2:WL->1:SL`,
`2:WR2->3:NR2`, `3:SR->0:WR`, `4:WL1->3:SR`, `4:ER->3:SL1`.
Edges that leave or enter rows 0-4:

* the E-departures `3:SL1,NR1,SL2,NR2,SL3` feed the E-arrivals `E1,...,E5`
  of row six;
* the N-departures `4:WR1,EL,WR2,WR3,[WR4]` feed the N-arrivals
  `n1,n2,...,n(4+b)` of row five;
* row five has exactly three W-departures `D1,D2,D3`, feeding
  `2:WR1,2:WL,2:WR2`;
* the W-departures `W1,W2,...` of row seven feed `4:WR1,4:WL1,4:WR2,...`
  (there are `6+b` of them).

Rows five to nine are nonempty because they receive these arrivals. The
argument therefore holds at every width.

#### Step 1: row five

**(1a) `D2=SR`.** Suppose `D2=NL`. Then `D2` is also an N-arrival, fed by
some N-departure `U` on row four. The walk

```text
U -N-> 5:D2 -W-> 2:WL -S-> 1:SL -E-> 4:EL
```

is closed, so `U` immediately precedes `4:EL`. That predecessor is `4:ER`,
which departs south. This is a contradiction.

**(1b) `D3=n2=NL` immediately follows `D2`.** The walk

```text
5:D2 -W-> 2:WL -S-> 1:SL -E-> 4:EL -N-> 5:n2
```

is closed (`4:EL` is the second N-departure of row four). Hence `n2`
immediately follows `D2=SR` and turns left, so `n2=NL`. It is a
W-departure right after `D2`, so `n2=D3`.

**(1c) Row five begins `D1,SL,n1,SL,D2,D3` or `n1,SL,D1,SL,D2,D3`, and
`D1=SR`, `n1=NR`.** If `D1=NL`, then `D1` is an N-arrival before `n2`, so
`D1=n1`. The first event of the row is an R and cannot be `D1`, which turns
left. That event would then be an N-arrival before `n1` or a W-departure
before `D1`, which is impossible. Hence `D1=SR`. Every `NL` is a
W-departure and `D1=D2=SR`, so the only `NL` is `D3`, and `n1=NR`. Every R before `D2` is an N-arrival before `n2` (only `n1`) or a
W-departure before `D2` (only `D1`). Also, `n1` precedes `D2`, because it
precedes `n2=D3`, which immediately follows `D2`. So exactly two R's
precede `D2`. By (A), `D2` sits at position 4 and positions 0-3 read
`R,L,R,L`. The two L's are not `NL`, so they are `SL`.

We call the first form **case P**
(`SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL`) and the second **case Q** (`NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL`).
In both cases `D2` is the fourth S-arrival of row five. `D1` is the first S-arrival
in case P and the second in case Q. In case P, the first three S-arrivals
are `5:SR@0, 5:SL@1, 5:SL@3`, and the first three E-departures are
`5:SL@1, 5:NR@2=n1, 5:SL@3`. (Here `@i` is the position in the stable
word, counted from 0.)

(Alternation and the counts then give the whole row,
`X,SL,Y,SL,SR,NL,(NR,SL)^(1+b),NR,[SL]^(1-omega_5)`. Only the six-event
prefix is used below.)

#### Step 2: row six

Write `sigma_r` for the r-th S-departure of row six. It feeds the r-th
S-arrival of row five.

**(2a) `E4=EL`.** The walk `5:D3 -W-> 2:WR2 -N-> 3:NR2 -E-> 6:E4` is forced.
If `E4=ER`, one further S step returns to row five. This closed walk would
make the successor of `D3=NL` an R-port that arrives from the north, that
is `SR`. That would be a fourth W-departure, but row five has only three.

**(2b) `E3=ER=sigma_4`, and `E4` immediately follows it.** Let `Z=sigma_4`.
It feeds `D2`, the fourth S-arrival. The eight-step walk

```text
Z -S-> 5:D2 -W-> 2:WL -S-> 1:SL -E-> 4:EL -N-> 5:D3 -W-> 2:WR2 -N-> 3:NR2 -E-> 6:E4
```

is closed. So `E4=EL` immediately follows `Z`, and `Z` turns right. As an
S-departure it is `ER`, an E-arrival immediately before `E4`, so `Z=E3`.
In particular **exactly three S-departures precede `E3`.**

**(2c)** Let `Z1` be the S-departure feeding `D1`. It is `sigma_1` in case
P and `sigma_2` in case Q. The walk

```text
Z1 -S-> 5:D1 -W-> 2:WR1 -N-> 3:NR1 -E-> 6:E2
```

is closed. So `E2` immediately follows `Z1` and has the opposite turn.
`Z1` is `ER` or `WL`, which gives four cases.

* **P1 (case P, `Z1=ER`).** `Z1` is an E-arrival immediately before `E2`,
  so `Z1=E1=ER` and `E2=EL`. An event before `E1` would be neither an
  E-arrival nor an S-departure, hence a `WR`. The event just before
  `E1=ER` would then be an L, which is impossible. So row six begins
  `ER(E1),EL(E2)`, and `E2` is its **first** N-departure.
* **P2 (case P, `Z1=WL`).** `E2=ER` immediately follows `Z1=sigma_1`. `E1`
  precedes `Z1` and is not an S-departure, so `E1=EL`. Position 0 is an R
  that is not an E-arrival (`E1` turns left), so it is `WR`. Position 1 is
  `E1`: a `WL` there would be `Z1`, which has `E1` before it. Position 2
  is R. It cannot be `E2`, since `Z1` must come directly before `E2`, so
  it is `WR`. Position 3 is an L that is not an E-arrival (`E2` turns
  right), so it is `WL=sigma_1=Z1`, and position 4 is `E2=ER=sigma_2`.
  Position 5 is an L and not `E3=ER`, so it is `WL=sigma_3`. If position 6
  were `WR`, position 7 would be a fourth S-departure before `E3`. So
  position 6 is `E3`, and position 7 is `E4`:

  ```text
  6: WR, EL(E1), WR, WL, ER(E2), WL, ER(E3), EL(E4), ...
  ```

  Here `E1` is the **second** N-departure. The first four W-arrivals are
  `@0,@2,@3,@5`, and `6:WL@3, 6:ER@4, 6:WL@5` are `sigma_1,sigma_2,sigma_3`.
* **Q1 (case Q, `Z1=ER`).** As in P1, `Z1=E1=ER` and `E2=EL`. Now
  `sigma_1` precedes `E1` and is a `WL`, so position 0 is `WR` and position 1
  is `WL=sigma_1`. If position 2 were `WR`, position 3 would be an L before
  `E1`, which is a second `WL` before `Z1=sigma_2`. So row six begins
  `WR,WL,ER(E1),EL(E2)`, and `E2` is the **second** N-departure.
* **Q2 (case Q, `Z1=WL`)** is impossible. `E2=ER` immediately follows
  `Z1=sigma_2`, so `E2=sigma_3` while `E3=sigma_4`. The L right after `E2`
  would be an E-arrival between `E2` and `E3` or an S-departure between
  `sigma_3` and `sigma_4`, and neither exists.

#### Step 3: row seven (only P2 survives)

Let `m_r` be the r-th N-arrival of row seven, fed by the r-th N-departure of
row six. Let `Gamma` be the forced walk from `4:ER`:

```text
P2:  4:ER -> 3:SL1 -> 6:E1(EL) -> 7:m2
P1:  4:ER -> 3:SL1 -> 6:E1(ER) -> 5:D1 -> 2:WR1 -> 3:NR1 -> 6:E2(EL) -> 7:m1
Q1:  4:ER -> 3:SL1 -> 6:E1(ER) -> 5:D1 -> 2:WR1 -> 3:NR1 -> 6:E2(EL) -> 7:m2
```

In P1 `E1` is `sigma_1`, which feeds the first S-arrival `D1` of case P. In
Q1 it is `sigma_2`, which feeds the second S-arrival `D1` of case Q. Write
`q=1` in P1 and `q=2` otherwise. `Gamma` has displacement `(1,0)` and no
row-four event after its start.

**(3a) `m_q=NR`.** If `m_q=NL`, it departs west into a W-arrival of row
four. That closes `Gamma` into a walk from `4:ER` that satisfies (F). The
stable successor of `4:ER` is `4:EL`, an E-arrival (this is where `k=0` is
used). Contradiction.

**(3b)** The walk `7:W2 -W-> 4:WL1 -S-> 3:SR -W-> 0:WR -N-> 1:NR -E->`
`Gamma` ends at `m_q`, and it is closed (8 steps in P2, 12 in P1 and Q1).
So `m_q=NR` immediately follows `W2`, and `W2` turns left: `W2=NL`, an
N-arrival before `m_q`. In **P1** (`q=1`) no N-arrival precedes `m1`, which is
a contradiction. In P2 and Q1, `W2=m1`, and `m2` follows it immediately.

**(3c) Q1 is impossible.** The walk

```text
6:WR@0 -N-> 7:m1=W2 -W-> 4:WL1 -S-> 3:SR -W-> 0:WR -N-> 1:NR -E-> 4:ER -S-> 3:SL1 -E-> 6:E1
```

is closed. `6:WR@0` is the first N-departure. The walk lands on `E1`,
which is at position 2 in Q1, but the successor of position 0 is position
1. (In P2 the same walk lands on position 1.)

**(3d) In P2, row seven begins `SR(W1), NL(W2=m1), NR(m2)`.** Events before
`W2=m1` are not N-arrivals, and the only W-departure among them is `W1`. So
the only R available before `W2` is `W1=SR`. Position 0 is therefore `W1`.
Position 1 is an L. If it were an `SL`, position 2 would be an R before
`W2`, since `W2` turns left and cannot sit at position 2. No such R
exists, so position 1 is `W2`.

#### Step 4: row eight begins `ER,EL`

Let `F1,F2,F3` be the first three E-arrivals of row eight, fed by
`5:SL@1, 5:n1, 5:SL@3` (Step 1, case P). The forced walk

```text
Delta:  7:W1 -W-> 4:WR1 -N-> 5:n1 -E-> 8:F2
```

has displacement `(0,1)`.

**(4a) `F2=EL`.** If `F2=ER`, its S step closes `Delta` from `7:W1` and lands
on an S-arrival of row seven. The successor of `W1=7:SR@0` is the
N-arrival `7:NL@1`, so this is a contradiction.

**(4b) `F1=ER` is the first S-departure, at position 0.** The first
S-departure `Y1` of row eight feeds the first S-arrival of row seven,
namely `W1`. The walk `Y1 -S-> Delta` is closed, so `Y1` immediately
precedes `F2=EL` and turns right. It is therefore `ER`, and hence `F1`. An
event before `F1` is neither an E-arrival nor an S-departure, so it is
`WR`. The event just before `F1=ER` would then be an L, which is
impossible. So row eight begins `ER(F1),EL(F2)`, and `F2` is its first
N-departure.

#### Step 5: every row nine is contradictory

Let `X_r` be the r-th W-departure of row nine. It feeds the r-th W-arrival
of row six, so `X3->6:WL@3` and `X4->6:WL@5`. Let `p_r` be the r-th
N-arrival of row nine; `p1` is fed by `F2`.

**(5a) `X3=SR` and `p1=NL=X4`, consecutively.** The twelve-step walk

```text
9:X3 -W-> 6:WL@3 -S-> 5:D1 -W-> 2:WR1 -N-> 3:NR1 -E-> 6:ER@4 -S-> 5:SL@1
     -E-> 8:F1 -S-> 7:W1 -W-> 4:WR1 -N-> 5:n1 -E-> 8:F2 -N-> 9:p1
```

is closed. It uses `6:WL@3=sigma_1`, `6:ER@4=E2=sigma_2`, `5:SL@1` as the
first E-departure, and `F1` as the first S-departure. So `p1` immediately
follows `X3` with the opposite turn. `X3=NL` would be an N-arrival before
`p1`, so `X3=SR`, `p1=NL`, and `p1` is the next W-departure `X4`. (With
(A) this forces row nine to begin `SR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL`.)

**(5b) The contradiction.** The forced walk

```text
9:X4 -W-> 6:WL@5 -S-> 5:SL@3 -E-> 8:F3
```

uses `6:WL@5` (the fourth W-arrival and `sigma_3`), which feeds the third
S-arrival `5:SL@3`, the third E-departure. `F3` has two possible ports.

* `F3=EL`. Then `F3` feeds `p_q`, where `q` is its rank among
  N-departures. `F2` and `F3` are consecutive E-arrivals and both turn left.
  The event right after `F2` is therefore an R that is not an E-arrival, a
  `WR` N-departure. So `q>=3`. The walk from `X4=p1` to `p_q` is closed,
  so `p_q` would be the stable successor of `p1`, but `p2` lies strictly
  between them.
* `F3=ER`. Then `F3` departs south into row seven, and the eight-step walk

  ```text
  7:W1 -> 4:WR1 -> 5:n1 -> 8:F2 -> 9:p1=X4 -> 6:WL@5 -> 5:SL@3 -> 8:F3 -> 7:(S-arrival)
  ```

  is closed. Its directions are `W,N,E,N,W,S,E,S`, and it meets row seven
  at no intermediate event. So it must end at the successor `7:NL@1` of
  `W1`, but it ends at an S-arrival.

Every choice of row nine is contradictory. Both initial bands are
impossible at every period. ∎

#### Remarks

* **What is used where.** `k=0` (`4:ER` immediately followed by `4:EL`) is
  used in (1a) and (3a). `j=1` (rows three and four begin
  `SR,SL1` and `WR1,WL1,ER`) fixes the edges `4:WL1->3:SR` and
  `4:ER->3:SL1` used in (3b) and (3c). The detailed forms of rows three and
  four fix the edges `3:NR1->E2` and `3:NR2->E4` and the ranks used in
  Step 2. `b` enters only through the row lengths, and no wake indicator
  at levels 5-9 is used.
* **The clock is not needed.** The published enumeration applied the
  lifted clock at every level and recorded clock rejections at levels 6-8.
  The hand proof shows these are inessential. As a consistency check (not
  a premise), rerunning the same enumeration with the clock switched off
  gives:

  | `b` | level 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 |
  |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | 0 | 4 (P 2, Q 2) | 42 (P1, P2, Q1: 14 each) | 72 (all P2) | 744 (all P2) | **0** |
  | 1 | 4 (P 2, Q 2) | 66 (22 each) | 504 (all P2) | see log | **see log** |

  The survivors at every level have exactly the prefixes derived above
  (a no-clock rerun of the enumeration asserts them).
* **Contrast with the upper cap.** At an upper `(1,2,3)` cap, the band
  `(1,0,2)` already dies at level five by an `N+10` clock inequality
  (`odd_skew_upper_fat123_four_band_reduction_2026-09-21.md`). At the lower
  cap the voltage signs reverse, and the same chain only bounds `N` from
  below. Put `V=tau(2:WL)`. Steps 1-2 give `tau(2:WR2)=V+4` and
  `tau(4:WL2)=V-4`, and the successor `n3` of `D3` then forces `N>=14`.
  There is no contradiction there. The obstruction is instead the purely
  combinatorial ladder of Steps 1-5.
* **Scope.** This is a hand proof inside the stable-word framework of the
  September 12 normal form (alternation, ordinal matching, and first
  returns, all proved there). It does not rely on the computer
  enumeration. It does not touch the open lower bands `(0,*,1,1,1)` and
  `(1,1,1,1,0)`, where `k=1` removes the step (3a).

#### Checks

* `python code/python/verify_lower_fat123_a0_102_hand_walks.py` (self-contained) re-derives every
  displayed walk from the stated row prefixes for `b=0,1`. For each walk it asserts the rank and
  heading of every step, the endpoint, zero displacement and voltage, and the absence of
  intermediate visits to the start row (`all walk certificates passed`).
* As evidence only, the enumeration `code/python/verify_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion.py`, rerun
  with the clock switched off, finds survivors with exactly the prefixes derived in Steps 1-4 at
  every level, and an empty level-nine frontier.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-111-open-prefix-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_111_open_prefix_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_111_open_prefix_2026-09-22.md`.

### Lower fat `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=0`, `(j,k,l)=111`: bounded frontier

22 September 2026. Both `omega_4` branches remain open. This is a necessary-prefix computation, not a periodic ant construction.

The initial stable rows are

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL,WR
3: SR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR,WL,[WR]^b     b = omega_4 in {0,1}.
```

`probe_111.py` derives every later port-count row from the flow and binary wake equations, generates all alternating stable words subject to necessary zero-voltage boundary return adjacency, rejects witnessed finite wrong first returns, and solves the exact lower-sign lifted clock equations over the integers with `N>=6` and successive same-cell gaps at least four. It uses no period upper bound, width, row-size, or lifetime cutoff, and no wake restriction. `probe_111_p22.py` adds only the necessary condition that the partial wake set extend to P22's `4+2+2` signed residue-three fibres. It does not infer a quotient-cycle return from repeated vertices.

| `omega_4` | Wake filter | Level 5 | Level 6 | Level 7 | Level 8 | Level 9 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | P22 partial extension | 14 | 50 | 32 | 102 | 394 |
| 1 | none | 16 | 80 | 319 | 675 | not checked |

For `omega_4=0`, P22 rejects no wake choice through level nine, so the same counts hold without that filter. At level nine, the 102 incoming prefixes induce 1,024 port-count rows and 3,016 alternating words satisfying necessary adjacency. Of these, 1,408 fail a witnessed first return and 1,214 fail the exact lifted clocks, leaving 394. The saved JSON includes wake-pattern counts and three full survivor prefixes. For `omega_4=1`, the unrestricted level-eight computation finds 4,192 port-count rows and 11,834 adjacency words; 8,475 fail returns and 2,684 fail clocks, leaving 675. Its JSON includes the complete level-by-level counts.

The level-ten `omega_4=0` expansion was stopped without a result. No level-nine `omega_4=1` expansion was run. A nonempty prefix does not establish a consistent infinite orbit or a periodic trace; neither branch is excluded by these tests.

Reproduce from the repository root with:

```text
python work_a0_111/probe_111_p22.py 4 0 9
python work_a0_111/probe_111.py 4 1 8
```

The corresponding saved results are `probe_111_p22_b0_through9.json` and `probe_111_b1_through8.json` in `work_a0_111/`.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-b0-111-upper-guard-families-2026-09-26-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_guard_families_2026-09-26

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_guard_families_2026-09-26.md`.

### All-width wake families: lower fat `(0,0,111)`, upper guard

Later results: [the early-wake exclusions](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-guard-early-wakes-2026-09-26-md) remove the two width-20 placements below, and [the width-22 join](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-b0-111-w22-guard-2026-09-27-md) excludes the remaining width-22 cases. This note retains the arithmetic classification; the cap pair at larger widths remains open.

For drift `(1,-3)` at growth eight, put the lower transverse level at zero
and the upper guard at even width `W`. The lower initial band fixes wakes
`0,2` and non-wakes `1,3,4`; the upper guard fixes wakes `W-2,W-1,W`.
The other three wakes must lie between levels five and `W-3`. The P22,
even-rank, and moment conditions are necessary for a highway. The
[upper-guard hand lemma](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-guard-offsets-3-6-impossible-2026-09-26-md)
adds that one of these three wakes must be at `W-3,W-4,W-5,W-6`.

Write `W=6m+r`, with `r` in `{0,2,4}`. Exactly **nine parameter families**
pass these four necessary conditions. In the following table, `F` means
`{0,2,W-2,W-1,W}`, and the displayed triple is added to `F`. All indices
are integers. Empty index ranges contribute no placements.

| `r` | Free wake triple | Index restrictions |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `6a+4, 6b+2, W-4` | `1<=a<b<=m-2`, `a+b` even |
| 4 | `6a+4, 6b, W-4` | `1<=a<b<=m-1`, `a+b` odd |
| 4 | `6b, 6c, W-6` | `1<=b<c<=m-1`, `b+c` even |
| 4 | `6b+3, 6c+3, W-6` | `1<=b<c<=m-1`, `b+c` odd |
| 2 | `6a+4, 6b+4, W-4` | `1<=a<b<=m-2`, `a+b` odd |
| 2 | `6b+1, 6c+1, W-4` | `1<=b<c<=m-1`, `b+c` even |
| 2 | `6c+1, 6a, W-5` | `1<=c<a<=m-1`, `a-c` odd |
| 2 | `6c+1, 6a+2, W-3` | `1<=c<a<=m-1`, `a-c` odd |
| 2 | `6c+1, 6b+5, W-6` | `1<=c<b<=m-2`, `b+c` odd |

These families are *static candidates*. No line in the table asserts a
return-consistent event order or a realizable ant path.

#### Derivation

The signed P22 sum in each residue fibre modulo three must be `2 mod 4`.
For `r=0`, the fixed wakes have signed sums `(2,1,0)` in fibres
`(0,1,2)`. Three free wakes therefore consist of one even wake in fibre
one and a same-parity pair in fibre two. For `r=4`, the fixed sums are
`(0,1,2)`, giving one even fibre-one wake and a same-parity pair in fibre
zero. For `r=2`, the fixed sums are `(2,-1,2)`. The three free wakes are
either all in fibre one (all even, or one even and two odd), or one odd
fibre-one wake together with an opposite-parity pair in fibre zero or two.
These possibilities exhaust P22 because each signed free contribution is
`+1` or `-1`, and there are exactly three of them.

All free wakes sit above both fixed lower wakes and below all fixed upper
wakes. Direct cross-fibre pair counting then makes the even-rank condition
an ordering rule. At `r=0`, the singleton fibre-one wake must precede both
fibre-two wakes. At `r=4`, the singleton must lie outside the pair's
interval. At `r=2`, a triple entirely in fibre one always has even rank;
for a pair in fibre zero or two, the odd fibre-one singleton must lie
outside the pair's interval. This count is independent of the widths and
of the spacings between the three free wakes.

The five fixed wakes contribute `2m` to the moment
`M=sum_{t in O}(-1)^t floor(t/3)`. A free wake `6q+s` contributes
`(+2q, -2q, +2q, -(2q+1), +(2q+1), -(2q+1))` for
`s=(0,1,2,3,4,5)` respectively. Requiring `M=3 mod 4` gives the parity
condition in each table row. The forced upper wake restricts the indices:

* At `r=0`, only the even fibre-two pair can contain a wake among the
  upper inward offsets three through six, and that wake is `W-4`.
* At `r=4`, either the even fibre-zero pair reaches `W-4`, or the even
  fibre-one singleton is `W-6`. The outside-interval rank rule separates
  the three listed cases.
* At `r=2`, a fibre-one triple must contain `W-4`. If the pair is in
  fibre zero, its odd member must be `W-5`. If the pair is in fibre two,
  its odd member can be `W-3` or its even member can be `W-6`.

These exhaust the locations of the required near-upper wake, proving the
table for every even width. The checker
`code/python/lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_guard_schedules.py` independently
enumerates all triples and compares with the table through width 160.
That finite audit tests the algebra; it is not the basis for the all-width
claim.

The minimum static width is 20. At that width the only two remaining
placements are `{0,2,7,12,15,18,19,20}` and
`{0,2,7,14,17,18,19,20}`, from the `r=2` fibre-zero and fibre-two
odd-top families respectively. Neither has yet been excluded by this
arithmetic classification.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-b0-111-upper-missing-wakes-2026-09-24-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_missing_wakes_2026-09-24

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_missing_wakes_2026-09-24.md`.

### Wake family for lower fat `(0,0,111)` with an upper missing cap

24 September 2026. At drift `(1,-3)`, let the lower `(1,2,3)` cap have
`omega_3=omega_4=0` and `(j,k,l)=(1,1,1)`. If the upper cap is missing,
then P22, the even rank condition, the additive moment, and the all-period
missing-cap offset-five/six theorem leave exactly one wake family:

```text
W = 6m+4,
O = {0,2,6i+2,6j,W-6,W-4,W-1,W},
1 <= i < j <= m-1,  i+j even.
```

In particular **`W>=28`**. This is a necessary wake classification, not an
exclusion of the cap pair or a construction of an ant orbit. The first
placement is `W=28`, `O={0,2,8,18,22,24,27,28}`.

The lower cap supplies wakes `0,2` and non-wakes `1,3,4`. The upper missing
cap supplies wakes `W,W-1,W-4`, non-wakes `W-2,W-3`, and, by the missing-cap
theorem, a non-wake at inward offset five and a wake at offset six.
Thus `W-6` is wake and `W-5` is non-wake. Both cap arrivals are horizontal, so
`W` is even. The six fixed wakes are distinct in any admissible placement.

P22 requires signed wake sum `2 mod 4` in each residue-three fibre. If
`W=0 mod 6`, the fixed residue-zero fibre already has three even wakes
and needs an odd fourth. The residue-two fibre has two even and one odd
and needs an even fourth. The residue-one fibre is empty and needs at least
two more wakes. Four extras would be needed, but only two remain.
If `W=2 mod 6`, residue two has three even fixed wakes and needs one
more; residue one has one even and one odd, so it needs at least two more;
residue zero contains only level zero and needs another. Again two extras
cannot suffice.

For `W=6m+4`, residue one already has the even pair `{W-6,W}`.
Residue zero has `{0,W-4,W-1}`, two even and one odd, and needs one
even wake `6j`. Residue two has only the even wake `2` and needs one
even wake `6i+2`. Distinctness and cap non-wakes give
`1<=i,j<=m-1`. These are all eight wakes.

The even-rank interlacing count across the three fibres is
`I=8+[6j<6i+2]`. Hence `I=0 mod 2` exactly when `i<j`.
The normalized odd-skew moment is
`M=sum_{t in O}(-1)^t floor(t/3)=4m-1+2(i+j) mod 4`.
Its required value `3 mod 4` is equivalent to `i+j` even. The smallest
indices with `1<=i<j` and even sum are `(i,j)=(1,3)`, requiring `m>=4`
and therefore `W>=28`.

From the upper cap, the only possible wake patterns through inward
offset fourteen are `{0,1,4,6}` and `{0,1,4,6,10}`. Offset ten is wake
exactly when `j=m-1`. The other free wake has upper offset at least
fourteen; equality at fourteen would require `i=m-2,j=m-1`, whose sum is
odd, so offset fourteen is in fact non-wake. This agrees with the earlier
finite upper-prefix audit. On the lower side, levels five through seven
are non-wake, and level eight can be wake only when `i=1`.

The independent checker
`code/python/lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_missing_schedules.py` enumerates
all eight-wake sets through even width 160 under the stated endpoint,
P22, rank and moment conditions, and compares them exactly with the
closed form. It finds 1,222 placements and records its output in
`results/lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_upper_missing_schedules_2026-09-24.json`.
The derivation above, rather than the finite audit, establishes the
all-width scope. Stable words and clocks have not yet ruled out the
remaining family.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-b0-111-w22-guard-2026-09-27-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_w22_guard_2026-09-27

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_w22_guard_2026-09-27.md`.

### Width 22 upper guard excluded for lower fat `(0,0,111)`

At growth eight and drift `(1,-3)`, the lower fat `(1,2,3)` band
`(omega_3,omega_4,j,k,l)=(0,0,1,1,1)` has two static width-22 upper-guard
wake placements after P22, rank and moment. The upper-guard early-wake
prefix lemma already excludes `{0,2,9,15,16,20,21,22}`. This certificate
excludes the other one,

```text
W=22,  O={0,2,10,12,18,20,21,22}.
```

Consequently this cap pair requires `W>=26`: width 20 is excluded by the
early-wake lemma, width 22 by its two parts, and the all-width static
classification has no width-24 placement. Wider placements and the full
drift orbit remain open. The first remaining static width is 26, with
three wake placements; this certificate does not test their stable words.

#### Two-cap exclusion

The verifier grows lower words through row nine from the fixed lower band,
and upper-guard words through inward offset nine. There are 12 and 222
exact-integer-clock prefixes. A necessary full-width port-count model has
one variable for every row and port, enforces nonnegative counts, row parity,
the eight wake excesses, directional flow, and one of the three count
profiles among the 12 lower-nine prefixes. The lower cap is then extended
through row ten and the upper cap
through inward offset ten. Exact clocks yield 2,815 lower and 2,095 upper
prefixes. The full-width count model leaves 1,409 upper prefixes. A
necessary center-count lookahead then leaves 2,182 lower prefixes. Neither
filter can discard an actual orbit.

At the physical center row eleven, the upper prefixes allow 28 rotated port
count vectors. Enumerating all lower row-eleven count vectors, alternating
words, finite first returns and exact integer clocks leaves 3,395 lower
prefixes with 2,165 distinct center words. The lower row-eleven sweep checks
17,610 count options; 15,325 miss every upper center count, 115,185 of
141,699 generated adjacency words have wrong finite returns, and 23,119
more fail exact clocks. These figures leave 3,395 words.

Each upper inward-ten prefix is joined to every rotated lower center word
with the required upper row-eleven counts. There are 1,723,256 such
center-word pairs. An explicit finite wrong return rejects 1,722,896;
an exact real upper-clock relaxation rejects another 149. The remaining
center words identify 480 full-row combinations with the lower prefixes.
Twenty-five fail full-width ordinal edge balance. In each of the other
455 complete 23-row systems, a finite path returns to the same cell and
horizontal copy at the wrong next stable event. No complete word survives.

The lower clock uses integer `N>=max(6, represented event count)` with
`N=6 mod 8` and stable event gaps of at least four. Its cycle-based
implementation is an exact decision procedure for these difference
constraints, with no upper bound on `N`. The upper center clock is relaxed
to real `N>=6`, which only enlarges the candidate set. Every decisive
full-width rejection is a directly traced finite path; a quotient cycle is
never extrapolated into a physical return.

Run `python code/python/verify_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_w22_guard.py` from
the repository root. It regenerates both caps, the count filter and every
center join without a checkpoint, then writes
`results/lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_w22_guard_checks_2026-09-27.json` with ten
complete sample word systems and paths. The separate standard-library
`code/python/check_lower_fat123_a0_b0_111_w22_guard_witnesses.py` replays
those ten paths from ordinal flow. The exhaustive verdict comes from the
first verifier; the sample checker independently checks the format and
physical meaning of its final obstructions.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-lower-fat123-a0-b1-111-upper-guard-families-2026-09-24-md"></a>

## odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b1_111_upper_guard_families_2026-09-24

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_b1_111_upper_guard_families_2026-09-24.md`.

### Upper-guard wake families above lower fat123 `(0,1,111)`

For drift `(1,-3)` and growth eight, normalize the lower transverse level
to zero and let the upper level be the even integer `W`. Assume the lower
fat `(1,2,3)` initial band is `(omega_3,omega_4,j,k,l)=(0,1,1,1,1)`
and the upper cap is a guard. P22, the rank parity and the moment condition
give the six families below. A stronger upper-guard hand lemma leaves
only the even-offset family with `i=0`, at every width and period.

#### Complete arithmetic classification

The six fixed wakes are `{0,2,4,W-2,W-1,W}`. The other two have upper
inward offsets `u<v`, so the full wake set is

```text
O = {0,2,4,W-2,W-1,W,W-u,W-v}.
```

Write `W=6m+r`, where `r` is `0`, `2`, or `4`. Exactly the following
choices pass P22, even rank and moment `3 mod 4`:

| Offsets | Index bounds | `r=0` | `r=2` | `r=4` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `u=6i+4, v=6j+4` | `0<=i<j<=floor((W-9)/6)` | `m+i+j` odd | even | odd |
| `u=6i+1, v=6j+1` | `1<=i<j<=floor((W-6)/6)` | `m+i+j` even | odd | even |

An empty index range contributes no wake set. These are necessary wake
schedules; satisfying them does not construct a highway.

To prove completeness, split transverse levels by residue modulo three.
Among the fixed wakes, two residue fibres already have two even wakes.
The remaining fibre contains one even wake and the odd wake `W-1`.
P22 requires signed wake count `2 mod 4` in each fibre. Both free wakes
must therefore lie in this last fibre and have the same parity. In upper
offset coordinates this is `u,v = 1 mod 3`, giving precisely the two
rows of the table. The bounds say that both free wakes lie between
physical levels five and `W-3`.

The interlacing number is even automatically. Let `I_ab` count pairs
