```text
V -> t:W_1 -> (t-3):WR -> (t-2):NR -> (t+1):E_1          directions S,W,N,E
```

has zero displacement and meets no event of row `t+1` in between, so `E_1` is the visit right
after `V`, with opposite turn. `V` is `ER` or `WL`; an `ER` would be an east arrival before
`E_1`. So `V=WL` and `E_1=ER`. Then `E_1` departs south, and
`W_1 -> (t-3):WR -> (t-2):NR -> E_1 -> t` (directions `W,N,E,S`) is a first return on row
`t`: the event after `W_1` is a south arrival with turn `L`, an `SL`. QED

##### 1.3 The two-west lemma (Lemma M)

*Let `t` be odd with rows `t-3`, `t-2` beginning `WR`, `NR`. Suppose*

* *(H2) some forced first return shows that `N_2` is the event immediately after `W_2`, and*
* *(H3) `N_2` is not `W_3` (this holds vacuously if row `t` has only two west departures).*

*Then no stable word exists on row `t`.*

*Proof.* By (H2) the turns of `W_2` and `N_2` are opposite. If `W_2=SR` then `N_2=NL`; it
departs west and comes right after `W_2`, so it is `W_3`, contrary to (H3). Hence `W_2=NL`
and `N_2=NR`. The `NL` is a north arrival immediately before `N_2`, so `W_2=N_1`. Then `W_1`
precedes `N_1` and is not a north arrival: `W_1=SR`. By the start rule the first event is
`N_1` or `W_1`; `N_1` is an `L`, so the row starts with `W_1=SR`. The square lemma puts an
`SL` in position 1. Position 2 must be an `R`. An `SR` there would be `W_2`, which is an
`NL`; an `NR` there would be a north arrival earlier than the first north arrival `N_1`
(which is at position 2 or later and is an `L`). The row has at least three events, so this
is a contradiction. QED

Lemma M uses row `t+1` only through the square lemma.

##### 1.4 The eight-step return on offset seven

Row three has a single east departure `3:SL`, so row six has a single east arrival `6:E_1`.
If it is an `EL`, the walk

```text
7:W_2 -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL -> 6:E_1=EL -> 7:N_k       WSWNESEN
```

is a first return (offsets `4,3,0,1,4,3,6` in between), where `k` is the rank of `6:EL`
among the north departures of row six. **Hence `W_2` of row seven is immediately followed
by `N_k`, with opposite turn.** The same walk from `7:NL3` is used below.

#### 2. Offset five is non-wake (both caps)

Suppose `omega_5=1`. Row five has one north arrival (from `4:WR`) and no west departure (row
two is empty), so its counts are `NR=1`, `SL=1-omega_5=0`: **row five is `NR`**. Row six has
one east arrival and no south departure, so `(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(0,1,1+omega_6,0)` and **row six is
`WR,EL` or `WR,EL,WR`**. Its `EL` is the second north departure, so the eight-step return
gives (H2) on row seven. Row seven has only two west departures, so (H3) holds. Rows four and
five begin `WR`, `NR`. Lemma M at `t=7` gives a contradiction. **Hence `omega_5=0`, at either
cap.** QED

(The contradiction needs row eight only through the square lemma, which is why the search
frontier `1,1,1,2,4,0` for levels `3..8` dies at level eight.)

#### 3. Offset six is wake

From now on `omega_5=0`, so **row five is `NR,SL`** (`5:NR->E_1(8)`, `5:SL->E_2(8)`). Suppose
`omega_6=0`. Row six has `a=1`, `d=1`; `er=1` would give `WR=-1`, so the counts are
`(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(0,1,2,1)` and row six is

```text
A: WR,EL,WR,WL        or        B: WR,WL,WR,EL.
```

##### 3.1 Order A

The `EL` is the second north departure, so the eight-step return gives (H2) on row seven,
which has two west departures. Lemma M at `t=7` excludes A.

##### 3.2 Order B forces row seven

Now `6:EL3` is the third north departure, so `W_2` of row seven is immediately followed by
`N_3`, with opposite turn. If `W_2=SR`, then `N_3=NL` would be a third west departure; row
seven has two. So `W_2=NL`, `N_3=NR`, and `W_2=N_2`. If `W_1` were an `NL` it would be `N_1`,
against the start rule; so `W_1=SR`. If `N_1` were an `NL`, it would be a west departure
before `W_2=N_2`, hence equal to `W_1`, which is an `SR`; so `N_1=NR`. Row seven has three north arrivals (from
`6:WR0,WR2,EL3`) and two west departures, so it contains one `SR`, one `NL`, two `NR`, and
`2-omega_7` further `SL` (from the excess). The square lemma (rows four and five begin `WR`,
`NR`) makes `SR` immediately followed by `SL`.

Place the alternating word, which has at most six events. The pair `NL,N_3` occupies an odd
and the next even position, so it is at `1,2` or `3,4`. At `1,2`, position 0 would have to be
both `N_1` and `W_1`. So `NL,N_3` are at `3,4`; positions `0,2` hold `W_1` and `N_1`, and `W_1` must be at 0 because its `SL`
follows it. Therefore

```text
7: SR0, SL1, NR2, NL3, NR4, [SL5]          (SL5 present iff omega_7=0),
```

with `6:WR0->7:NR2`, `6:WR2->7:NL3`, `6:EL3->7:NR4`, `7:SR0->4:WR`, `7:NL3->4:WL`, and east
departures `SL1,NR2,NR4,[SL5]` feeding `E_1,E_2,E_3,[E_4]` of row ten. The eight-step return
from `7:NL3` lands on `7:NR4`.

##### 3.3 Row eight is P or Q

By the square lemma at `t=7`, the south departure `D_1` of row eight (it feeds `7:SR0=S_1`)
is a `WL`, immediately followed by `E_1=ER`; so `E_1=D_2`. Row eight has two east arrivals
and `3-omega_7` south departures, so

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL) = (er, 2-er, omega_8+5-omega_7-3er, 3-omega_7-er),     er>=1, WL>=1.
```

By the start rule row eight begins with a `WR` (an `ER` there would be `E_1=D_1`). An event
before `D_1` is not a south departure, and not an east arrival (it would precede `E_1`), hence
a `WR`; alternation allows only one: row eight begins `WR0,WL1,ER2`. If `er=2`, then `WL>=1` forces `omega_7=0`
and `WR=omega_8-1`, so `WR<=0`, contradicting `8:WR0`. Hence `er=1` and the remaining events
are `EL` (`=E_2`), `1-omega_7` more `WL` and `omega_8+1-omega_7` more `WR`, alternating:

```text
omega_7=1:   8: WR0, WL1, ER2, EL3, [WR4]                       (type P)
omega_7=0:   P: WR0, WL1, ER2, EL3, WR4, WL5, [WR6]
             Q: WR0, WL1, ER2, WL3, WR4, EL5, [WR6]              (final WR iff omega_8=1).
```

In both types `8:WL1->7:SR0`, `8:ER2->7:SL1`, `5:NR->8:ER2`, `5:SL->8:E_2`.

**Three walks out of row nine.** Row nine has exactly three west departures (row six has three
west arrivals `WR0,WL1,WR2`). By ordinal matching, in both types:

```text
(alpha)  9:W_1 -> 6:WR0 -> 7:NR2 -> 10:E_2                                        W N E
(beta)   9:W_2 -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SL -> 8:E_2 (an EL) -> 9:N_k                           W S E N
(gamma)  9:W_3 -> 6:WR2 -> 7:NL3 -> 4:WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL
               -> 6:EL3 -> 7:NR4 -> 10:E_3                                        W N W S W N E S E N E
```

where `k` is the rank of `8:E_2` among the north departures of row eight (`k=2` in P, where
`8:E_2=EL3`; `k=3` in Q, where `8:E_2=EL5`). If `10:E_2` (resp. `10:E_3`) is an `ER`, the walk
`alpha` (resp. `gamma`) continues south to row nine and is a zero-displacement first return of
4 (resp. 12) steps. Walk `beta` is a first return of 4 steps.

##### 3.4 Type P is impossible (both caps)

* **P1.** By `beta` with `k=2`, `W_2` is immediately followed by `N_2`, with opposite turn.
* **P2.** If `W_2=NL`, then `N_2=NR` and `W_2=N_1`. Then
  `8:WR0 -> 9:N_1=W_2 -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SL -> 8:EL3` (`N W S E`) is a first return on row eight,
  so `WR0` would be followed by `EL3`; it is followed by `WL1`. Hence `W_2=SR`, `N_2=NL`, and
  `N_2` is the next west departure: `N_2=W_3`.
* **P3.** `10:E_3=EL`: otherwise `gamma` returns to row nine right after `W_3=NL` at a south
  arrival with turn `R`, an `SR`, which would be a fourth west departure.
* **P4.** Let `J` be the south departure of row ten feeding `W_2=SR`. The walk
  `J -> 9:W_2 -(beta)-> 9:N_2=W_3 -(gamma)-> 10:E_3` has 16 steps
  `SWSENWNWSWNESENE`, zero displacement, and no intermediate visit to offset ten. So `E_3` is
  the event right after `J`, with opposite turn: `J=ER`, an east arrival immediately before
  `E_3`, i.e. `J=E_2`.
* **P5.** Now `alpha` returns: `9:W_1 -> 6:WR0 -> 7:NR2 -> 10:E_2=J -> 9:W_2`. So `W_2`
  immediately follows `W_1` with opposite turn: `W_1=NL`. It is a north arrival before
  `W_2<N_2`, hence `W_1=N_1`.
* **P6.** Then `8:WR0 -> 9:N_1=W_1 -(alpha)-> 10:E_2 -> 9:W_2 -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SL -> 8:EL3`
  (`NWNESWSE`, offsets `9,6,7,10,9,6,5` in between) is a first return on row eight landing on
  `EL3`, whereas `8:WR0` is followed by `WL1`. Contradiction.

No clock and no count beyond "three west departures on row nine" was used, and no parity of
offsets `7..10` was assumed. **Type P is impossible at both caps**; this explains why the lower
frontier dies at level ten.

(This re-proves, more briefly and with no parity assumption on offsets `7..10`, the
September 1 lower-cap results of `odd_skew_excursions_and_domino_transfer_2026-08-31.md`,
whose level-nine and level-ten arguments used the 12- and 16-step walks above for particular
count rows.)

##### 3.5 Type Q at the lower cap

Type Q needs `omega_7=0`, so `7:SL5` exists and `8:WL3=D_3` feeds it. Put `c=tau(6:WL1)` and
`b=tau(6:WR2)`, so `c<b`. With `sigma=-1`:

```text
tau(8:EL5) = c + 2 - N                       (6:WL1 -> 5:SL -> 8:EL5, two steps, voltage +1)
tau(7:NL3) = b + 1,   tau(7:NR4) = b + 9      (6:WR2 -> 7:NL3; eight-step return)
tau(8:WL3) = tau(7:SL5) - 1 > tau(7:NR4) - 1 = b + 8.
```

Hence `tau(8:EL5) < b+2-N < tau(8:WL3)`, while Q lists `WL3` before `EL5`. **Type Q is
impossible at the lower cap.** (This is the inequality of the September 1 note.)

This completes the lower cap: **a lower missing cap has `omega_6=1`.**

##### 3.6 Type Q at the upper cap

At the upper cap (`sigma=+1`) the inequality of 3.5 is void, and Q must be followed further.
In Q the north departures of row eight are `WR0,WR4,EL5,[WR6]`, so `beta` has `k=3`:

* **Q0.** `W_2` of row nine is immediately followed by `N_3`, with opposite turn.

**Clock fact (upper cap only).** `tau(9:N_1) < tau(9:W_1)`. Put `p=tau(4:WR)`, `q=tau(6:WR0)`.

```text
4:WR -> 5:NR -> 8:ER2 -> 7:SL1          tau(7:SL1) = p + 3 + N
7:SR0 -> 4:WR,  8:WL1 -> 7:SR0          tau(8:WL1) = p - 2 + N
8:WR0 before 8:WL1, 8:WR0 -> 9:N_1      tau(9:N_1) < p - 1 + N
6:WR0 -> 7:NR2 after 7:SL1              q + 1 > p + 3 + N
9:W_1 -> 6:WR0 (a west step)            tau(9:W_1) = q - 1 + N > p + 1 + 2N.
```

So `tau(9:W_1)-tau(9:N_1) > N+2 > 0`. Consequently `N_1` is not a west departure:
**`N_1=NR`**, and it precedes every west departure.

**Case II: `W_2=SR`.** By Q0, `N_3=NL`, the next west departure: `N_3=W_3`. Steps P3, P4 and P5
apply verbatim, with `beta` now ending at `N_3=W_3` (the 16-step walk is
`J -> 9:W_2 -(beta)-> 9:N_3=W_3 -(gamma)-> 10:E_3`): `10:E_3=EL`, the feeder `J` of `W_2` is
`E_2=ER`, and `W_1=NL` is immediately followed by `W_2`. The north arrival `W_1` precedes `N_3` and is not
`N_1` (clock fact), so `W_1=N_2`.

* *Row nine begins `NR, NL, SR, NL`.* By the start rule the first event is `N_1=NR` (`W_1` is
  an `NL`). Between `N_1` and `W_1=N_2` lie no north arrivals and no west departures, hence at
  most `SL`s; an `SL` after `N_1` would need an `R` next, and none is available before `W_1`.
  So `N_1,W_1,W_2,W_3` are positions `0,1,2,3`.
* *Row ten begins `WR, EL, ER, EL`.* `W_2` is the first south arrival of row nine, so
  `J=E_2=D_1`. Then `E_1` precedes `D_1`, so it is not an `ER`: `E_1=EL`. The first event is a
  `WR` (an `ER` would be `E_1`). Events before `E_1` are west arrivals that are not south
  departures, so exactly one `WR` precedes `E_1`. After `E_1` comes an `R`; a `WR` would need
  an `L` next, which would be an east arrival (`E_2`, an `ER`) or a south departure before
  `D_1`. So positions `2,3` are `E_2=ER`, `E_3=EL`. The north departures of row ten begin
  `WR0, EL1`, and `10:EL1=E_1` is fed by `7:SL1`.

**Case I: `W_2=NL`.** By Q0, `N_3=NR` and `W_2=N_2`. If `W_1` were an `NL` it would be `N_1`,
against the clock fact; so `W_1=SR`.

* *Row nine begins `NR, SL, SR, NL, NR`.* By the clock fact the row starts with `N_1=NR`.
  No `R` lies strictly between `N_1` and `W_2`, except `W_1`: an `NR` there would be a north
  arrival between `N_1` and `N_2=W_2`, and an `SR` there would be a west departure before
  `W_2`, hence `W_1`. By alternation exactly one `L` lies between `N_1` and `W_1`; it is not a
  north arrival, so it is an `SL`. Nothing lies between `W_1` (an `R`) and `W_2` (an `L`):
  the events there contain no `R`, so they would be a single `L` followed by the `L` `W_2`. So
  `N_1, SL, W_1, W_2, N_3` are at positions `0..4`.
* *Row ten begins `WR, WL, ER, EL`.* If `10:E_2` were an `ER`, walk `alpha` would return to
  row nine right after `W_1=9:SR2` at a south arrival, but `9:NL3` follows. So `E_2=EL`. Let
  `K` be the south departure feeding `9:SR2`, the second south arrival: `K=D_2`. The walk
  `K -> 9:SR2 -> 6:WR0 -> 7:NR2 -> 10:E_2` (`S W N E`) is a first return, so `K` is an `R`
  immediately before `E_2`: `K=ER=E_1`. `D_1` precedes `E_1` and is not an east arrival:
  `D_1=WL`. The first event is a `WR` (an `ER` would be `E_1=D_1`, but `E_1=D_2`); before
  `E_1` the only south departure is `D_1`; alternation forces `WR, WL, ER=E_1, EL=E_2`. The
  north departures of row ten begin `WR0, EL3`; `10:EL3=E_2` is fed by `7:NR2`, and
  `10:ER2=D_2` feeds `9:SR2=W_1`.

**Lemma M at `t=11` (both cases).** Rows eight and nine begin with `WR` and `NR`. Row eleven
has west departures feeding `8:WR0, WL1, WL3, WR4, [WR6]`; in particular `W_3(11)->8:WL3->7:SL5`
(`8:WL3=D_3` feeds `S_3(7)=SL5`).

* (H2), case II:
  `11:W_2 -> 8:WL1 -> 7:SR0 -> 4:WR -> 5:NR -> 8:ER2 -> 7:SL1 -> 10:EL1 -> 11:N_2`
  (`WSWNESEN`).
* (H2), case I:
  `11:W_2 -> 8:WL1 -> 7:SR0 -> 4:WR -> 5:NR -> 8:ER2 -> 7:SL1 -> 10:ER2 -> 9:SR2 -> 6:WR0
  -> 7:NR2 -> 10:EL3 -> 11:N_2` (`WSWNESESWNEN`).

  Both walks have zero displacement and no intermediate visit to offset eleven.
* (H3), case II: if `N_2(11)=W_3(11)`, then `7:SL1 -> 10:EL1 -> 11:W_3 -> 8:WL3 -> 7:SL5`
  (`E N W S`) is a first return on row seven, so `SL1` would be followed by `SL5`; it is
  followed by `NR2`.
* (H3), case I: if `N_2(11)=W_3(11)`, then `7:NR2 -> 10:EL3 -> 11:W_3 -> 8:WL3 -> 7:SL5` makes
  `NR2` followed by `SL5`; it is followed by `NL3`.

Lemma M gives a contradiction. **Type Q is impossible at the upper cap**, and therefore **an
upper missing cap has `omega_6=1`.** QED

The self-similarity is worth recording: in upper type Q, rows `8..11` play the part that rows
`4..7` play in the proof of `omega_5=0`, and the same Lemma M finishes both. The search
frontier `...,23,21,88,0` dies at level twelve because Lemma M needs row twelve only through
the square lemma; every level-eleven survivor of that search begins `SR,NL,NR`, the shape
forced in the proof of Lemma M just before the square lemma is applied.

#### 4. Summary of what is used where

| Step | Returns | Counts | Clock |
|---|---|---|---|
| `omega_5=0` (both caps) | square, eight-step | rows 5, 6 | none |
| order A | square, eight-step | row 6 | none |
| rows 7, 8 | square, eight-step | rows 7, 8 | none |
| type P (both caps) | `alpha`, `beta`, `gamma`, 16-step, 8-step | "three west departures on row 9" | none |
| type Q, lower | eight-step | none | `tau(8:EL5)<tau(8:WL3)`, `sigma=-1` |
| type Q, upper | `alpha`,`beta`,`gamma`, 16-step, Lemma M at 11 | "three west departures on row 9" | `tau(9:N_1)<tau(9:W_1)`, `sigma=+1` |

Only `N>0` and strict order within rows are used from the clock.

#### 5. Finite claims checked by computer

The checkers are in `code/python/` and need only Python:

* `python verify_missing_cap_hand_walks.py` follows every displayed walk by ordinal matching on
  the displayed (partial) rows. It checks each walk's direction word, its endpoint (heading,
  ordinal and, when known, position), its zero displacement where a return is claimed, and
  the step counts and net voltages behind the clock identities of 3.5 and 3.6. All checks pass.
* `python verify_missing_cap_hand_words.py` enumerates all alternating words with the stated
  counts. It confirms the word lists of sections 2 and 3.1-3.3 (rows six, seven, eight). In
  upper type Q, it also confirms two things. Every row-nine word satisfying the clock fact and
  Q0 begins `NR,SL,SR,NL,NR` (case I) or, after the P5 adjacency, `NR,NL,SR,NL` (case II).
  Every row-ten word satisfying the stated conditions begins `WR,WL,ER,EL` (case I) or
  `WR,EL,ER,EL` (case II).
* `python verify_missing_cap_lemma_m.py` confirms the combinatorial Lemma M by brute force for all
  odd-row counts with `2<=b<=7`, `2<=c<=6` (65,763 words; none satisfies the hypotheses). This is
  redundant with the hand proof.
* The earlier search frontiers (`verify_missing_cap_offset6_lemma.py`) only corroborate the
  proof; nothing above rests on them.

#### 6. Consequences

* **Missing-cap wake pattern through offset six**, at both caps and every period: offsets
  `0,1,4,6` are wake and `2,3,5` are non-wake.
* The offset-six lemma (`odd_skew_missing_cap_offset6_2026-09-23.md`) now has a hand proof, and
  with it the impossibility of family M-II and the second proof that the lower band
  `(0,1,1,1,1)` has no upper missing cap.
* At the upper cap, `omega_5=0` is new in this generality. It contains the September 3 return
  lemma (offset five non-wake when six and seven are) and the rule used to remove `q=m-1` in
  guard/missing family I.
* The quiet upper-missing lemma (`odd_skew_upper_missing_quiet_band_2026-09-23.md`) is implied.
* Nothing here closes a cap pair by itself; the `(1,-3)` orbit remains open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-missing-guard-exclusion-2026-09-03-md"></a>

## odd_skew_missing_guard_exclusion_2026-09-03

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_missing_guard_exclusion_2026-09-03.md`.

### The lower-missing / upper-guard cap pair is impossible

Later result (9 September 2026): [the complete guard/missing exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-guard-missing-complete-exclusion-2026-09-09-md) closes both lower-guard / upper-missing families. The remaining-family statements below describe the earlier stage; the full drift orbit remains open.

Consider a growth-eight periodic trace with drift `(1,-3)`.  A missing cap has
outer multiplicities `(1,1,0)`; a guard cap has `(1,1,1)`.  The lower-missing /
upper-guard pair is impossible at every period.  The proof combines an exact
P3 return argument with the P22 and odd-skew rank conditions.  It uses neither
an additive moment condition nor a computer search.

Normalize the transverse levels to `0<=t<=W`.  Both extreme arrivals are
horizontal, so `W` is even.  Since the primitive drift has one translation
class per level, eight wake classes require `W>=8`.  Write `O` for the set of
wake levels.

#### The local input

At a lower missing cap, the four-step cap calculation gives wake levels
`0,1,4`, with level two empty.  The strengthened same-cell return argument in
`odd_skew_excursions_and_domino_transfer_2026-08-31.md` proves

```text
omega_6+omega_7 >= 1.                                  (1)
```

For clarity, this argument does not assume an upper guard.  If levels five
through seven are even, the exact return paths force level nine to be wake,
regardless of level eight's parity.  P22 forbids that wake at growth eight.
If level five is wake while six and seven are even, a shorter four-step
same-cell return gives a contradiction.  These exhaust the two possibilities
for level five and prove (1).

#### Residues and the rank sign

Let `O_j={t in O:t=j mod 3}`.  Up to one common sign, the checker sign at level
`t` is `(-1)^t`.  P22 says that the signed sum in every fibre is `2 mod 4`.
Each fibre therefore contains an even number of wake classes, at least two.

The wakes at levels one and four have opposite signs in the same fibre.
That fibre needs at least two more wakes.  Since there are eight in total,

```text
|O_0|=2,       |O_1|=4,       |O_2|=2.                 (2)
```

The two wakes in either two-class fibre have the same parity.  The odd-skew
rank theorem also gives

```text
I = sum_(i<j) #{(u,v) in O_i x O_j : u<v} = 0 mod 2.  (3)
```

The usual normalization labels these fibres by `(t+1) mod 3`.  Using `t mod 3`
in (3) is harmless: a cyclic relabelling reverses comparisons only across a
whole fibre, and every product of two fibre cardinalities is even.

#### An upper guard forbids both levels six and seven

An upper guard contributes singleton wakes `W-2,W-1,W`.

If `W=1 mod 3`, the two-class fibre `O_0` contains level zero and level `W-1`.
Their parities are opposite, contrary to P22.

Suppose `W=6m`.  The entire fibre `O_0` is `{0,W}`, so level six is not wake.
The four-class fibre has the form

```text
O_1={1,4,a,W-2}.
```

Its extra level `a` must be even: levels one and four cancel, and `W-2` is
even.  Thus level seven is not wake either.

It remains to take `W=6m+2`.  At width eight the fibre containing levels one
and four has only the available levels `1,4,7`, too few for (2).  For larger
widths the residue sets must be

```text
O_0={0,W-2},
O_1={1,4,a,W-1},       a=1 mod 6,
O_2={b,W},             b=2 mod 6.
```

Here `a>=7`, while the empty level two gives `b>=8`; both extra levels lie
below the upper guard.  Directly counting the ordered cross-fibre pairs gives

```text
I_01=1,       I_02=1,       I_12=[a<b]       mod 2.
```

For example, the unreduced first two counts are five and three.  The last is
`6+[a<b]`.  Equation (3) therefore requires `b<a`, excluding `a=7`.
Also `O_0={0,W-2}` excludes level six once width eight has been removed.

Thus every possible width of a lower-missing / upper-guard trace makes both
levels six and seven non-wake.  This contradicts (1) and excludes the pair.

#### The other homogeneous cap pairs

The same residue/rank calculation gives hand proofs of the two exclusions
previously recorded by the all-width invariant sieve.

For two missing caps, each opposite-sign wake pair at offsets one and four
requires a four-class fibre.  If the two pairs lie in different fibres, they
require at least `4+4+2=10` wake classes.  If they lie in the same fibre and
`W>8`, their four distinct signs cancel, rather than summing to `2 mod 4`.
At `W=8` the shared fibre has only the levels `1,4,7`, so it cannot contain
four wake classes.  Two missing caps are therefore impossible using P22
alone.

For two guard caps, six distinct wakes are fixed at

```text
0,1,2,W-2,W-1,W.
```

At widths `0` or `4 mod 6`, two fibres contain opposite-sign fixed pairs;
each needs two additional wakes, exceeding the remaining budget of two.
At width `2 mod 6`, all fixed pairs already have the required signed sum.
The two extra wakes must then lie in one fibre with opposite signs.
They make an even contribution to (3): both are between the fixed caps and
have the same residue, so their cross terms with the fixed wakes cancel.
The residue word of the six fixed wakes is `0,1,2,0,1,2`, whose rank sum is
nine, hence odd.  This contradicts (3).

Consequently, among caps restricted to missing or guard, the only orientation
not excluded is

```text
lower guard / upper missing.
```

This is not an exclusion of the whole `(1,-3)` orbit.  Caps with profiles
`(1,2,2)` or `(1,2,3)` remain outside this argument.  The surviving orientation
also has the static witness

```text
W=18,       O={0,1,2,8,13,14,17,18}.
```

Its signed fibre sums are `-2,2,-2`, its rank sum is even, and its normalized
additive moment is `-5=3 mod 4`.  These static checks do not construct a
periodic trace.  A further exact return lemma now rules out this placement:
upper offset five is wake while offsets six and seven are non-wake.

The surviving guard/missing wake placements are reduced to two parameter
families in `odd_skew_guard_missing_families_2026-09-03.md`.  That proof gives
width at least 20, and at least 30 when the width is divisible by six.  It
does not exclude the families at arbitrary width.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-prefix-reductions-2026-09-22-md"></a>

## odd_skew_prefix_reductions_2026-09-22

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_prefix_reductions_2026-09-22.md`.

### Further prefix reductions for drift `(1,-3)`

22 September 2026

The stable-word and lifted-time conditions exclude the following branches at every period:

| Cap and initial band | Additional condition | First level with no continuation |
|---|---|---:|
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=0`, `(j,k,l)=(1,0,2)` | Both values of `omega_4`; all later wake choices | 9 |
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=0`, `(j,k,l)=(2,0,1)` | Both values of `omega_4`; all wake choices at levels 5 and 6 | 6 |
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=1`, `(j,k,l)=(2,0,0)` | Both values of `omega_4`; all later wake choices | 6 |
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=1`, `omega_4=0`, `(j,k,l)=(1,0,1)` | P22 forces `omega_5=0`; both values of `omega_6,omega_7` | 7 |
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=1`, `omega_4=0`, `(j,k,l)=(1,1,0)` | P22 forces `omega_5=0` and then `omega_9=0`; all other early wake choices | 10 |
| Lower `(1,2,3)`, `omega_3=1`, `omega_4=1`, `(j,k,l)=(1,0,1)` | P22 forces `omega_5=omega_7=0`, and `omega_9=0` if `omega_6=1`; all other early wake choices | 9 |
| Upper `(1,2,3)`, non-wake offsets 3 and 4, `(j,k,l)=(2,1,0)` | Offset 6 non-wake; offsets 5, 7 and 8 non-wake | 8 |

The first six rows exclude nine of the twelve initial lower `(1,2,3)` bands beyond the remaining lower `(1,2,2)` Family B. The upper result restricts Family B but does not exclude it. None of these results closes the whole `(1,-3)` drift orbit.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(1,0,2)`

Both `omega_4` values now have an empty level-nine frontier after exhaustive count rows, alternating stable words, witnessed finite first returns, and exact integer lifted clocks. No P22 wake pruning, period upper bound, width bound, or row-length bound is imposed. The level-eight survivor counts are 452 and 3,112; at level nine every word satisfying necessary boundary adjacency has a witnessed wrong return, leaving zero. Empty rows are included, so the check also covers widths below nine. The separate audited note `odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion_2026-09-22.md`, checker `verify_lower_fat123_a0_102_exclusion.py`, and result `lower_fat123_a0_102_checks_2026-09-22.json` give the exact counts and scope.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(2,0,1)`

For `omega_3=0`, both `omega_4` values have a forced six-step contradiction at level six. Eight distinct wake levels require a transverse width at least seven, so this level exists in every full candidate. The raw-word checker `verify_lower_fat123_a0_j2_exclusion.py` explores both wake values at levels five and six. It finds eight physical-return-compatible level-five words for each `omega_4`; their level-six extensions number 49 and 98 after physical returns. In every extension, ordinal matching forces the level and port path

```text
1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL2 -> 6:ER -> 5:SR -> 2:WL -> 1:SL.
```

The precise level-five and level-six event ordinals vary, but every path has directions `E,S,E,S,W,S`, voltage `+1`, and six steps. Thus `tau(1:SL)-tau(1:NR)=6-N<=0` for `N>=6`, contradicting the stable same-cell gap of at least four. No P22 pruning or period cutoff is used. The separate note `odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_j2_exclusion_2026-09-22.md` gives the raw counts and scope.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(2,0,0)`

The first five rows, in the lower-cap coordinates of `odd_skew_lower_fat_cap_normal_form_2026-09-12.md`, are

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL,WR
3: NR,SL,SR,SL,NR
4: WR,WL,WR,WL,ER,EL,[WR]^omega_4.
```

This is the sole `(j,k,l)=(2,0,0)` band with `omega_3=1`. Both `omega_4` values are checked. The flow equations and alternating right/left order produce 84 level-five words when `omega_4=0`, and 172 when `omega_4=1`, across both possible level-five wake indicators. Exact same-cell first returns reduce these to four and eight words. Lifted clocks leave two and four words, respectively. No period upper bound is imposed.

All retained level-five words have the prefix

```text
NR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL
or
SR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL.
```

For `omega_4=1`, each has a final `NR` or `NR,SL` tail. At level six, the return condition leaves ten words for `omega_4=0` and twenty for `omega_4=1`, across both wake choices. Every one contains the same six-step path, with ordinal matching along each edge:

```text
1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:SL2 -> 6:ER2 -> 5:SR2 -> 2:WL -> 1:SL.
```

Its directions are `E,S,E,S,W,S`, giving displacement `(1,-3)`. In the lower-cap lifted clock, the two row-one visits therefore satisfy

```text
tau(1:SL)-tau(1:NR)=6-N.
```

The left side is at least four because these are successive stable visits to one representative cell. A period has `N>=6`; hence the right side is at most zero. This contradiction disposes of every level-six candidate. `verify_lower_fat123_a1_j2_exclusion.py` enumerates the words, checks each first-return order, asserts the same forced path in all thirty final candidates, and checks the integer clock equations independently.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(1,0,1)` with non-wake level four

Here rows three and four are `SR,SL,NR,SL,NR` and `WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL`. The already proved P22 consequence for `omega_3=1` makes every level `5 mod 6` non-wake, so `omega_5=0`. The generic stable-word adjacency enumerator leaves two words on level five. Both wake choices at level six leave six prefixes. At level seven, both wake choices give forty-five count rows, twelve words satisfying the one-excursion adjacency test, and zero words satisfying all physical first-return requirements.

`verify_lower_fat123_a1_b0_101_exclusion.py` reproduces the complete finite prefix calculation. The third step uses no clock infeasibility and no restriction on `omega_6` or `omega_7` beyond their binary wake values. This is a computer-checked local exclusion; a short hand derivation of the twelve rejected level-seven words has not been written.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(1,1,0)` with non-wake level four

Here rows three and four are `SR,SL,NR,SL,NR` and `WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL`. P22 forces `omega_5=0`. Exact prefix enumeration leaves four, five, four, fourteen and six states after levels five through nine, then none after level ten. Every level-six survivor has `omega_6=1`. Since wakes `0,3,6` already occupy the four-wake residue-zero fibre with signs `+,-,+`, level nine cannot be wake; otherwise that fibre would have a forbidden `2+2` checker split. The fourth wake in it must occur at an even level at least twelve, so the level-ten row is present in any full candidate.

The standalone checker `verify_lower_fat123_a1_b0_110_exclusion.py` independently implements row count equations, word generation, ordinal matching, physical returns, P22 prefix restrictions and lifted clocks. It checks all 17,680 level-ten alternating words. The separate note `odd_skew_lower_fat123_b0_110_exclusion_2026-09-22.md` and result `lower_fat123_a1_b0_110_checks_2026-09-22.json` give the complete counts and exact scope. This is an all-period computer-checked prefix exclusion, not a hand classification of the deeper orbit.

#### Lower `(1,2,3)` band `(1,0,1)` with wake level four

Here rows three and four are `SR,SL,NR,SL,NR` and `WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,WR`. The wakes at levels zero and three occupy opposite checker signs of one residue-three fibre. With only eight total wakes, that fibre must have a `3+1` split and the two other fibres must each have a matching-sign pair. Hence P22 forces `omega_5=omega_7=0`. It also rules out simultaneous wakes at levels six and nine, which would make the residue-zero fibre `2+2`. The fourth wake of the residue-zero fibre cannot occur earlier than level twelve: after wakes zero and three, the earliest same-parity pair is six and twelve, or nine and fifteen. Therefore every full candidate has width at least twelve, so level nine is present.

The standalone `verify_lower_fat123_a1_b1_101_exclusion.py` derives every row count from directional flow, enumerates alternating words subject only to necessary first-return adjacency, then checks explicit physical first returns. It leaves respectively 2, 6, 6 and 88 prefixes after levels five through eight. At level nine, all 88 boundary-adjacent words have a witnessed wrong return: level-seven event zero returns to event three of the same cell, skipping events one and two. No lifted-clock, period, width, or row-length cutoff is used. The detailed counts are in `lower_fat123_a1_b1_101_checks_2026-09-22.json`.

#### Remaining lower `(1,2,3)` bands

The three initial bands not excluded here are `(j,k,l)=(1,1,1)` with `omega_3=0` and either `omega_4`, and `(1,1,0)` with `omega_3=omega_4=1`. The last of these has 104 necessary-condition prefixes after an exact level-nine clock check. At level ten, a forced `ER` source/arrival clock equation excludes 4,894 of 6,044 possible pairs, but an explicit prefix still passes full local return and clock tests; see `odd_skew_lower_fat123_a1_b1_110_level10_restriction_2026-09-22.md`. For `(1,1,1)`, the `omega_4=0` branch retains 394 prefixes through level nine; the `omega_4=1` branch retains 675 through level eight without P22 pruning. See `odd_skew_lower_fat123_a0_111_open_prefix_2026-09-22.md`. These prefixes are not periodic ant traces.

#### Upper `(1,2,3)` band `(2,1,0)` with non-wake level six

In upper inward coordinates with headings rotated by 180 degrees, the initial rows are

```text
0: WR
1: NR,SL
2: WR,WL,WR
3: NR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL
4: WR,WL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL.
```

Assume inward levels five through eight are non-wake. The generic stable-word adjacency enumeration and exact upper-sign lifted clocks leave respectively 9, 36 and 23 prefixes after levels five, six and seven. The 23 level-seven prefixes induce 130 count rows at level eight. None has a stable word satisfying the forced same-cell return adjacency. `verify_upper_fat123_210_w6zero.py` reproduces these counts using the generic enumerator, separately from the earlier fast unary frontier script. It uses `N>=max(6, number of represented events)`, a necessary bound because all represented quotient events occupy distinct phases of the period. No upper bound on `N`, row size, or black lifetime is used.

Family B has width `W=6m+4` and wake set

```text
{0,5,6,8,6p+2,6r+4,W-2,W},
2<=p<r<=m-1,   p+r odd.
```

It has non-wake upper offsets five, seven and eight. Its upper offset six is wake exactly when `r=m-1`. Consequently the surviving `(2,1,0)` initial upper band of Family B requires `r=m-1` and `p+m` even. This leaves the short upper band, the `(1,1,1)` and `(1,2,0)` bands, and the `(2,1,0)` band with a wake at offset six unresolved.

For the wake-six `(2,1,0)` branch, exact integer-clock enumeration leaves 6,574 locally consistent prefixes through inward row ten, all with one of two paired row-five/six words. The other three upper initial bands also have locally consistent prefixes through row eight for both wake-six values. See `upper210_wake6_prefix_restriction_2026-09-22.md`; those survivors do not establish a periodic ant.

Separately, Family B has a unique lower level-nine word `(NR,SL)^4` for either surviving level-eight word. This follows from its non-wake level nine and directional flow; `family_b_level9_necessary_word_2026-09-22.md` records the argument. At non-wake level ten, 32 alternating words are count-compatible in each branch; exact lower lifted clocks leave eight after row-eight word A and seventeen after word B. See `family_b_lower_level10_restriction_2026-09-22.md`. These local survivors do not exclude Family B.

#### Verification boundary

These checks enumerate all stable words permitted by the stated local count equations; they do not assume a period upper bound. The adjacency and physical-return filters are necessary conditions from the stable-word framework. The lower `(2,0,1)` and `(2,0,0)` contradictions have explicit drift paths after finite word reduction. The lower `(1,0,2)` check for both wake-four values, the `(1,1,0)` non-wake-four check, and the `(1,0,1)` wake-four check also have standalone enumeration implementations. The exclusions have not been formalized in Lean. An empty prefix proves those stated branches impossible; a surviving prefix in any other branch is not an ant trace.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-n-first-east-reduction-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_n_first_east_reduction_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_n_first_east_reduction_2026-09-15.md`.

### Four words on ten for the remaining N continuation

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: N is excluded uniformly in
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
All four words remaining at the end of this reduction
are therefore closed.

In form R, continuation N, the first east arrival on ten
precedes its third south departure by at least sixteen
steps in lifted time. This initially leaves five words on
ten. The left-turn word L0 is then impossible, leaving
four. In L1, the second west departure on eleven must
turn left; its initial word and the first three events
on twelve are forced.

These restrictions do not exclude N. Forms P and Q,
family B, and the other cap profiles of the `(1,-3)`
orbit remain open.

#### Notation

Use the words established in
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_middle_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
In particular,

```text
7: SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL;
8: a=WR,C=WL,E1=ER,I=WL,b=WR,E2=EL,c=WR,E3=EL,
   d=WR,E4=EL,e=WR,J=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL;
9: NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL.
```

Let `F1,F2,F3` be the east arrivals on ten, and let
`S1,...,S9` be its south departures. The previous note
fixes its final five events as

```text
T = Y=WL, F2=ER, K=WL, X=WR, F3=EL,
```

where `Y=S7`, `F2=S8`, and `K=S9`. All words in this
note are in stable order. Powers of a two-letter block
mean concatenation; an exponent of zero gives an empty
block.

#### A gap identity before S3

Write `A` for the first `ER` on six. There is a fixed
eight-step path

```text
8:I=WL -> 7:second SR -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR
       -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:first SL -> 6:A=ER.
```

Its direction word is `S W S W N E S E`. It has zero
voltage, so

```text
tau(A) = tau(I) + 8.
```

The following two paths have the same length and voltage:

```text
8:E1=ER -> 7:first SL -> 10:F1;
6:A=ER  -> 5:first SL -> 8:E2=EL.
```

Both have directions `S E`. Cancelling their common
lifted-time increment gives

```text
tau(E2) - tau(F1) = tau(I) - tau(E1) + 8.             (1)
```

On eight, `E1` immediately precedes `I`. Their gap is
at least four, so `E2` is at least twelve steps later
than `F1`.

The third north arrival on nine is supplied by `E2=EL`.
It immediately precedes the third south arrival, `SL`,
which is supplied by `S3` on ten. The two incoming
vertical steps have zero voltage. Thus

```text
tau(S3) - tau(E2)
  = tau(9:third SL) - tau(9:third NR) >= 4.
```

Together with (1), this proves

```text
tau(S3) - tau(F1)
  = [tau(9:third SL) - tau(9:third NR)]
    + [tau(I) - tau(E1)] + 8
  >= 16.                                           (2)
```

In particular, `F1<S3`. No upper bound on a black or
white lifetime is used. The period term cancels in (1).

#### Five preliminary words

If `F1=ER`, it is one of the south departures and must
therefore be `S1` or `S2`. With `F2=ER,F3=EL`, the
non-wake condition gives seven `WL` and six `WR` ports
on ten. Alternation and the fixed tail give exactly these
two possibilities:

```text
R1: F1=ER, (WL,WR)^5, T;
R2: WR,WL, F1=ER, (WL,WR)^4, T.
```

If `F1=EL`, every south departure preceding it is a
`WL`. By (2), their number `k` is zero, one, or two.
There are now eight `WL` and nine `WR` ports on ten.
The three possible words are

```text
L0: WR, F1=EL, (WR,WL)^6, WR, T;
L1: WR,WL,WR, F1=EL, (WR,WL)^5, WR, T;
L2: (WR,WL)^2, WR, F1=EL, (WR,WL)^4, WR, T.
```

Before (2), alternation alone allowed `F1=ER` at any
of `S1,...,S6`, or `F1=EL` after `k=0,...,6` south
departures. The gap identity excludes eight of those
thirteen words. This is a reduction of words within N,
not a count of closed or remaining drift orbits.

#### The second west departure on eleven

Let `P1,P2,P3` be the first three west departures on
eleven, feeding `8:a,C,I` respectively. In each left-turn
case `Lk`, the path

```text
P2 -> 8:C=WL -> 7:first SR -> 4:first WR -> 5:first NR
   -> 8:E1=ER -> 7:first SL -> 10:F1=EL
   -> north arrival on 11
```

is an eight-step first return. Its endpoint is the
`(k+2)`-nd north arrival on eleven.

If `P2=NL`, its consecutive target is `NR`, and `P2`
is the `(k+1)`-st north arrival. If `P2=SR`, its target
is `NL=P3`. In the latter case there is also the square

```text
8:E1=ER -> 7:first SL -> 10:F1=EL -> 11:P3=NL -> 8:I=WL,
```

so `tau(I)-tau(E1)=4`.

In L0, `P2=NL` is impossible. It would be the first
north arrival, forcing the earlier west departure to
be `P1=SR`, immediately before `P2=NL`. There is no
available right turn for an intervening event.

But

```text
P1 -> 8:a=WR -> 9:first NR -> 12:first east arrival G1
```

has only three steps. If `G1=ER`, its south step gives
a square return from `P1=SR` to `SL`, contrary to the
immediate successor `P2=NL`. If `G1=EL`, the south
predecessor of `P1` on twelve has a square first return
to `G1`. It must be `ER`, requiring an east arrival
before the first one. Both turns are impossible.

Consequently L0 forces `P2=SR`, `P3=NL` supplied by
`F1`, and the four-step gap from `E1` to `I`.

#### L0 is impossible

The forced square gives `tau(I)-tau(E1)=4`, so (1)
becomes

```text
tau(E2) - tau(F1) = 12.                             (3)
```

But the L0 word on ten contains the consecutive events

```text
F1=EL, WR, S1=WL, WR, S2=WL.
```

Their four gaps total at least sixteen. On nine, the
second `SL`, supplied by `S2`, immediately precedes
the third `NR`, supplied by `E2`. Cancelling their
incoming vertical steps gives `tau(E2)-tau(S2)>=4`.
Therefore

```text
tau(E2) - tau(F1)
  = [tau(E2) - tau(S2)] + [tau(S2) - tau(F1)]
  >= 4 + 16 = 20,
```

contrary to (3). This excludes the whole word L0.

#### L1 requires P2=NL

Suppose L1 instead has `P2=SR`. The same square again
gives (3). Now the consecutive events after `F1` are

```text
F1=EL, WR, S2=WL.
```

Each of their two gaps is at least four, as is the gap
from `S2` to `E2` obtained on nine. Equation (3) forces
all three to equal four. In particular, `F1` must return
to the same physical cell at the next `WR` after four
steps.

Its actual four-step continuation, however, is fixed:

```text
10:F1=EL -> 11:P3=NL -> 8:I=WL -> 7:second SR -> 4:first WL.
```

The direction word `N W S W` does not return to the
starting cell. Thus `P2=SR` is impossible in L1.

It follows that `P2=NL` is the second north arrival
on eleven, immediately followed by its third north
arrival `NR`, supplied by `F1`.

The first west departure `P1` must be `SR`. Its only
possible north predecessor would be the first north
arrival, which cannot itself be `NL=P1`: no earlier
right turn would then be available on eleven.

As above, a right turn at the first east arrival `G1`
on twelve gives a square from `P1` to `SL`; a left turn
would require an earlier east arrival. Hence `G1=ER`,
and the immediate successor of `P1` is `SL`, not `P2`.
The first north arrival must lie between them. The
initial word on eleven is therefore

```text
11: P1=SR,SL,first NR,P2=NL,third NR,... .
```

The south predecessor of `P1` on twelve is `WL`,
immediately before `G1=ER`, by its square return. Since
`P1` is the first south arrival on eleven and `G1` is
the first east arrival on twelve, this also fixes

```text
12: WR,WL,G1=ER,... .
```

The surviving words on ten are R1, R2, L1, and L2.
This excludes nine of the thirteen initial placements,
but does not exclude N or the `(1,-3)` orbit.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-n-third-west-corridor-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_n_third_west_corridor_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_n_third_west_corridor_2026-09-15.md`.

### The third west departure in continuation N

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: the whole N continuation, including
R1, R2, L1, and L2, is excluded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.

The four offset-ten words R1, R2, L1, and L2 remain
open. Their possible north arrivals on eleven can be
restricted further without choosing a word on twelve.

In both right-turn cases R1 and R2, the second west
departure on eleven is `SR`. In R2 the third is also
`SR`. In L1, if the third west departure is `NL`, it
must be the fifth north arrival, supplied by the fourth
`WR` on ten. In either turn case, L1 requires a gap of
at least sixteen between `E1=ER` and `I=WL` on eight.

Use the notation of
`odd_skew_upper_a_n_first_east_reduction_2026-09-15.md`.
The labels R1 and R2 here name words on ten, not the
upper-cap form or distinct drift orbits.

#### A twelve-step segment with no return to ten

Let `P3` be the third west departure on eleven, feeding
`8:I=WL`. Suppose `P3=NL`, and let `U` be its north
predecessor on ten. Thus `U` is either `WR` or `EL`.
There is a fixed path

```text
10:U -> 11:P3=NL -> 8:I=WL -> 7:second SR -> 4:first WL
     -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:first SL
     -> 6:first ER -> 5:first SL -> 8:E2=EL.
```

Its direction word is

```text
N W S W S W N E S E S E.
```

The voltage is zero, giving

```text
tau(E2) = tau(U) + 12.                              (1)
```

There is no visit to offset ten after the starting event
anywhere in these twelve steps. Hence there is no return
to the starting physical cell during that segment. The
next stable visit on ten is at least sixteen steps after
`U`, since same-cell return lengths are multiples of four.
In particular,

```text
U is the last stable event on ten before E2.          (2)
```

On nine, the second `SL`, the third `NR`, and the third
`SL` are supplied by `S2`, `E2`, and `S3` respectively,
by zero-voltage vertical steps. Therefore

```text
S2 < E2 < S3.                                       (3)
```

These restrictions are stronger than the order of north
arrivals alone: a north predecessor earlier in the word
would require a visit to ten inside the fixed segment.

#### The possible predecessors of P3=NL

Combining (2) and (3) with the four words on ten gives
the following list. Subscripts on `WR` count west right
turns on ten.

| Word on ten | Possible predecessor U of P3=NL |
|---|---|
| R1 | `WR1`, between `S2` and `S3` |
| R2 | none |
| L1 | `WR4`, between `S2` and `S3` |
| L2 | `F1=EL` or the following `WR4` |

For R2, `S2=F1=ER` immediately precedes `S3=WL`.
Neither event departs north, and there is no intervening
event that could be `U`. Thus `P3=SR` in R2.

In L1, the relevant segment is

```text
F1=EL, WR3, S2=WL, WR4, S3=WL.
```

The north arrival from `F1` is already `NR`, by the
previous reduction. Only `WR4` can satisfy (2). It is
the fifth north departure on ten, so `P3` is the fifth
north arrival on eleven. In particular, the fourth north
arrival, supplied by `WR3`, is `NR`.

In L2, the segment is

```text
S2=WL, WR3, F1=EL, WR4, S3=WL.
```

The event `WR3` cannot be `U`, because `F1<E2` would
then be an intervening visit to ten. If `U=F1`, (1)
gives `E2=F1+12`, and the following `WR4` is at least
sixteen steps after `F1`; in particular, `E2<WR4`.
This is precisely the possibility with `P2=SR` and
`P3=NL` supplied by `F1`. If instead `U=WR4`, the
north arrival from `F1` is `NR`, and `P2=NL`.

#### P2 turns right in R1 and R2

The fixed square through the first `SR` on seven gives

```text
tau(8:C=WL) = tau(E1) - 4.
```

The west step from `P2` to `C` has upper voltage one,
while the two-step path from `E1` to `F1` has voltage
minus one. Consequently

```text
tau(P2) = tau(C) + N - 1 = tau(F1) - 7.              (4)
```

In R1, `F1=ER` is the first event on ten. No north
arrival on eleven can occur before it, so (4) excludes
`P2=NL`. Thus `P2=SR`. There is also no north arrival
before `P1`, fixing the prefix

```text
11: P1=SR,SL,P2=SR,... .
```

In R2 the only north departure on ten before `F1` is
its initial `WR1`. If `P2=NL`, it must therefore be
the first north arrival on eleven. Then `P1=SR`
immediately precedes it. The first-east obstruction
already used in the L0 exclusion applies: the path

```text
P1 -> 8:first WR -> 9:first NR -> 12:first east arrival
```

cannot end at `ER`, which would return to `SL` instead
of `P2=NL`, or at `EL`, which would require an earlier
east arrival on twelve. Thus `P2=SR` in R2 as well.

The first west departure is again `P1=SR`: an `NL`
there would be the first north arrival, with no earlier
right turn available. The initial `WR1` on ten precedes
`S1=WL,F1=ER` by at least eight steps. Its north arrival
therefore precedes `P2`, strictly since `P2=SR`.

The next north departure on ten is after `S3`, hence
after `E2`. But `tau(P3)=tau(E2)-11`. There is only
one north arrival before `P3`, and it precedes `P2`.
The two possible prefixes in R2 are therefore

```text
11: P1=SR,SL,first NR,SL,P2=SR,SL,P3=SR,...;
11: first NR,SL,P1=SR,SL,P2=SR,SL,P3=SR,... .
```

#### The gap in L1 is at least sixteen

Put `d=tau(I)-tau(E1)`. In L1 the north arrival from
`F1=EL` is `NR`; call it `V`. Since

```text
tau(P3) = tau(I) + N - 1,
tau(V)  = tau(F1) + 1 = tau(E1) + N + 3,
```

one has

```text
tau(P3) - tau(V) = d - 4.                           (5)
```

If `P3=NL`, its predecessor is `WR4`, as above. The
three consecutive gaps in

```text
10: F1=EL,WR3,S2=WL,WR4
```

total at least twelve. Comparing the north predecessors
of `V` and `P3` in (5) gives `d-4>=12`, hence `d>=16`.

Suppose instead that `P3=SR`. There must be at least
one event between the right turns `V=NR` and `P3=SR`,
so (5) gives `d>=12`. Equality would force exactly
three consecutive events

```text
11: V=NR,SL,P3=SR
```

with both gaps equal to four. Each four-step return
starts with an east departure and ends with a south
arrival, so both are the square `E N W S`. The two
east arrivals on fourteen supplied by `V` and `SL`
would consequently both be `EL`, four steps apart
at the same physical cell. This violates turn alternation.
Thus `d=12` is impossible. Since `d` is a multiple of
four, this also gives `d>=16` in the `SR` case.

No entire word on ten is excluded by these further
restrictions. R1, R2, L1, L2, continuation N, and the
`(1,-3)` orbit remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-eighth-word-forms-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_eighth_word_forms_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_eighth_word_forms_2026-09-15.md`.

### The offset-eight words remaining for Q

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: suffixes `M_R0,M_R1` are excluded
for all four prefixes in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_right_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
The further exclusion of `M_L0` is in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
The list below now leaves twelve formal words, using
only `M_L1,A_0,A_1` as suffixes.

The four offset-seven words left for Q determine a
finite list of full words on eight. There are four
possible prefixes through `E3=ER`. Each can be followed
by one of four suffixes in the middle placement, or
one of two suffixes in the after placement.

These are necessary words, not realizations. No whole
remaining Q word is excluded here. The split at `E5`
in the middle placement remains unresolved.

Use the notation of
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15.md`.
The north arrival `V=NR` on seven is supplied by the
last `WR` on six. Its placement is either between
`W4,W5` or after `W5`. The prefixes A and B refer
to the two orders before `W3=NL` on seven.

#### The second west departure on nine

The west departures on nine feed

```text
D1 -> 6:WR1;
D2 -> 6:WL1;
D3 -> 6:WL2;
D4 -> 6:WR2.
```

The turn at `E2` determines the turn at `D2`:

```text
E2=ER  => D2=SR;
E2=EL  => D2=NL, supplied by WR immediately before E2.
```

For the first claim, suppose `D2=NL`. Its north
predecessor on eight has the square first return

```text
north predecessor -> 9:D2=NL -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SL1 -> 8:E2.
```

If `E2=ER`, that predecessor must be `EL`, immediately
before `E2`. It would be the first east arrival `E1`,
which is already `ER`. Hence `D2=SR`.

For the second claim, suppose `E2=EL,D2=SR`. The
square from `D2` through `6:WL1,5:SL1,E2` forces its
consecutive target to be `D3=NL`, supplied by `E2`.
Then

```text
8:E2=EL -> 9:D3=NL -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
```

is a square with two left-turn endpoints. Thus
`D2=NL`. Its incoming square on eight forces the
`WR` immediately before `E2`.

#### Four prefixes through E3

The south ordinals on eight are fixed through `E3`:

```text
prefix A: E1=ER is second;  E3=ER is sixth;
prefix B: E1=ER is third;   E3=ER is sixth.
```

The fifth south departure, supplying `W4=SR`, is
`WL`, immediately before `E3=ER`. Its eight-step
first return through `W4,4:WR2,5:NL,2:WL,1:SL,4:EL,
5:NR2` also fixes that gap to eight.

If `E2=ER`, successive east right turns require a
`WL` between them. Prefix B would put `E2` no earlier
than the fifth south ordinal and `E3` no earlier than
the seventh. It is therefore impossible. In prefix A,
`E2=ER` must be the fourth south departure, giving
the first prefix below.

If `E2=EL`, its forced incoming `WR` rules out placing
it immediately after `E1=ER`. It cannot follow the fifth
south departure either, since that `WL` immediately
precedes `E3`. In A it can follow the third or fourth
south departure; in B only the fourth. Alternation gives
exactly four prefixes:

```text
A_R:  WR,WL,E1=ER,WL,E2=ER,WL,E3=ER;

A_L3: WR,WL,E1=ER,WL,WR,E2=EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,E3=ER;

A_L4: WR,WL,E1=ER,WL,WR,WL,WR,E2=EL,WR,WL,E3=ER;

B_L4: WR,WL,WR,WL,E1=ER,WL,WR,E2=EL,WR,WL,E3=ER.
```

In `A_R`, `D2=SR`; in the other three, `D2=NL`.

#### Four suffixes for the middle placement

Here `E3=ER` is the sixth south departure, `W5` is
the eighth south arrival on seven, and there are nine
south departures on eight.

If `E5=ER`, it is the ninth and last south departure,
immediately followed by `E6=EL`. The eighth south
departure is `WL`, immediately before `E5`, by the
square through `W5,4:WR3,5:NR3`. Taking `E4=ER`
would exceed the nine south departures, so `E4=EL`.
It can occur before or after the seventh south departure,
but not after the eighth.

If `E5=EL`, its square forces the consecutive pair
`E4=ER,E5=EL`, with `E4` the eighth south departure.
The seventh is the `WL` between `E3` and `E4`.
The ninth is also `WL`, either before or after `E6=EL`.

Thus append one of the following suffixes after `E3`
in any of the four prefixes above. Subscripts on `WL`
give its south ordinal on eight.

```text
M_R0: E4=EL,WR,WL7,WR,WL8,E5=ER,E6=EL;
M_R1: WL7,WR,E4=EL,WR,WL8,E5=ER,E6=EL;

M_L0: WL7,E4=ER,E5=EL,WR,WL9,WR,E6=EL;
M_L1: WL7,E4=ER,E5=EL,WR,E6=EL,WR,WL9.
```

#### Two suffixes for the after placement

Here `E3=ER` is the sixth south departure, `W5` is
the seventh south arrival, and `E5=ER` is the eighth
south departure. The seventh is a `WL` immediately
before `E5`. The port `E4` must be `EL`, before that
`WL`, so it immediately follows `E3`.

The remaining south departure is a `WL`, before or
after `E6=EL`. The two suffixes after `E3` are

```text
A_0: E4=EL,WR,WL7,E5=ER,WL9,WR,E6=EL;
A_1: E4=EL,WR,WL7,E5=ER,E6=EL,WR,WL9.
```

Every suffix has seven events. The full word on eight
therefore has fourteen visits when its prefix is `A_R`,
and eighteen for each of the other prefixes. There are
sixteen formal middle words and eight formal after words
before imposing further return restrictions.

#### Consequences when E5 and E6 are consecutive

In `M_R0,M_R1,A_1`, the final east pair is the
consecutive pair `E5=ER,E6=EL`. The square at `D3`
therefore forces `D3=SR,D4=NL`, with `E6` supplying
`D4`: an `NL` at `D3` would require `WR` immediately
before `E6` instead.

Write `Fj` for the east arrival on ten supplied by
`V=NR`. Since `D4` is the last west departure, its
square through `6:WR2,V,Fj` rules out `Fj=ER`.
Thus `Fj=EL`. The south predecessor of `D3` on ten
then has an eight-step first return to `Fj`, forcing
the consecutive pair `F(j-1)=ER,Fj=EL`.

Consequently,

```text
M_R0 or M_R1: F4=ER,F5=EL are consecutive;
A_1:         F5=ER,F6=EL are consecutive.
```

The same conclusion holds in any other suffix if
`D3=SR`, because its square then gives `D4=NL`
supplied by `E6`. These conditions do not yet exclude
the remaining Q continuations.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-middle-right-exclusion-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_right_exclusion_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_right_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.

### The middle placement in Q requires E5=EL

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

In the middle placement of Q, the fifth east arrival
on eight cannot be `ER`. This excludes both suffixes
`M_R0,M_R1` for all four prefixes in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_eighth_word_forms_2026-09-15.md`.

The middle placement must therefore have the consecutive
pair `E4=ER,E5=EL`. Its two remaining suffixes and both
after-placement suffixes remain open. There are now
sixteen formal words on eight for Q, not twenty-four.
This does not exclude Q or the `(1,-3)` orbit.

#### Consequences of E5=ER in the middle placement

Assume this turn for a contradiction. The word on eight
has one of the following endings, including `E3`:

```text
M_R0: E3=ER,E4=EL,b=WR,L7=WL,c=WR,L8=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL;
M_R1: E3=ER,L7=WL,b=WR,E4=EL,c=WR,L8=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

Here `Li` denotes the `WL` at south ordinal `i` on
eight. In both endings, `L5=WL` immediately precedes
`E3=ER`. It supplies `W4=SR` on seven.

The consecutive pair `E5=ER,E6=EL` forces
`D3=SR,D4=NL` on nine, with `E6` supplying `D4`.
The middle north arrival `V=NR` on seven supplies
`F5=EL` on ten, and the adjacent predecessor is
`F4=ER`, supplying `D3`.

The last north arrival and the last west departure on
nine are both `D4=NL`. No right turn can occur after
it, so the word ends there. Its immediate predecessor
`D3=SR` is consequently the last south arrival. Thus
`F4=ER` is the last south departure on ten. All later
east arrivals turn left, fixing the ending

```text
10: ...,F4=ER,F5=EL,WR,F6=EL,WR,F7=EL.              (1)
```

The port `F6` is supplied by the `SL` after `V` on
seven; that `SL` is supplied by `8:L7=WL`.

#### The west departure feeding L5 is SR

Let `P` be the west departure on eleven feeding `L5`.
There is an eleven-step path

```text
P -> 8:L5=WL -> 7:W4=SR -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL -> 2:WL
  -> 1:SL -> 4:EL -> 5:NR2 -> 8:E3=ER
  -> 7:SL after W4 -> 10:F4=ER.
```

If `P=NL`, starting at its north predecessor on ten
gives a twelve-step first return to `F4=ER`. The
predecessor must be `F3=EL`, immediately before `F4`.

Let `Z` be the south departure on eight supplying the
`SL` before `W2` on seven, and hence `F3`. In prefix
A, `Z` is the third south departure `WL`; in prefix B,
it is `E1=ER`. In every prefix under consideration,

```text
Z < E2 < L5
```

on eight. But the forced path

```text
8:Z -> 7:SL before W2 -> 10:F3=EL -> 11:P=NL -> 8:L5
```

is a square first return. It skips `E2`. Therefore
`P=SR`.

Extend the eleven-step path above through

```text
F4=ER -> 9:D3=SR -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
      -> 9:D4=NL -> 6:WR2 -> 7:V=NR -> 10:F5=EL
      -> north arrival on eleven.
```

This is a twenty-step first return from `P`. There is
no intervening visit to offset eleven. Its endpoint
must be `NL`, the next west departure after `P`.
Write this next departure as `P+`. It is supplied by
`F5=EL`.

#### M_R1 gives a left/left square

In `M_R1`, the next west arrival on eight after `L5`
is `L7=WL`. Thus `P+` feeds `L7`, and

```text
10:F5=EL -> 11:P+=NL -> 8:L7=WL -> 7:SL after V -> 10:F6=EL
```

is a square first return with two left-turn endpoints.
This excludes `M_R1` for every prefix.

#### M_R0 forces a left turn on twelve

In `M_R0`, the next three west arrivals after `L5`
are `b=WR,L7=WL,c=WR`. Let their predecessors on
eleven be `Pb=P+`, `PL`, and `Pc`, respectively.
We already have `Pb=NL`, supplied by `F5=EL`.

The north arrivals on nine supplied by `E4,b,c` are
all `NR`. Indeed, `D1,D2` precede `E4`: the fixed
three-step path from `D2` to `E2` has zero voltage,
so `tau(D2)=tau(E2)-3`. The remaining west departures
are `D3=SR,D4=NL`, and `D4` is supplied by `E6`,
after all three of these north arrivals.

Let `Gb,Gc` be the east arrivals on twelve supplied
by the north arrivals from `b,c`. Suppose `Gb=ER`.
The square through `Pb,b` and the north arrival on
nine returns from `Pb=NL` to `PL=SR`. Its next square
through `L7`, the `SL` after `V`, and `F6=EL` then
returns to `Pc=NL`.

Consequently

```text
12:Gb=ER -> 11:PL=SR -> 8:L7=WL -> 7:SL after V -> 10:F6=EL
         -> 11:Pc=NL -> 8:c=WR -> 9:north arrival from c -> 12:Gc
```

is an eight-step first return. There is at least one
east arrival between `Gb` and `Gc`, supplied by the
`SL` between the north arrivals from `b,c` on nine.
The return skips it. Hence `Gb=EL`.

#### The resulting time order is impossible

Start at the south predecessor `H` of `P=SR` on
twelve. Follow the twenty-step return from `P` to
`Pb=NL`, and then the three steps through
`8:b=WR,9:north arrival from b,Gb=EL`. This is a
twenty-four-step first return to twelve. It forces
`H=ER`, immediately before `Gb=EL`.

On nine, the two consecutive north arrivals supplied
by `E4` and `b` are both `NR`, with a single `SL`
between them. Thus the east arrival immediately before
`Gb`, namely `H`, is supplied by that `SL`. Let `S`
be its south predecessor on ten.

The incoming vertical steps give

```text
tau(S) > tau(E4).                                   (2)
```

But there is now the four-step, zero-voltage path

```text
10:S -> 9:that SL -> 12:H=ER -> 11:P=SR -> 8:L5=WL.
```

Therefore

```text
tau(L5) = tau(S) + 4 > tau(E4).
```

This contradicts `L5<E3<E4` on eight. Hence `M_R0`
is also impossible for every prefix.

Both right-turn suffixes in the middle placement are
excluded. Q's remaining middle words use `M_L0` or
`M_L1`, and its after words use `A_0` or `A_1`.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-middle-third-west-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_third_west_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_third_west_2026-09-15.md`.

### The third west departure is NL in Q's middle placement

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: `M_L0` is excluded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
Only `M_L1` remains in the middle placement.
Its `D4=SR`-after-the-last-north-arrival order is
excluded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_last_west_orders_2026-09-15.md`.

In both remaining middle suffixes of Q, `D3=NL` on
nine. The right-turn alternative is impossible for all
four prefixes on eight. This fixes its north predecessor
to be the `WR` immediately before `E6=EL`.

In `M_L0`, the last west departure is then `D4=SR`.
The east arrival `F5` on ten must also be `ER`.
In `M_L1`, either turn at `D4` remains possible, with
the restricted endings on nine given below. Neither
whole suffix is excluded here; Q and the `(1,-3)`
orbit remain open.

#### Fixed data

After excluding the right-turn middle branch, the two
suffixes on eight, starting at `E3`, are

```text
M_L0: E3=ER,L7=WL,E4=ER,E5=EL,b=WR,L9=WL,c=WR,E6=EL;
M_L1: E3=ER,L7=WL,E4=ER,E5=EL,b=WR,E6=EL,c=WR,L9=WL.
```

Here `Li` denotes the south departure at ordinal `i`
on eight. The `WL` at ordinal five, `L5`, immediately
precedes `E3`. On seven, `L5` supplies `W4=SR`,
`E3` supplies the following `SL`, `L7` supplies the
`SL` after the middle north arrival `V=NR`, and `L9`
supplies the final `SL`.

Thus the last four east arrivals on ten are supplied by

```text
F4: SL after W4;
F5: V=NR;
F6: SL after V;
F7: final SL.
```

The west targets of `D3,D4` on nine are `6:WL2,WR2`.

#### What D3=SR would force

Assume `D3=SR`. Its square through
`6:WL2,5:SL3,E6=EL` has consecutive target `D4=NL`,
supplied by `E6`. Since `D4` is the last west
departure, the square through `6:WR2,V,F5` forces
`F5=EL`. The south predecessor of `D3` on ten then
has an eight-step first return to `F5`, forcing the
consecutive pair

```text
F4=ER,F5=EL.
```

Let `P` be the west departure on eleven feeding `L5`.
The same argument used in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_right_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`
forces `P=SR`: if it were `NL`, its north predecessor
would be `F3=EL`, immediately before `F4=ER`. The
square from the south predecessor of `F3` on eight
to `L5` would skip `E2`.

Its fixed twenty-step first return ends at the north
arrival supplied by `F5=EL`:

```text
P -> 8:L5 -> 7:W4 -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL -> 2:WL -> 1:SL
  -> 4:EL -> 5:NR2 -> 8:E3 -> 7:SL after W4 -> 10:F4
  -> 9:D3 -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6 -> 9:D4
  -> 6:WR2 -> 7:V -> 10:F5 -> north arrival on eleven.
```

Therefore the next west departure after `P` is `NL`,
supplied by `F5`. In both current suffixes the next
west arrival on eight is `L7=WL`. Hence

```text
10:F5=EL -> 11:that NL -> 8:L7=WL -> 7:SL after V -> 10:F6
```

is a square first return. It forces `F6=ER`.

#### M_L0 has no south departure left for F6

In `M_L0`, `E6` is the last north departure on eight,
so its target `D4=NL` would be both the last north
arrival and last west departure on nine. The word
on nine must end there. Its immediate predecessor
`D3=SR` is then the last south arrival.

Thus `F4=ER`, which supplies `D3`, is the last south
departure on ten. This contradicts `F6=ER`.

#### M_L1 contradicts the final square on eleven

In `M_L1`, exactly one north departure on eight follows
`E6`: the port `c=WR`. With `D4` already the last
west departure, its target is `NR`, and the non-wake
word on nine ends

```text
D3=SR,D4=NL,NR,SL.
```

There is only one south arrival after `D3`. Hence
`F4=ER` is the penultimate south departure on ten,
and the forced `F6=ER` is the last. Alternation and
east-arrival order give the final four events

```text
10: ...,F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER,F7=EL.                    (1)
```

The last west arrival on eight is `L9=WL`. Let `T`
be the last west departure on eleven, feeding it.
There is a square first return

```text
T -> 8:L9=WL -> 7:final SL -> 10:F7=EL -> north arrival on eleven.
```

An `SR` source would produce another west departure
`NL` after the last one. Thus `T=NL`. Starting at its
north predecessor on ten gives a square first return
to `F7=EL`; that predecessor must be `WR`, immediately
before `F7`. But in (1) the predecessor is `F6=ER`.
This is impossible.

Both suffixes therefore require `D3=NL`.

#### The remaining endings on nine

The incoming square at `D3=NL` now forces a `WR`
immediately before `E6=EL` on eight. Its consecutive
target on nine is the `NR` supplied by `E6`.

In `M_L0`, this predecessor is `c`. No further north
arrival remains for `D4`, so `D4=SR`. The ending is

```text
9: ...,NR from b,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,D4=SR,SL.
```

In `M_L1`, the predecessor is `b`, and the final
north arrival is supplied by `c`. It is either
`D4=NL`, or an `NR` before or after `D4=SR`. The
three possible endings are

```text
...,D3=NL,NR from E6,D4=NL;
...,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,NR from c,SL,D4=SR,SL;
...,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,D4=SR,SL,NR from c,SL.
```

The earlier restrictions on `D1,D2` and the four
prefixes on eight still apply. These endings narrow
the remaining middle cases without excluding either
whole suffix.

#### M_L0 also requires F5=ER

In `M_L0`, we now have `D3=NL,D4=SR`. Suppose
`F5=EL`. The south predecessor of `D4` on ten has
the square first return through `D4,6:WR2,V,F5`,
forcing the consecutive pair `F4=ER,F5=EL`.

The west departure `P` on eleven feeding `L5` must
again be `SR`, by the skipped-`E2` argument above.
Its eleven-step path to `F4` now continues through
`D4,6:WR2,V,F5` and north. This is a sixteen-step
first return on eleven. Hence the next west departure,
feeding `L7`, is `NL`, supplied by `F5`.

Its square through `L7` and the `SL` after `V` forces
`F6=ER`. The last two south arrivals on nine are
`D4=SR,SL`, so `F4` is the penultimate south departure
on ten and `F6` is the last. Its ending is again

```text
F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER,F7=EL.                            (2)
```

Let `Pb,PL,Pc` be the west departures on eleven
feeding `8:b=WR,L9=WL,c=WR`, in that order. The
last of these is the last west departure on eleven.

The square from `PL` through `L9`, the final `SL`
on seven, and `F7=EL` cannot have an `NL` source:
its incoming north predecessor on ten would have to
be `WR` immediately before `F7`, contrary to (2).
Thus `PL=SR`, and its consecutive target is `Pc=NL`,
supplied by `F7`.

Write `H` for the east arrival on twelve supplied by
the north arrival from `E6` on nine. The path

```text
Pc -> 8:c=WR -> 9:D3=NL -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
   -> 9:north arrival from E6 -> 12:H
```

has seven steps. A right turn at `H` would complete
a first return from `Pc=NL` to another west departure
`SR`, after the last one. Hence `H=EL`.

Starting at the south predecessor `Q` of `PL` on
twelve, use the square from `PL` to `Pc` and then
this seven-step path. The resulting twelve-step first
return to `H=EL` forces `Q=ER`, immediately before H.

The north arrival from `b` on nine is `NR` and
immediately precedes `D3=NL`; that pair is followed
by the `NR` from `E6`. Therefore the east arrival
immediately before `H` is the one supplied by the
north arrival from `b`. Call it `Gb`. We have
`Gb=Q=ER`, supplying `PL`.

Now

```text
Pb -> 8:b=WR -> 9:north arrival from b -> 12:Gb=ER -> 11:PL=SR
```

is a square first return. It forces `Pb=NL`.

But `Pb` occurs strictly between the west departure
feeding `L7`, supplied by `F5=EL`, and `Pc`, supplied
by `F7=EL`. There is no north departure on ten between
`F5` and `F7` in (2): their only intervening event is
`F6=ER`. No north arrival is available for `Pb=NL`.
This contradiction proves `F5=ER` in `M_L0`.

Its south step completes the square from `D4=SR`
to the final `SL` on nine, so `F5` is the last south
departure on ten. The last east arrivals are consequently
`F6=EL,F7=EL`, with `WR` between them. The south
predecessor of `D4` is `WL`, immediately before `F5`.
Thus the necessary ending in this suffix is

```text
10: ...,WL,F5=ER,F6=EL,WR,F7=EL.
```

The turn at `F4` and the rest of `M_L0` remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-ml0-exclusion-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.

### Excluding M_L0 in form Q

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

The middle suffix `M_L0` is impossible for all four
prefixes on eight. Its two higher-row endings need
not be treated separately: the square at `L7` already
has no possible west predecessor on eleven.

This removes four more formal words on eight from Q.
Its middle placement now has only suffix `M_L1`;
the after placement still has `A_0,A_1`. With four
prefixes, twelve formal words remain. This is not an
exclusion of Q or the whole `(1,-3)` orbit.

#### The fixed pair on ten

The existing reduction
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_third_west_2026-09-15.md`
gives, in `M_L0`,

```text
8: ...,E3=ER,L7=WL,E4=ER,E5=EL,b=WR,L9=WL,c=WR,E6=EL;
10: ...,U=WL,F5=ER,F6=EL,X=WR,F7=EL.
```

The port `L7=WL` supplies the `SL` after the middle
north arrival on seven, which in turn supplies `F6`.
In particular, `F5=ER,F6=EL` are consecutive stable
events on ten.

#### Neither turn is possible on eleven

Let `P` be the west departure on eleven feeding `L7`.
There is a square first return

```text
P -> 8:L7=WL -> 7:SL after the middle north arrival
  -> 10:F6=EL -> north arrival on eleven.
```

Suppose `P=SR`. Its consecutive target is `NL`, the
next west departure after `P`. The next west arrival
on eight after `L7` is `b=WR`; call its predecessor
on eleven `Pb`. Thus `Pb=NL` is supplied by `F6`.
But then

```text
8:L7=WL -> 7:that SL -> 10:F6=EL -> 11:Pb=NL -> 8:b=WR
```

is a square first return on eight. It skips both
`E4=ER` and `E5=EL`, which lie between `L7` and `b`
in stable order. Hence `P` cannot be `SR`.

Suppose instead that `P=NL`. Starting at its north
predecessor on ten gives the square first return

```text
north predecessor -> 11:P=NL -> 8:L7=WL -> 7:that SL -> 10:F6=EL.
```

The predecessor must turn right, hence be `WR`,
immediately before `F6=EL`. The actual predecessor
is `F5=ER`, which departs south and cannot supply
`P`. This is also impossible.

Both possible west turns at `P` have been excluded.
Therefore no `M_L0` continuation exists, including
either of the higher-row endings recorded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_two_tails_2026-09-15.md`.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-ml0-two-tails-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_two_tails_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_two_tails_2026-09-15.md`.

### Two higher-row endings for Q, M_L0

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: both endings are excluded by the
common square obstruction in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
The entire `M_L0` suffix is closed.

The suffix `M_L0` has only two possible endings on
eleven, according to the turn at its last west departure.
Each forces an explicit ending on twelve. The turn at
`F4` on ten remains open; this note does not exclude
either entire ending or the suffix itself.

#### Notation and the fixed endings below

Use
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_third_west_2026-09-15.md`.
The relevant suffix on eight is

```text
E3=ER,L7=WL,E4=ER,E5=EL,b=WR,L9=WL,c=WR,E6=EL.
```

On nine, `D3=NL` is supplied by `c`, and `D4=SR`.
The final portion, starting at the north arrival from
`E5`, is

```text
NR from E5,SL,NR from b,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,D4=SR,SL.
```

Let the corresponding six east arrivals on twelve be
`A,B,C,D,E,F`, in that order. Thus `C` is supplied
by the north arrival from `b`, `D` by the north
arrival from `E6`, and `F` is the last east arrival
on twelve. These labels refer only to this local ending.

The word on ten ends

```text
...,U=WL,F5=ER,F6=EL,X=WR,F7=EL.                    (1)
```

Write `Pb,PL,Pc` for the west departures on eleven
feeding `b,L9,c` on eight, respectively. They are
the last three west departures, with `Pc` the last.

#### All three occur after the north arrival from F6

The port `F6` is supplied by `L7` through the `SL`
after the middle north arrival on seven. Hence

```text
tau(F6) = tau(L7) + N + 2.
```

For each of the three west departures,

```text
tau(Pb) = tau(b)  + N - 1;
tau(PL) = tau(L9) + N - 1;
tau(Pc) = tau(c)  + N - 1.
```

There are at least three stable gaps between `L7`
and `b`, giving `tau(b)-tau(L7)>=12`; the other
two west targets occur still later. Therefore all
three departures occur strictly after `tau(F6)+1`,
the north arrival on eleven supplied by `F6`.

By (1), only two north arrivals remain available:
those supplied by `X` and `F7`.

The square

```text
PL -> 8:L9=WL -> 7:final SL -> 10:F7=EL -> north arrival on eleven
```

gives two possibilities. If `PL=SR`, its consecutive
target is `Pc=NL`, supplied by `F7`. If `PL=NL`,
its incoming predecessor on ten must be `X=WR`,
and the target is the final `NR`, supplied by `F7`.
In the latter case neither `Pb` nor `Pc` has an
available north predecessor, so both are `SR`.

#### Case I: Pc=NL

Here `PL=SR` and `Pc=NL` is supplied by `F7`.
The seven-step path

```text
Pc -> 8:c=WR -> 9:D3=NL -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
   -> 9:north arrival from E6 -> 12:D
```

rules out `D=ER`: its south step would give another
west departure after the last one. Thus `D=EL`.

Starting at the south predecessor of `PL` on twelve,
follow its square to `Pc` and then this seven-step
path. The twelve-step first return to `D=EL` forces
that predecessor to be the immediately preceding east
arrival `C=ER`, supplying `PL`.

The square through `Pb,b`, the north arrival from
`b`, and `C=ER` then forces `Pb=NL`, immediately
before `PL=SR`. Its only available north predecessor
is `X`. Since `Pc` is also the last north arrival
on eleven, the ending is

```text
11: ...,Pb=NL,PL=SR,Pc=NL.
```

The last south arrival on eleven is `PL`, supplied
by `C=ER`. There is no south departure on twelve
after `C`. The remaining three east arrivals therefore
turn left, giving the full ending

```text
12: ...,C=ER,D=EL,WR,E=EL,WR,F=EL.
```

The three consecutive gaps `b -> L9 -> c -> E6`
on eight are all four, by the squares through
`C,PL`, through `F7,Pc`, and through `D3,6:WL2,5:SL3`.
The twelve-step return above also gives
`tau(D)-tau(C)=12`.

#### Case II: Pc=SR

Here `PL=NL` is supplied by `X`; the north arrival
from `F7` is `NR`, and `Pb=SR`. There is no north
arrival between `Pb` and `PL`, so they are consecutive.
After `PL`, alternation gives

```text
11: ...,Pb=SR,PL=NL,NR,SL,Pc=SR,SL.
```

The three-step path from `Pb` through `b` and its
north arrival on nine ends at `C` on twelve. A right
turn there would return from `Pb=SR` to `SL`, rather
than its immediate successor `PL=NL`. Hence `C=EL`.
The south predecessor of `Pb` must consequently be
the immediately preceding east arrival `B=ER`.

If `D=EL`, the south predecessor of `Pc` would have
an eight-step first return to `D`, forcing `C=ER`
immediately before it. This contradicts `C=EL`.
Thus `D=ER`; its south step returns from `Pc=SR`
to the final `SL` on eleven. It is the last south
departure on twelve.

Between `B` and `D`, there are exactly two further
south departures, supplying the `SL` before `Pc`
and `Pc` itself. Both are `WL`, since `C=EL` is
the only intervening east arrival. Alternation fixes
the complete ending

```text
12: ...,B=ER,C=EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,D=ER,E=EL,WR,F=EL.
```

The `WL` immediately before `D` supplies `Pc` and
has an eight-step first return to `D`. Also, if `S`
is the south predecessor on ten of the `SL` supplying
`B`, the square through `B,Pb,b` gives
`tau(b)=tau(S)+4`.

#### The unresolved turn at F4

Let `P5` denote the west departure on eleven feeding
the fifth south departure `L5=WL` on eight. If
`F4=ER`, the skipped-`E2` argument in the preceding
notes forces `P5=SR`.

If `F4=EL`, an `SR` source at `P5` would have a
twelve-step first return to the north arrival from
`F4`, forcing the next west departure, feeding `L7`,
to be `NL`. The resulting square from `F4` through
`L7` and the `SL` after the middle north arrival
would end at `F6=EL`, skipping `F5`. Hence `P5=NL`
in this case, with a `WR` immediately before `F4`
on ten.

Thus the remaining split at `F4` is equivalent to
