the turn at this earlier west departure. Neither turn
has been excluded here. The two endings above and
the rest of Q remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-ml1-last-west-orders-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_last_west_orders_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_last_west_orders_2026-09-15.md`.

### The last west-departure orders in Q, M_L1

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

In `M_L1`, the case `D4=SR` after the last north
arrival on nine is impossible. If `D4=SR`, it must
precede that north arrival. The alternative `D4=NL`
remains open.

The resulting endings on ten are restricted below.
In all remaining cases the ending on eleven is

```text
SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL.
```

This does not exclude the entire `M_L1` suffix or Q.

#### Data and two square constraints

The suffix on eight is

```text
E3=ER,L7=WL,E4=ER,E5=EL,b=WR,E6=EL,c=WR,L9=WL.
```

The established turn `D3=NL` on nine is supplied
by `b`; its following `NR` is supplied by `E6`.
The final north arrival is supplied by `c`. Write
`Vc` for that arrival when it is `NR` rather than
the last west departure `D4=NL`.

The east arrivals `F4,F5,F6,F7` on ten are supplied
by the `SL` after `W4`, the middle north arrival,
the following `SL`, and the final `SL` on seven.
The first of those two later `SL` ports is supplied
by `L7`; the last by `L9`.

Two square constraints will be used repeatedly:

1. If `F6=EL`, it must have `WR` immediately before
   it on ten. To see this, let `P7` be the west
   departure on eleven feeding `L7`. An `SR` there
   would return to the next west departure `NL`,
   feeding `b`, and force a square from `L7` to `b`
   that skips `E4,E5`. Thus `P7=NL`. Its incoming
   square on ten forces the claimed `WR` predecessor.

2. If `F7=EL`, it must also have `WR` immediately
   before it on ten. The last west departure on
   eleven feeds `L9`; its square through the final
   `SL` on seven and `F7` cannot have an `SR` source,
   since that would give another west departure after
   the last one. Its `NL` source therefore has the
   required incoming `WR` predecessor on ten.

#### A consequence of D4=SR and F5=EL

Whenever these two turns occur, the square through
`D4,6:WR2`, the middle north arrival on seven, and
`F5` forces the consecutive pair `F4=ER,F5=EL`.

The west departure on eleven feeding `L5`, the `WL`
immediately before `E3`, must then be `SR`: an `NL`
source would require `F3=EL` and a square on eight
that skips `E2`. Its sixteen-step first return through
`F4,D4,6:WR2` and `F5` forces the next west departure,
feeding `L7`, to be `NL`, supplied by `F5`. The square
through `L7` and the `SL` after the middle north
arrival consequently forces `F6=ER`.

These are the same paths used in the last section of
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_third_west_2026-09-15.md`;
none depends on the position of the final `c=WR`.

#### D4=SR cannot follow Vc

Suppose the ending on nine is

```text
...,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,Vc=NR,SL,D4=SR,SL.
```

If `F5=ER`, its square from `D4` supplies the final
`SL` on nine, making `F5` the last south departure
on ten. Thus `F6=EL` immediately follows `F5=ER`.
This contradicts square constraint 1.

If `F5=EL`, the preceding argument forces
`F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER`. Here `F4` supplies `D4`, the
penultimate south arrival on nine, so `F6` is the
last south departure on ten. The final east arrival
is `F7=EL`, immediately after `F6=ER`, contradicting
square constraint 2.

Both turns at `F5` are impossible. Hence a right-turn
`D4` must precede `Vc`.

#### Two endings on ten when D4=SR precedes Vc

The ending on nine is now

```text
...,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL,D4=SR,SL,Vc=NR,SL.           (1)
```

First suppose `F5=ER`. It supplies the `SL` immediately
after `D4`, so it is the penultimate south departure
on ten. Another east right turn at `F6` would require
an intervening `WL`, exceeding the single remaining
south departure. Thus `F6=EL`. A right turn at `F7`
would be both the last east arrival and last south
departure, forcing a wake on ten. Hence `F7=EL`.

Constraint 1 requires a `WR` before `F6`. The required
left turn between `F5=ER` and that `WR` must be the
last south departure `Z=WL`. Alternation fixes

```text
10: ...,F5=ER,Z=WL,WR,F6=EL,WR,F7=EL.               (2)
```

Now suppose `F5=EL`. Then `F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER` are
forced. The first of these supplies `D4`, the third-last
south arrival on nine. If `F6` were the last south
departure on ten, it would immediately precede `F7=EL`,
contradicting constraint 2. Thus `F6` is the penultimate
south departure, and the last is `Z=WL` before `F7`.
The resulting ending is

```text
10: ...,F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER,Z=WL,WR,F7=EL.             (3)
```

In (2), `F5<c<Z` in lifted time. In (3), `F6<c<Z`.
These orders follow from the consecutive `SL,Vc,SL`
at the end of (1), by their incoming vertical steps.

#### The D4=NL alternative

If `D4=NL`, it is supplied by `c`, and nine ends

```text
...,NR from E5,D3=NL,NR from E6,D4=NL.
```

Its last south arrival occurs before the north arrival
from `E5`. Thus the last south departure on ten occurs
before `E5` in lifted time.

The fixed lower paths give `F4>E5`. For completeness,
let `J` be `6:ER2` and let
`g=tau(7:W4)-tau(7:W3)>0`. The square from `6:EL`
to `J`, followed by the seven-step path from `W4`
to `E3`, gives

```text
tau(E3) = tau(J) + g + 4.
```

The two-step path from `J` to `E4`, and the square
from `E4=ER` to `E5=EL`, give

```text
tau(E5) = tau(J) + N + 6.
```

Since `tau(F4)=tau(E3)+N+2`, it follows that
`tau(F4)-tau(E5)=g>0`.

All of `F4,F5,F6,F7` consequently occur after the
last south departure on ten and must be `EL`.
Their ending is

```text
10: ...,F4=EL,WR,F5=EL,WR,F6=EL,WR,F7=EL.           (4)
```

#### A common ending on eleven

Let `Pb,Pc,PL` be the last three west departures on
eleven, feeding `8:b=WR,c=WR,L9=WL` in order.
In every remaining case `F7=EL`, so the last-square
constraint gives `PL=NL`, supplied by the `WR`
immediately before `F7`. Its target is the final `NR`
on eleven, supplied by `F7`.

In (2) and (4), `F6=EL`. Both `Pb` and `Pc` occur
after its north arrival on eleven: for example,

```text
tau(Pb) - [tau(F6)+1] = tau(b)-tau(L7)-4 >= 8,
```

because `L7,E4,E5,b` have at least three four-step
gaps. The only north departures after `F6` are the
last `WR` and `F7`, already assigned to `PL` and
its following `NR`. Hence `Pb=Pc=SR`.

In (3), the west departure feeding `L7` is `NL`,
supplied by `F5`. Both `Pb,Pc` occur after it in
west-arrival order. Again the only later north
departures on ten are the last `WR` and `F7`, so
both must be `SR`.

Alternation now gives the same ending in all cases:

```text
11: ...,Pb=SR,SL,Pc=SR,PL=NL,NR,SL.
```

This common ending leaves the `D4=NL` case and
the two possibilities (2), (3) to be handled. The
whole middle suffix remains open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-ml1-right-west-return-constraints-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_right_west_return_constraints_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_right_west_return_constraints_2026-09-15.md`.

### Return constraints when D4=SR in Q, M_L1

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

In the remaining right-turn case for `D4`, three late
east arrivals on twelve must turn left. If `F5=ER`,
the earlier turn `F4=ER` is also forced, and ten has
the consecutive triple `F4=ER,WL,F5=ER` with both
gaps equal to four. If `F5=EL`, the west departure
on thirteen feeding ten's last south departure is `NL`.

Neither entire `F5` branch is excluded here. The
`D4=NL` alternative and the rest of Q remain open.

#### Notation

Use
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml1_last_west_orders_2026-09-15.md`.
The relevant ending on nine is

```text
NR from E5,D3=NL,NR from E6,SL0,D4=SR,SL1,NR from c,SL2.
```

Call the six corresponding east arrivals on twelve
`A,B,C,D,E,F`, in that order. Thus `C` is supplied
by `SL0`, `D` by `SL1`, and `F` by the final `SL2`.
The ending on eleven is

```text
Pb=SR,SLm,Pc=SR,PL=NL,NR,SL.
```

The west targets of `Pb,Pc,PL` on eight are
`b=WR,c=WR,L9=WL`. The port `PL` is supplied by
the final `WR` on ten, and the following `NR` by
`F7=EL`.

#### The fixed east turns on twelve

The three-step path from `Pc` through `c` and its
north arrival on nine ends at `E`. A right turn
would return from `Pc=SR` to `SL`, contrary to its
immediate successor `PL=NL`. Hence `E=EL`.

The south predecessor of `Pc` on twelve has a square
first return to `E=EL`. It is therefore the preceding
east arrival `D=ER`, immediately before `E`, and it
supplies `Pc`.

This is the penultimate south departure on twelve.
Only one south departure remains, supplying the final
`SL` on eleven. If the last east arrival `F` were
`ER`, it would also be the last south departure,
forcing a wake on twelve. Thus `F=EL`.

The turn at `C` must also be left. The seven-step
path from `Pb` through

```text
8:b=WR,9:D3=NL,6:WL2,5:SL3,8:E6=EL,9:NR from E6,12:B
```

has the following alternatives. If `B=ER`, its south
step supplies `SLm`, the only south arrival between
`Pb` and `Pc`. Then `C=ER` would add a further south
arrival before `D=ER`, which supplies `Pc`.

If `B=EL`, the incoming first return on twelve forces
the consecutive pair `A=ER,B=EL`, with `A` supplying
`Pb`. A right turn at `C` would then have to supply
`SLm`. But `C,D` would be consecutive east right
turns supplying consecutive south arrivals, leaving
no possible `WL` between them. Both alternatives
exclude `C=ER`.

We have therefore proved

```text
C=EL, D=ER, E=EL, F=EL,
```

with `D,E` consecutive stable events. The last south
departure on twelve is a `WL`, either before or
after `F`.

#### F5=ER forces F4=ER

In this branch the ending on ten is

```text
...,U=WL,F5=ER,Z=WL,Y=WR,F6=EL,X=WR,F7=EL.
```

Here `U` supplies `D4`, and `Z` is the last south
departure, supplying `SL2` on nine. The pair `U,F5`
is consecutive with gap four, by the square through
`D4,6:WR2` and the middle north arrival on seven.

Let `Ru` be the west departure on thirteen feeding
`U`. Its next west departure `Rz` feeds `Z`. There
is a twelve-step first return

```text
Ru -> 10:U -> 9:D4 -> 6:WR2 -> 7:middle NR -> 10:F5=ER
   -> 9:SL1 -> 12:D=ER -> 11:Pc=SR -> 8:c=WR
   -> 9:NR from c -> 12:E=EL -> north arrival on thirteen.
```

If `Ru=SR`, its target is `Rz=NL`, supplied by
`E=EL`. Then

```text
12:E=EL -> 13:Rz=NL -> 10:Z=WL -> 9:SL2 -> 12:F=EL
```

is a square with two left-turn endpoints. Therefore
`Ru=NL`.

Starting at the north predecessor of `Ru` on twelve
gives an eight-step first return to `D=ER`. That
predecessor must be `C=EL`, immediately before `D`.
Consequently

```text
9:SL0 -> 12:C=EL -> 13:Ru=NL -> 10:U=WL -> 9:D4=SR
```

is a square first return. Let `T` be the south
predecessor of `SL0` on ten. The incoming vertical
steps give `tau(U)-tau(T)=4`. Hence `T` and `U`
are consecutive stable events with opposite turns.
Since `U=WL`, the south departure `T` must be `ER`.

The following event after `U` is `F5=ER`, so `T`
is the preceding east arrival `F4=ER`. We obtain

```text
10: ...,F4=ER,U=WL,F5=ER,Z=WL,Y=WR,F6=EL,X=WR,F7=EL;
tau(U)-tau(F4)=4;
tau(F5)-tau(U)=4.
```

In this branch, `C=EL,D=ER,E=EL` are also three
consecutive stable events on twelve.

#### F5=EL forces a left west departure on thirteen

The other branch has

```text
10: ...,F4=ER,F5=EL,F6=ER,Z=WL,X=WR,F7=EL.
```

Let `Rz,Rx` be the last two west departures on
thirteen, feeding `Z,X` respectively. Suppose
`Rz=SR`. Its square through `Z,SL2,F=EL` forces
the consecutive target `Rx=NL`, supplied by `F`.

Let `J` be the east arrival on fourteen supplied by
the penultimate east departure on eleven, namely
the final `NR` from `F7`. The path

```text
Rx -> 10:X=WR -> 11:PL=NL -> 8:L9=WL -> 7:final SL
   -> 10:F7=EL -> 11:final NR -> 14:J
```

has seven steps. If `J=ER`, it would return from
the last west departure `Rx=NL` to another west
departure `SR`. Thus `J=EL`.

Starting at the south predecessor of `Rz` on fourteen,
follow its square to `Rx` and then this seven-step
path. The twelve-step first return to `J=EL` forces
its immediately preceding east arrival `J0=ER` to
supply `Rz`. The source of `J0` is `SLm` on eleven:
the events between `SLm` and the final `NR` are the
west departures `Pc,PL`.

This gives the eight-step first return

```text
11:SLm -> 14:J0=ER -> 13:Rz=SR -> 10:Z=WL -> 9:SL2
       -> 12:F=EL -> 13:Rx=NL -> 10:X=WR -> 11:PL=NL.
```

Its direction word is `E S W S E N W N`. Both
endpoints turn left. Hence `Rz=SR` is impossible.

Therefore `Rz=NL`. Its incoming square on twelve
forces `WR` immediately before `F=EL`; the following
north arrival on thirteen, supplied by `F`, is `NR`.
This fixes another west turn but does not exclude
the entire branch.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-q-requires-wake-six-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_q_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_q_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15.md`.

### Form Q requires a wake at six

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: in the remaining middle placement,
`E5=EL` is now forced. See
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_middle_right_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
The exclusion of `M_L0` in
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_ml0_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`
further leaves only `M_L1` in that placement.

At the upper cap of family A, form Q cannot have
`omega_6=0`. With `omega_6=1`, the third north arrival
on seven cannot precede its fourth west departure.
Only four words on seven remain, listed below.

Thus Q requires `h=1`, or `p=m-1`. The argument uses
stable order and same-cell first returns, without a
simulation or a bound on a black lifetime. It does not
exclude the whole Q form. Form P, family B, and the
other cap profiles in `(1,-3)` remain open.

#### Fixed ports and the first north arrival on seven

The normal form is

```text
5: NR1,SL1,SR,NL,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3;
6: WR1,WL1,ER1,EL,ER2,WL2,[WR2]^e,     e=omega_6.
```

Use `W1,...,W5` for the west departures on seven,
feeding `4:WR1,WL1,WL2,WR2,WR3`. Its north arrivals
are supplied by `6:WR1,EL,[WR2]^e` in that order.

The return through the first `WL` on four already
forces `W2=SR`, immediately followed by `W3=NL`;
the latter is the second north arrival on seven. See
`odd_skew_upper_a_two_er_normal_form_2026-09-15.md`.

The first west departure cannot be the first north
arrival `NL`: no earlier right turn would then be
available on seven. Hence `W1=SR`, and the first
north arrival is `NR`.

Let `E1,...,E6` be the east arrivals on eight, supplied
by `5:NR1,SL1,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3`. The three-step path

```text
W1 -> 4:WR1 -> 5:NR1 -> 8:E1
```

forces `E1=ER`. A left turn would make the south
predecessor of `W1` an earlier east right turn, before
the first east arrival. Its south step therefore gives
a square from `W1=SR` to the following `SL`.

The first north arrival must precede the consecutive
pair `W2,W3`. These facts give two possible prefixes:

```text
A: W1=SR,SL,first NR,SL,W2=SR,W3=NL;
B: first NR,SL,W1=SR,SL,W2=SR,W3=NL.
```

In A, `E1=ER` is the second south departure on eight;
in B it is the third.

#### W4 and W5 both turn right

If `W4=NL`, it must be the optional third north
arrival. No right turn could then occur between
`W3=NL` and `W4=NL`. Thus `W4=SR`.

There is a seven-step path

```text
W4 -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL -> 2:WL -> 1:SL -> 4:EL
   -> 5:NR2 -> 8:E3.
```

If `E3=EL`, the south predecessor of `W4` on eight
has an eight-step first return to `E3`. It must be
`E2=ER`, immediately before it. But then

```text
5:SL1 -> 8:E2=ER -> 7:W4=SR -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL
```

is a square with two left-turn endpoints. Hence
`E3=ER`, and its south step gives a first return
from `W4=SR` to the following `SL`.

The last west departure `W5` cannot be the remaining
north arrival `NL`, since no right turn would be
available after this `SL` and before `W5`. Therefore
`W5=SR`. If the third north arrival exists, it is `NR`.

For `e=0`, the suffix after either prefix A or B is

```text
W4=SR,SL,W5=SR,SL.
```

For `e=1`, the third north arrival `V=NR` has three
possible positions:

```text
before W4:       V=NR,SL,W4=SR,SL,W5=SR,SL;
between W4,W5:   W4=SR,SL,V=NR,SL,W5=SR,SL;
after W5:        W4=SR,SL,W5=SR,SL,V=NR,SL.
```

#### The last east arrival on eight is EL

The relevant south ordinals are the same for prefixes
A and B:

| Case | E3=ER | W5 | Total south arrivals on seven |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `e=0` | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| `V` before `W4` | 7 | 8 | 9 |
| `V` between `W4,W5` | 6 | 8 | 9 |
| `V` after `W5` | 6 | 7 | 9 |

If `E5=EL`, the square through `W5,4:WR3,5:NR3`
forces its south predecessor on eight to be the
consecutive east arrival `E4=ER`, supplying `W5`.
In the first, second, and fourth rows this would put
the successive east right turns `E3,E4` at consecutive
south ordinals. A `WL` between them is required by
alternation, so those three rows instead have `E5=ER`.
It supplies the `SL` following `W5`.

In the first two rows `E5` is the last south departure;
hence `E6=EL`, immediately following it. In the fourth
row `E5` is the penultimate south departure. Taking
`E6=ER` would require both an intervening `WL` and
another south departure, so again `E6=EL`.

In the middle row, either `E5=ER` is the last south
departure, or `E4=ER,E5=EL` are consecutive with `E4`
at the penultimate south ordinal. In the latter case,
`E6=ER` would be both the last east arrival and last
south departure, forcing a wake on eight. Eight is
non-wake. Thus `E6=EL` in every row.

#### The zero-wake band is impossible

The west departures `D1,...,D(3+e)` on nine feed

```text
6: WR1,WL1,WL2,[WR2]^e.
```

There is a square path from `D3` through
`6:WL2,5:SL3,E6=EL` to a north arrival on nine.
If `D3=NL`, its north predecessor on eight must be
`WR`, immediately before `E6`, by the corresponding
square return on eight.

In the zero-wake row the predecessor of `E6` is
instead `E5=ER`. Hence `D3=SR`. Its square return
would then have the consecutive target `NL`, producing
a fourth west departure on nine. There are only three
when `e=0`. This excludes the entire zero-wake band
of Q, for either prefix A or B.

#### The third north arrival cannot precede W4

In the `V`-before-`W4` row, `E5=ER,E6=EL` are
again consecutive. The same argument forces
`D3=SR,D4=NL`, with `E6` supplying `D4`.

Let `F1,...,F7` be the east arrivals on ten. In this
row the optional north arrival `V`, supplied by the
last `WR` on six, supplies `F4`. If `F4=ER`, the path

```text
D4=NL -> 6:WR2 -> 7:V=NR -> 10:F4=ER -> south arrival on 9
```

would be a square first return with target `SR`, giving
another west departure after the last one. Thus `F4=EL`.

The south predecessor of `D3` on ten has the
eight-step first return

```text
south predecessor -> 9:D3=SR -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
                  -> 9:D4=NL -> 6:WR2 -> 7:V=NR -> 10:F4=EL.
```

That predecessor must therefore be `F3=ER`, immediately
before `F4`. In both prefixes A and B, `F3` is supplied
by the `SL` immediately before `W2` on seven. Thus

```text
7:SL before W2 -> 10:F3=ER -> 9:D3=SR -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3
               -> 8:E6=EL -> 9:D4=NL -> 6:WR2 -> 7:V=NR
```

is an eight-step first return. Its direction word is
`E S W S E N W N`. It skips `W2=SR,W3=NL` between
the displayed endpoints on seven. This is impossible.

#### Remaining Q words

Only `e=1`, with `V` between `W4,W5` or after `W5`,
remains. The four complete words on seven are

```text
A, middle: SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,SL;
B, middle: NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,SL;
A, after:  SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL;
B, after:  NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL.
```

In every word `E6=EL`. In the after case `E5=ER`;
the middle case still permits either turn at `E5`.
These four words and their higher continuations are
not excluded here. Since Q now requires `h=1`, upper
offsets seven through fifteen are all non-wake.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-r-exclusion-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.

### Excluding form R at the upper cap of family A

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Form R is impossible. Its zero-wake band was excluded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15.md`,
and continuation M in
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_middle_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
The argument below excludes the remaining continuation N.

The upper clock forces the last two unresolved west
departures on eleven to be `SR`. Their return paths then
require two consecutive east right turns on twelve with
no possible left turn between them. The alternative east
turn gives an eight-step right/right first return.

The argument covers N directly; it does not split into
the four remaining words on ten. Thus all of R1, R2,
L1, and L2 from the first-east reduction are excluded.
Forms P and Q, family B, and the other cap profiles of
the `(1,-3)` orbit remain open.

#### The fixed words and notation

Use upper inward headings and lifted times. In N,

```text
5: NR1,SL1,SR,NL,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3;
6: ER1,WL1,ER2,WL2,WR1,EL,WR2;
7: SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL;
8: a=WR,C=WL,E1=ER,I=WL,b=WR,E2=EL,c=WR,E3=EL,
   d=WR,E4=EL,e=WR,J=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

The four west departures on nine feed, in order,
`6:WL1,WL2,WR1,WR2`. Its full word is

```text
9: NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,D1=NL,NR,SL,NR,SL,
   D2=SR,D3=NL,D4=SR,SL.
```

Let `P1,...,P8` be the west departures on eleven,
feeding `8:a,C,I,b,c,d,e,J` in order. The existing
reduction fixes

```text
10: ...,Y=WL,F2=ER,K=WL,X=WR,F3=EL;
11: ...,P8=NL,NR,SL.
```

Here `X` supplies `P8`, and `F3` supplies the following
`NR`. Let `G1,...,G14` be the east arrivals on twelve.
The four needed below are supplied as follows:

```text
G9:  8:E3 -> 9:fifth NR -> 12:G9;
G10: 8:E4 -> 9:seventh NR -> 12:G10;
G11: 10:fifth south departure -> 9:fifth SL -> 12:G11;
G12: 8:e -> 9:eighth NR -> 12:G12.
```

In particular, `G10,G11` are consecutive east arrivals.

#### P6 and P7 occur after the early north arrivals

The established square gaps on six give

```text
tau(6:WR2) = tau(6:WL2) + 12.
```

There is an eight-step, zero-voltage path

```text
6:WR2 -> 7:second NL -> 4:second WR -> 5:NL -> 2:WL
      -> 1:SL -> 4:EL -> 5:NR2 -> 8:E3=EL.
```

Thus `tau(E3)=tau(6:WL2)+20`. The two-step path
`6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6` has upper voltage minus one,
so

```text
tau(E6) = tau(E3) + N - 18.
```

The path

```text
10:Y=WL -> 9:D2=SR -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
```

has length four and zero voltage. Consequently

```text
tau(Y) = tau(E3) + N - 22.                          (1)
```

The west steps from `P6,P7` to `d,e` have upper voltage
one. Since both `d` and `e` follow `E3` on eight,

```text
tau(P6) = tau(d) + N - 1 > tau(Y) + 21;
tau(P7) = tau(e) + N - 1 > tau(Y) + 21.              (2)
```

Every north departure on ten before `Y` supplies a
north arrival on eleven before `tau(Y)+1`. After `Y`,
the only north departures on ten are `X` and `F3`,
whose targets are already `P8=NL` and the final `NR`.
Neither can supply `P6` or `P7`. Equation (2) therefore
forces both departures to be `SR`.

There is no remaining north arrival between `P6` and
`P8`. Alternation fixes the ending

```text
11: ...,P6=SR,SL,P7=SR,P8=NL,NR,SL.                 (3)
```

In particular, the `SL` following `P6` and `P7` itself
are consecutive south arrivals on eleven.

#### The square at P7 forces G11=ER

The path

```text
P7 -> 8:e=WR -> 9:eighth NR -> 12:G12
```

has three steps. If `G12=ER`, its south step completes
a square first return from `P7=SR` to `SL`. But the
immediate successor in (3) is `P8=NL`. Hence `G12=EL`.

Starting instead at the south predecessor of `P7` on
twelve gives a square first return to `G12=EL`. That
predecessor must be `ER`, immediately before `G12`.
It is therefore `G11=ER`, and it supplies `P7`.

#### A left turn at G10 gives a right/right return

There is an eleven-step path

```text
P6 -> 8:d=WR -> 9:D1=NL -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SR -> 2:WR
   -> 3:NR -> 6:ER2 -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=EL
   -> 9:seventh NR -> 12:G10.
```

Starting at the south predecessor of `P6` on twelve
gives a twelve-step first return to `G10`. Suppose
`G10=EL`. Its predecessor must then be `ER`, immediately
before it, so it is `G9=ER` and supplies `P6`.

This forces the eight-step path

```text
5:NR2 -> 8:E3=EL -> 9:fifth NR -> 12:G9=ER
      -> 11:P6=SR -> 8:d=WR -> 9:D1=NL -> 6:WL1 -> 5:SR.
```

Its direction word is

```text
E N E S W N W S.
```

The displayed endpoint is its first return to the starting
physical cell. Both endpoint ports, `NR2` and `SR`, turn
right. This violates turn alternation. Hence `G10=ER`.

#### Two east right turns with no intervening left turn

With `G10=ER`, the eleven-step path from `P6` followed
by its south step is a twelve-step first return on eleven.
It supplies the `SL` immediately after `P6` in (3).

Thus the consecutive east arrivals `G10=ER,G11=ER`
on twelve supply consecutive south arrivals on eleven:
the `SL` after `P6`, and `P7=SR`. There can be no `WL`
between them on twelve, since it would supply another
south arrival between those two. There can be no `EL`
between them either, since they are consecutive east
arrivals. No left turn is available between these two
right turns, contradicting alternation on twelve.

This excludes N and completes the exclusion of form R
at the upper cap of family A. The remaining upper forms
in this family are P and Q, each with `omega_6=0` or
`1`. This is not an exclusion of the whole `(1,-3)` orbit.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-r-middle-exclusion-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_r_middle_exclusion_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_r_middle_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.

### Excluding the middle-north continuation of form R

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: the remaining N continuation is excluded
in `odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`, closing
the entire upper form R in family A.

Of the two offset-seven words left for form R, word M is
impossible. Only word N remains:

```text
7: SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL.
```

For N, the word on nine is fixed as

```text
NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL,SR,NL,SR,SL.
```

The second east arrival on ten turns right, and its final
five events are `WL,ER,WL,WR,EL`. The remaining word N
is not excluded here. Forms P and Q, family B, and the
other cap profiles in the `(1,-3)` orbit remain open.

#### A common left/left obstruction

Both candidate continuations have

```text
5: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
6: ER1,WL1,ER2,WL2,WR1,EL,WR2.
```

Write `Wi` for west departures and `Ni` for north arrivals
on seven. The first north arrival is `W3=NL=N1`, supplied
by `6:WR1`; the next is `N2=NR`, supplied by `6:EL`.
In both M and N, the first `SL` on seven precedes `W3`.

Let `Ei` be the east arrivals on eight, `Di` the west
departures on nine, and `Fi` the east arrivals on ten.
The previous reduction gives the consecutive final pair
`E5=ER,E6=EL` on eight. The west targets on six are

```text
D1 -> WL1,       D2 -> WL2,       D3 -> WR1,       D4 -> WR2.
```

The square from `D2` through `6:WL2,5:SL3,E6=EL`
cannot have an `NL` source: its incoming predecessor on
eight would need to be `WR` immediately before `E6`,
where the predecessor is `E5=ER`. Thus `D2=SR`, with
consecutive target `D3=NL`.

The first east arrival `F1` on ten is supplied by the first
`SL` on seven; `F2` by `7:N2=NR`. There is a seven-step
path

```text
D3 -> 6:WR1 -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL
   -> 6:EL -> 7:N2=NR -> 10:F2.
```

Suppose `F2=EL`. The south predecessor of `D2` on ten
follows the square from `D2` to `D3` and then this
seven-step path. Its twelve-step first return to `F2`
forces it to be `F1=ER`, immediately before `F2`.

But this forces the eight-step first return

```text
7:first SL -> 10:F1=ER -> 9:D2=SR -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SL3
           -> 8:E6=EL -> 9:D3=NL -> 6:WR1 -> 7:W3=NL.
```

Its direction word is `E S W S E N W N`; the first
return to the starting physical cell is at the displayed
endpoint. Both endpoints turn left. Hence `F2=ER` in
both M and N.

Its south step completes an eight-step first return from
`D3=NL` to `D4=SR`.

#### Word M is impossible

In M the third north arrival `N3`, supplied by `6:WR2`,
is `NR`. It supplies the fifth east arrival `F5` on ten.
Consequently

```text
10:F2=ER -> 9:D4=SR -> 6:WR2 -> 7:N3=NR -> 10:F5
```

is a square return. It skips the intervening east arrivals
`F3,F4`, regardless of the turn at `F5`. This excludes M.

In N, `N3=W4=NL`, so this square is not available.
The rest of the note records the restrictions that do follow
for N.

#### The complete offset-nine word for N

The word on eight is already fixed:

```text
WR,WL,E1=ER,I=WL,WR,E2=EL,WR,E3=EL,WR,E4=EL,
WR,J=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

The eight-step return from `D1` through
`6:WL1,5:SR,2:WR,3:NR,6:ER2,5:SL2,E4=EL` cannot
have an `SR` source, whose consecutive target would be
`D2=NL`, contrary to `D2=SR`. Thus `D1=NL`, supplied
by the `WR` immediately before `E4`. It is the sixth
north arrival on nine, and its consecutive target is the
seventh north arrival `NR`, supplied by `E4`.

Before this first west departure, all five north arrivals
are `NR`; alternation forces a single `SL` between each
pair. The eighth north arrival is also `NR`, since the
remaining west departures are `D2=SR,D3=NL,D4=SR`,
and `D3` is the ninth north arrival, supplied by `E6`.
The square from `D2` to `D3` places `D2` after the
eighth north arrival. Since nine is non-wake, these facts
give the entire word

```text
9: NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,SL,NR,D1=NL,NR,SL,NR,SL,
   D2=SR,D3=NL,D4=SR,SL.
```

In particular, `D2,D4` are the seventh and eighth south
arrivals, and the final `SL` is the ninth.

#### The ending on ten and eleven

There are only three east arrivals on ten. The second,
`F2=ER`, feeds `D4`, the penultimate south arrival on
nine. If `F3=ER`, alternation between the consecutive
east right turns `F2,F3` would require a `WL` between
them, using two additional south departures. Only one is
available. Thus `F3=EL`.

The south predecessor of `D2` on ten has a twelve-step
first return to `F2=ER`, by the common path above. It
is therefore `Y=WL`, immediately before `F2`, and is
the seventh south departure on ten.

The last west arrival on eight is `J=WL`, immediately
before `E5=ER,E6=EL`. Let `P_J` be the last west
departure on eleven, feeding `J`. It has the eight-step
first return

```text
P_J -> 8:J=WL -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR -> 5:NR3
    -> 8:E5=ER -> 7:last SL -> 10:F3=EL
    -> north arrival on 11.
```

An `SR` source would create a further west departure
`NL`, so `P_J=NL`. Starting at its incoming predecessor
on ten gives an eight-step first return to `F3=EL`;
that predecessor is `X=WR`, immediately before `F3`.

The only south departure after `F2` is `K=WL`, supplying
the last `SL` on nine. Alternation and the non-wake
condition on ten therefore fix its final five events:

```text
10: ...,Y=WL,F2=ER,K=WL,X=WR,F3=EL.
```

There are no further north departures on ten. With eleven
non-wake and no west departures after `P_J`, its final
three events are consequently `P_J=NL,NR,SL`.

#### Square gaps and the last east arrival on twelve

The final four events `WL2,WR1,EL,WR2` on six have
successive four-step gaps, by the squares

```text
6:WL2 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL -> 9:D3=NL -> 6:WR1;
6:WR1 -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL -> 6:EL;
6:EL -> 7:N2=NR -> 10:F2=ER -> 9:D4=SR -> 6:WR2.
```

Let `H` be the last east arrival on twelve, supplied by
`10:K -> 9:last SL`. The equal-voltage paths from
`6:WL2` to `E6` and from `6:EL` to `F2` give
`tau(F2)=tau(E6)+8`. In upper inward time,

```text
tau(H)=tau(K)+2+N,
tau(F3)=tau(E5)+2+N.
```

Since `K>F2` and `E6>E5`, one obtains
`tau(H)>tau(F3)+8`. A right turn at `H` would feed
the last `SL` on eleven, after its last north arrival
from `F3`. It would be both the last east arrival and
last south departure on twelve, forcing a wake there.
But form R now has `h=1`, and upper offsets seven
through fifteen are non-wake. Hence `H=EL`.

At most one south departure on twelve can follow `H`.
Its word therefore ends at `H=EL`, or with `H=EL,WR,WL`.
These endings and the unresolved first east arrival on
ten remain to be handled. They do not close word N or
the entire R form.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-r-requires-wake-six-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_r_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_r_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15.md`.

### Form R requires a wake at six and one of two orders at seven

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: M and N are both excluded. See
`odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md` for the
completion of the upper form-R exclusion in family A.

Consider form R at the upper cap of family A:

```text
5: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
6: ER,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,[WR]^e,       e=omega_6.
```

The non-wake conditions at offsets seven and eight exclude
`e=0`. If `e=1`, only the following two words on seven
remain:

```text
M: SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,SL;
N: SR,SL,SR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL.
```

In M, the tail on eight is forced to be

```text
E3=ER,WL,WR,E4=EL,WR,WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

In N, the complete word on eight is

```text
WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL.
```

Neither continuation is excluded here. Thus form R in
family A now requires `h=1`, or `p=m-1`. Forms P and
Q from the two-`ER` normal form, family B, and the other
cap profiles remain open.

#### The prefix on seven

Let `Wi` be the west departures on seven and `Ni` its
north arrivals. The last-`EL` obstruction for word B
already forces `W3=NL`, supplied by `6:WR`. This is
the first north arrival `N1`; its square through
`4:second WL,3:last SL,6:EL` ends at `N2=NR`.
The earlier west departures must both be `SR`, giving
the fixed prefix

```text
7: W1=SR,SL,W2=SR,W3=NL,N2=NR,... .                 (1)
```

If `e=1`, there is one further north arrival, supplied
by the final `WR` on six. Write it as `N3`.

Label the six east arrivals on eight by `Ei`; they are
supplied by `5:NR1,SL1,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3`. The square
through `W1,4:first WR,5:NR1` forces `E1=ER`, the
second south departure on eight. Its first south departure
is a `WL` immediately before `E1`.

If `W4=SR`, then `E3=ER`. Otherwise its seven-step
path through `4:second WR,5:NL,2:WL,1:SL,4:EL,5:NR2`
would end at `E3=EL`. The south predecessor of `W4`
would then be `E2=ER`, forcing the square

```text
5:SL1 -> 8:E2=ER -> 7:W4=SR -> 4:second WR -> 5:NL
```

with two left-turn endpoints. Thus `W4=SR` is immediately
followed by `SL`. In that case `W5` cannot be `NL=N3`,
since no right turn is available between that `SL` and
the next `NL`. Hence `W5=SR` too.

If `W4` instead turns left, it must be `N3`, forcing
`e=1`. There are then no north arrivals left for `W5`,
so `W5=SR`. Alternation gives word N in the statement.

#### The possibilities with W4=SR

For `e=0`, append `SL,SR,SL,SR,SL` to (1). For
`e=1`, the remaining `NR=N3` can precede `W4`, lie
between `W4,W5`, or follow `W5`. Since seven is
non-wake, the respective tails after (1) are

```text
before W4:       SL,NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,SL;
between W4,W5:   SL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,SL;
after W5:        SL,SR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL.
```

The relevant south ordinals are

| Case | E3=ER | W5 | Total south arrivals on seven |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `e=0` | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| `N3` before `W4` | 7 | 8 | 9 |
| `N3` between `W4,W5` | 6 | 8 | 9 |
| `N3` after `W5` | 6 | 7 | 9 |

If `E5=EL`, the square through `W5` forces the
consecutive pair `E4=ER,E5=EL`, with `E4` supplying
`W5`. In the first, second, and fourth rows, its south
ordinal would be immediately after that of `E3=ER`.
Successive east right turns require an intervening `WL`,
so this is impossible. In those rows `E5=ER` instead,
at south ordinal eight, nine, or eight respectively.

In every row, `E6=EL`. In the first two, `E5` is
already the last south departure. In the fourth, another
east right turn after `E5` would require an intervening
`WL` and exceed the nine south departures. In the middle
row, `E5=ER` would already be the ninth south departure;
if `E5=EL`, a right turn at `E6` would be both the last
east arrival and last south departure, making eight wake.

#### A common obstruction at D1

The west departures on nine feed the offset-six west
arrivals in order:

```text
D1 -> first WL,       D2 -> second WL,
D3 -> WR,            [D4 -> final WR]^e.
```

Whenever `E4=EL`, `D1` has the eight-step first return

```text
D1 -> 6:first WL -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR
   -> 6:second ER -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=EL
   -> north arrival on 9.
```

Its source cannot be `SR`: its consecutive target would
be `D2=NL`, and the square from `E4=EL` through
`D2,6:second WL,5:SL3` to `E6=EL` would have two
left-turn endpoints. Therefore `D1=NL`, and its incoming
predecessor on eight must be `WR` immediately before `E4`.

In the `e=0`, `N3`-before-`W4`, and `N3`-after-`W5`
rows, the south ordinals force `E4=EL`. The `WL`
supplying `W5` immediately precedes `E5=ER`, by its
square return. It is the only south departure between
`E3` and `E5`, so `E3=ER,E4=EL` are consecutive.
There is no incoming `WR` before `E4`. The obstruction
therefore excludes all three rows, including the whole
`e=0` band.

#### The middle row

Suppose first that `E5=EL`. Then `E4=ER,E5=EL` are
consecutive. Extending the path from `D1` through `E4`
by the square through `W5,4:last WR,5:NR3,E5` gives a
twelve-step first return to a north arrival on nine.
An `SR` source would again force `D2=NL`, and the
square from `E5=EL` to `E6=EL` would have two left
endpoints. An `NL` source would have an incoming north
predecessor before `E4`; its eight-step first return to
`E4=ER` would force that predecessor to be `E3=EL`,
contrary to `E3=ER`. Hence `E5=ER`.

This is the ninth and last south departure. Thus `E6=EL`
immediately follows it. Since `E3=ER` is the sixth south
departure, `E4` must be `EL`: taking it to be `ER`
would put `E5` at south ordinal at least ten.

The common obstruction forces a `WR` immediately before
`E4`. The eighth south departure is a `WL` immediately
before `E5`. Alternation fixes the remaining tail as

```text
E3=ER,WL,WR,E4=EL,WR,WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

The corresponding word on seven is M. This tail is not
excluded here.

#### The word N on seven

In word N, `W4=NL=N3`. Its seven-step path to `E3`
cannot end at `ER`: that would return from `W4` to
`W5=SR`, whose square to `E5` would skip `E4`.
Hence `E3=EL`.

If `E5=EL`, the square through `W5` forces the
consecutive pair `E4=ER,E5=EL`, with `E4` the fourth
south departure. A right turn at `E6` would then be
the last east arrival and south departure, contradicting
the non-wake condition at eight. Thus `E6=EL`.

The twelve-step return from `D1` through `E4,E5` has
neither possible source turn. An `SR` source gives
`D2=NL` and the left/left square from `E5` to `E6`.
An `NL` source forces its incoming predecessor to be
`E3=EL` by the eight-step return to `E4=ER`; then

```text
5:NR2 -> 8:E3=EL -> 9:D1=NL -> 6:first WL -> 5:SR
```

is a square with two right-turn endpoints. Therefore
`E5=ER`, the fifth and last south departure, followed
immediately by `E6=EL`.

As in the U reduction, `E1=ER` is the second south
departure and the `WL` before `E5` is the fourth.
Taking `E2=ER` would require an intervening `WL`
after `E1`, putting `E2` at ordinal at least four,
where that fixed `WL` already occurs. Thus `E2=EL`.

Let `I` be the third south departure on eight, supplying
`7:W2=SR`. The nine-step path from `W2` through
`4:first WL,3:SR,0:WR,1:NR,4:ER,3:first SL,6:first ER,
5:SL1` ends at `8:E2`. Its upper inward voltage is
minus one. Including the initial south step gives

```text
tau(E2)=tau(I)+10+N > tau(I).
```

Hence the third south departure occurs before `E2`.
It cannot be `E4=ER`, and its only possible position
as `WL` in the alternating word is immediately after
`E1`, before the `WR` preceding `E2`. This fixes

```text
8: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL.
```

The final comparison is an upper-orientation comparison.
The later note `odd_skew_upper_a_r_middle_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`
excludes M and further restricts N. The full R form and
family A remain open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-single-er-exclusion-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_exclusion_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_exclusion_2026-09-14.md`.

### Excluding the single-ER upper cap in family A

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Family A cannot have exactly one `ER` port at upper
offset six. The reductions through U and B0 leave three
possible initial words on offset ten. Two give skipped
returns; the third contradicts the upper lifted-time order.

Consequently the upper `(1,2,2)` cap in family A must
have exactly two `ER` ports at offset six. Those branches,
family B, and the other cap profiles in the `(1,-3)` orbit
remain open.

The argument uses the established non-wake conditions at
upper offsets seven through eleven. Although it follows
paths to twelve, it does not need twelve to be non-wake.

#### Fixed notation and the first north arrival on nine

Use inward offsets and rotated headings. The previous
reductions leave

```text
5: NR1,SL1,SR,NL,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3
6: WR1,WL1,WR2,EL1,WR3,WL2,ER,WL3,WR4,EL2,WR5
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL
8: a=WR,C=WL,E1=ER,I=WL,b=WR,E2=EL,c=WR,E3=EL,
   d=WR,E4=EL,e=WR,J=WL,E5=ER,E6=EL.
```

The west departures `Di` on nine feed, in order,

```text
6:WR1,WL1,WR2,WR3,WL2,WL3,WR4,WR5.
```

The preceding note
`odd_skew_upper_a_u_unique_eighth_word_2026-09-14.md`
forces `D1=SR,D2=NL`. The latter is supplied by `8:b`,
the second north departure on eight. Its square through
`6:WL1,5:SL1,E2=EL` returns to the following `NR` on
nine. Write `M1=NR` for the first north arrival on nine,
supplied by `8:a`.

There are exactly two possible initial orders on nine:

```text
A: D1=SR,SL,M1=NR,D2=NL,NR,...;
B: M1=NR,SL,D1=SR,D2=NL,NR,... .                     (1)
```

Before `D2`, the only right turns available are `D1`
and `M1`. Alternation forces the single intervening `SL`
and excludes any additional events in these prefixes.

Let `Fi` be the east arrivals on ten. The first is supplied
by the first `SL` on seven, from `8:E1`; the second by
the first `NR` on seven, from `6:WR1`; and `F4` by the
second `SL` on seven, from `8:I`.

#### Three initial words on ten

The three-step path from `D1` through `6:WR1,7:first NR`
ends at `F2`. If `F2=EL`, the south predecessor of
`D1` on ten has a square return to it, forcing the
consecutive pair `F1=ER,F2=EL`.

In prefix A of (1), `D1` is the first south arrival,
so this pair starts the word on ten. In prefix B, it
is the second south arrival, forcing the prefix
`WR,WL,F1=ER,F2=EL`.

If `F2=ER`, its south step completes a square from
`D1=SR` to the following `SL`. This excludes prefix B,
where the immediate successor of `D1` is `D2=NL`.
In prefix A, `F2` is the second south departure on ten.
The first east arrival must be `F1=EL`: taking it to
be `ER` would require a `WL` between the first two
south departures. The south predecessor of `D1` is
then `WL`, immediately before `F2=ER` by its square
return. This fixes the third prefix below:

```text
I:   F1=ER,F2=EL,...;
II:  WR,WL,F1=ER,F2=EL,...;
III: WR,F1=EL,WR,WL,F2=ER,... .                      (2)
```

Cases I and III have prefix A on nine; case II has
prefix B.

#### The first three west departures on eleven

Let `P1,P2,P3` be the west departures on eleven feeding
`8:a=WR,C=WL,I=WL`. In cases I and II, `P2` has the
twelve-step first return

```text
P2 -> 8:C=WL -> 7:W1=SR -> 4:first WR -> 5:NR1
   -> 8:E1=ER -> 7:first SL -> 10:F1=ER -> 9:D1=SR
   -> 6:WR1 -> 7:first NR -> 10:F2=EL
   -> north arrival on 11.
```

In case III it instead has the eight-step first return
ending at the north arrival supplied by `F1=EL`:

```text
P2 -> 8:C=WL -> 7:W1=SR -> 4:first WR -> 5:NR1
   -> 8:E1=ER -> 7:first SL -> 10:F1=EL
   -> north arrival on 11.
```

These paths have no earlier visit to their starting cell.

#### Case I is impossible

In case I the target of the twelve-step return is the
first north arrival on eleven. Its source cannot be
`NL`, since there is no preceding north arrival to
supply it. Thus `P2=SR`, and its consecutive target
is the next west departure `P3=NL`.

It follows that

```text
10:F2=EL -> 11:P3=NL -> 8:I=WL -> 7:second SL -> 10:F4
```

is a square return. It skips `F3` in east-arrival order,
excluding case I.

#### Case II is impossible

Here the same target is the second north arrival on eleven.
If `P2=NL`, it must be the first north arrival. The earlier
west departure is consequently `P1=SR`, immediately
followed by `P2=NL`; no other north arrival or west
departure is available between them.

The path `P1 -> 8:a=WR -> 9:M1=NR` then reaches the
first east arrival `G1` on twelve, because case II has
prefix B on nine. If `G1=ER`, its south step would
return from `P1=SR` to `SL`, contrary to its immediate
successor `P2=NL`. Hence `G1=EL`. But the south
predecessor of `P1` on twelve would then have a square
return to this first east arrival, requiring an earlier
east arrival `ER`. This is impossible.

Thus `P2=SR`, with consecutive target `P3=NL` supplied
by `F2=EL`. The same square from `F2` to `F4` that
excluded case I excludes case II.

#### Case III forces one more square

In case III the eight-step return from `P2` ends at
the second north arrival on eleven, supplied by `F1=EL`.
Taking `P2=SR` would make its consecutive target
`P3=NL`, and the square from `F1` through `P3,I` and
the second `SL` on seven would end at `F4`, skipping
`F2,F3`. Therefore `P2=NL`, the first north arrival on
eleven. Again `P1=SR` immediately precedes it.

Prefix A on nine begins `D1=SR,SL,M1=NR`. Thus the
three-step path from `P1` through `8:a,9:M1` ends
at the second east arrival `G2` on twelve. It cannot
turn right: that would return from `P1=SR` to `SL`
instead of its immediate successor `P2=NL`. Hence
`G2=EL`.

The south predecessor of `P1` on twelve now has a square
return to `G2`. It must be the immediately preceding
east arrival `G1=ER`. In particular,

```text
12:G1=ER -> 11:P1=SR -> 8:a=WR -> 9:M1=NR -> 12:G2=EL,
tau(G2)=tau(G1)+4.                                  (3)
```

The first east arrival `G1` is supplied by the `SL`
after `D1` on nine. Its incoming predecessor is `F2=ER`.

#### The upper clock contradiction

Use upper inward lifted times. A vertical step followed
by an east step has voltage minus one and time increment
`2+N`. Therefore

```text
tau(G1)=tau(F2)+2+N,
tau(G2)=tau(a)+2+N.
```

Equation (3) gives `tau(a)=tau(F2)+4`. Since `F2` is
supplied by `6:WR1` through the first `NR` on seven,

```text
tau(a)=tau(6:WR1)+N+6.                              (4)
```

But the first `SL` on seven precedes its first `NR`.
They are supplied by `8:E1` and `6:WR1` respectively,
by vertical steps with zero voltage. Hence

```text
tau(E1)<tau(6:WR1).
```

The initial event `a` on eight also precedes `E1` there.
Consequently

```text
tau(a)<tau(E1)<tau(6:WR1),
```

contradicting (4) for every positive period. This excludes
case III and finishes the exclusion of U.

Words T and V and the other U orders were excluded in
the preceding notes. Thus there is no single-`ER` upper
cap in family A. The final comparison uses the upper
voltage sign; it is not an orientation-free contradiction.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-single-er-reduction-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_reduction_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_reduction_2026-09-14.md`.

### The remaining single-ER continuation at the upper cap of family A

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Use inward offsets and rotated heading labels at an upper cap.
Suppose a `(1,2,2)` cap has a wake at offset four, non-wakes at
offsets five, seven, and eight, and exactly one `ER` port at
offset six. Then offset six is wake, and offsets five through
seven have the necessary words

```text
5: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
6: WR,WL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL.
```

In particular, for family A, the single-`ER` branch requires
`h=1`, equivalently `p=m-1`. This last continuation is not
excluded here. Nor are the upper two-`ER` branches.

The proof uses only first same-cell returns and stable order.
It has no longitudinal voltage inequality, so it applies with
the same inward words at either cap.

#### A left-return obstruction for word B

The two-sided normal form with a wake at four is

```text
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR
5 B: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,[SL]^(1-c).
```

Let `W1,...,W5` be the west departures on offset seven,
feeding `4:WR1,WL1,WL2,WR2,WR3`. Let `Ei` be the east
arrivals on offset eight, supplied by the east departures
of word B on offset five. In particular, `E2` is supplied
by `5:SL1`, and `E3,E4,E5` by `5:NR2,SL2,NR3`.

Suppose the last east arrival on offset six is `L=EL`.
The square from `W3` through `4:WL2,3:last SL,L` ends
at a north arrival on offset seven. If `W3=SR`, its
consecutive target must be `W4=NL`.

The seven-step path

```text
W4 -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL -> 2:WL -> 1:SL
   -> 4:EL -> 5:NR2 -> E3
```

cannot end at `E3=ER`. Its south step would return from
`W4=NL` to `W5=SR`, after which the square through
`4:WR3,5:NR3` to `E5` would skip `E4` in east-arrival
order. Thus `E3=EL`.

The south predecessor of `W3` on offset eight follows
the square to `W4` and then this seven-step path. The
twelve-step first return to `E3=EL` forces that predecessor
to be `E2=ER`. It follows that

```text
5:SL1 -> 8:E2=ER -> 7:W3=SR -> 4:WL2 -> 3:last SL
      -> 6:L=EL -> 7:W4=NL -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL
```

is an eight-step first return. Its direction word is
`E S W S E N W N`. Both endpoints turn left, a
contradiction.

Consequently `W3=NL`. Its square return now forces its
incoming predecessor on offset six to be `WR`, immediately
preceding `L=EL`. This conclusion applies to any word B
with the stated last east arrival, not just the single-`ER`
count row.

The single-`ER` reduction in
`odd_skew_fat122_four_wake_single_er_2026-09-13.md` leaves
words T and U when `c=0`, and V when `c=1`. In T and V,
the last `EL` is immediately preceded by `ER`, not `WR`.
They are therefore excluded at either cap. Only U remains
when `c=0`:

```text
6: WR1,WL1,WR2,EL1,WR3,WL2,ER,WL3,WR4,EL2,[WR5]^e.
```

Here `e=omega_6`. In particular, there is no single-`ER`
continuation at either cap when `c=1`.

#### The prefix on offset seven for U

Write `Ni` for the north arrivals on offset seven. The
earlier return reduction gives `W2=NL=N2`, with consecutive
target `N3=NR`. The preceding section gives `W3=NL=N5`,
with consecutive target `N6=NR`.

The path from `W1` through `4:WR1,5:NR1` reaches the
first east arrival `E1` on offset eight. The square from
its south predecessor forces `E1=ER`: an `EL` endpoint
would require an earlier east arrival as its source. Its
south step then forces `W1=SR` to be immediately followed
by `SL`.

The first north arrival `N1` cannot precede this pair.
If it did, there would be no right turn available between
the `SL` after `W1` and `W2=NL=N2`. Hence alternation
and the north order fix the prefix through `N6`:

```text
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,... .                  (1)
```

Its first nine events contain `W1,W2,W3` and the first
six north arrivals. If `e=1`, the only remaining north
arrival is `N7`, supplied by `6:WR5`.

#### The two remaining west departures

If `W4=SR`, then `E3=ER`. Indeed, taking `E3=EL` in
the seven-step path from `W4` would force its south
predecessor to be `E2=ER`. But then

```text
5:SL1 -> 8:E2=ER -> 7:W4=SR -> 4:WR2 -> 5:NL
```

would be a square with two left-turn endpoints. Thus
`W4=SR` is followed immediately by `SL`.

In this case `W5` cannot be `NL`: it would have to be
`N7`, but no right turn is available between the `SL`
after `W4` and that `NL`. Therefore `W5=SR` as well.

Assume this case until the final section. If `e=0`,
append `SL,SR,SL,SR,SL` to (1). If `e=1`, append one
of the following tails, according to the position of `N7`:

```text
before W4:       SL,NR,SL,SR,SL,SR,SL;
between W4,W5:   SL,SR,SL,NR,SL,SR,SL;
after W5:        SL,SR,SL,SR,SL,NR,SL.
```

All words end with `SL`, since offset seven is non-wake.
The resulting south ordinals are

| Case | Ordinal of E3=ER | Ordinal of W5 | Total south arrivals on seven |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `e=0` | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| `N7` before `W4` | 7 | 8 | 9 |
| `N7` between `W4,W5` | 6 | 8 | 9 |
| `N7` after `W5` | 6 | 7 | 9 |

An `E5=EL` would force `E4=ER` at the south ordinal
feeding `W5`. In the first, second, and fourth rows this
is immediately after the ordinal of `E3=ER`, which is
impossible for successive east right turns. Hence `E5=ER`
in those rows. In the first two rows it is the last south
departure, and in the fourth it is the eighth.

In all four rows, `E6=EL`. In the first two there is no
south departure left for another `ER`. In the fourth,
two consecutive east right turns `E5,E6` would require
an intervening `WL`, exceeding the nine south departures.
In the third row, either `E5=ER` is already the ninth
south departure, or `E5=EL` follows `E4=ER` at ordinal
eight. In the latter case `E6=ER` would be both the last
east arrival and the last south departure, forcing offset
eight to be wake, contrary to the hypothesis.

#### The sixth west departure on offset nine must be NL

Label the west departures on offset nine by `Di`. They
feed the offset-six west arrivals in the order

```text
WR1,WL1,WR2,WR3,WL2,WL3,WR4,[WR5]^e.
```

Suppose `D6=SR`. Its square through `6:WL3,5:SL3,E6=EL`
forces the consecutive target `D7=NL`. The latter has
the seven-step path

```text
D7 -> 6:WR4 -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:WL2 -> 3:last SL
   -> 6:EL2 -> 7:N6=NR -> 10:F6.
```

If `F6=EL`, the south predecessor of `D6` on offset ten
has a twelve-step first return to `F6`, forcing it to
be `F5=ER`. But `F5` is supplied by `7:N4=NR`, from
`6:WR3`. The resulting square

```text
6:WR3 -> 7:N4=NR -> 10:F5=ER -> 9:D6=SR -> 6:WL3
```

skips `WL2,ER` in the offset-six word. Thus `F6=ER`.
Its south step returns from `D7=NL` to another west
departure `D8=SR`. This already excludes `e=0`.

For `e=1`, `D8` feeds `6:WR5`, then `7:N7=NR`, then
an east arrival `Fq` on offset ten. In the three displayed
tails, `q` is respectively eight, nine, or ten. The square

```text
10:F6=ER -> 9:D8=SR -> 6:WR5 -> 7:N7=NR -> 10:Fq
```

would force `Fq=EL` immediately after `F6`, skipping
at least `F7`. This is impossible. Therefore `D6=NL`.
Its incoming predecessor on offset eight must be `WR`,
immediately preceding `E6=EL`.

In the `e=0` and `N7`-before-`W4` rows, `E5=ER` is
the last south departure, so `E5,E6` are consecutive.
The same holds in the middle row if `E5=ER`. Their
right-turn predecessor is not `WR`, excluding these cases.

#### The middle row with E5=EL

Here `E4=ER,E5=EL` are consecutive. The fifth west
departure on offset nine has the twelve-step first return

```text
D5 -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR -> 6:ER
   -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=ER -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:WR3
   -> 5:NR3 -> 8:E5=EL -> north arrival on 9.
```

If `D5=NL`, its north predecessor on offset eight lies
before `E4`. The initial eight-step return to `E4=ER`
would force that predecessor to be `E3=EL`, contrary
to `E3=ER`. Hence `D5=SR`, with consecutive target
`D6=NL` supplied by `8:E5=EL`.

But the square from `E5` through `D6,6:WL3,5:SL3`
ends at `E6=EL`. Both endpoints turn left. This excludes
the middle row.

#### The row with N7 after W5

Here `E3=ER` is the sixth south departure and `E5=ER`
the eighth. Their intervening east arrival is `E4=EL`;
the square through `W5` forces its south predecessor
`J=WL` immediately before `E5`. Hence the tail begins

```text
E3=ER,E4=EL,WR,J=WL,E5=ER.
```

Now `D5` has the eight-step first return through
`6:WL2,5:SR,2:WR,3:NR,6:ER,5:SL2,E4=EL` to a
north arrival on offset nine. An `NL` source would
force an incoming `WR` immediately before `E4`, where
the stable predecessor is instead `E3=ER`. Thus
`D5=SR`, with consecutive target `D6=NL` supplied by
`E4=EL`. Its square through `6:WL3,5:SL3` to `E6=EL`
again has two left-turn endpoints. This excludes the last
row with `W4=SR`.

#### The one remaining order

Therefore `W4=NL`. The only north arrival available to
it is `N7`, so `e=1`. There are no north arrivals left
for `W5`, which must be `SR`. Alternation, the prefix
(1), and the non-wake condition at offset seven give

```text
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL.
```

Its `W4=NL` also forces `E3=EL` by the skipped-east-
arrival square used at the start of the note. In family A
the wake at upper offset six is equivalent to `h=1`, so
this single-`ER` branch has `p=m-1`. The remaining order
is subsequently excluded in
`odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_exclusion_2026-09-14.md`.
The two-`ER` branch is separate and remains open.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-two-er-normal-form-2026-09-15-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_two_er_normal_form_2026-09-15

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_two_er_normal_form_2026-09-15.md`.

### Three two-ER forms at the upper cap of family A

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Subsequent status: form R is excluded at the upper cap
of family A in `odd_skew_upper_a_r_exclusion_2026-09-15.md`.
Only P and Q remain there. P still permits `e=0` or
`1`; Q now requires `e=1`, by
`odd_skew_upper_a_q_requires_wake_six_2026-09-15.md`.
The three-form classification below is the earlier local
reduction, which also applies without that upper-cap
exclusion's hypotheses.

The upper cap in family A now has exactly two `ER` ports
at offset six. Let `e=omega_6`. Its remaining possibilities
through offsets five and six are

| Form | Offset five | Offset six |
|---|---|---|
| P | `SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL` | `ER,EL,ER,WL,WR,WL,[WR]^e` |
| Q | `NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL` | `WR,WL,ER,EL,ER,WL,[WR]^e` |
| R | `NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL` | `ER,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,[WR]^e` |

Both values `e=0,1` remain in each form, giving six
necessary local bands, not six realizations. In family A,
`e=1` means `h=1`, or `p=m-1`; `e=0` means `h>=2`.
These bands and family B remain open.

The reduction below uses only same-cell first returns. In
fact, the three-form statement holds at either `(1,2,2)`
cap with `omega_4=1,omega_5=0` and two `ER` ports at
offset six. It does not use the later non-wake band of
family A or a longitudinal time comparison.

#### The square through offset five

The two-sided normal form gives

```text
4: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR
5 A: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL
5 B: NR,SL,SR,NL,NR,SL,NR,SL.
```

With two `ER` ports, the complete count row on six is

```text
(ER,EL,WR,WL)=(2,1,1+e,2).
```

The south predecessor of `5:SR` has the square return
through `5:SR,2:WR,3:NR` to the second east arrival
on six. The source is the first south departure for word
A and the second for word B. Its source and target are
consecutive stable events with opposite turns.

For A, if the second east arrival is `EL`, the word
starts `ER,EL`. The remaining east right turn can occur
before both remaining `WL` ports, between them, or after
both. If the second east arrival is `ER`, its predecessor
is the first south departure `WL`, preceded by the first
east arrival `EL`. This forces the prefix
`WR,EL,WR,WL,ER` and uses both available `WR` ports,
so `e=1`.

For B, an `EL` at the second east arrival forces the
prefix `WR,WL,ER,EL`. If the second east arrival is
`ER`, its immediately preceding `WL` is the second south
departure. The first east arrival must also be `ER`:
an earlier `EL` and two earlier `WL` ports would require
three earlier `WR` ports, but at most two are available.
The prefix is therefore `ER,WL,ER`.

Alternation gives the following complete list before further
return restrictions:

| Name | Offset five | Offset six | Allowed e |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | A | `ER,EL,ER,WL,WR,WL,[WR]^e` | 0 or 1 |
| P1 | A | `ER,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,[WR]^e` | 0 or 1 |
| P2 | A | `ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER` | 1 |
| P3 | A | `WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,WL,ER` | 1 |
| Q0 | B | `WR,WL,ER,EL,ER,WL,[WR]^e` | 0 or 1 |
| Q1 | B | `WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,ER` | 1 |
| Q2 | B | `ER,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,[WR]^e` | 0 or 1 |
| Q3 | B | `ER,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,[WR]^e` | 0 or 1 |

Counting the separate values of `e`, there are thirteen
initial combinations. The following returns exclude seven.

#### The initial ER,EL pair excludes P1 and P2

Let `W1,...,W5` be the west departures on seven, feeding
the west arrivals `WR,WL,WL,WR,WR` on four. The fixed
path from `W2` starts

```text
W2 -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR -> 1:NR -> 4:ER
   -> 3:first SL -> first east arrival on 6.
```

For P0, P1, and P2, it continues through
`5:SR,2:WR,3:NR` to the second east arrival `EL`, then
north. This twelve-step first return ends at the first
north arrival on seven. Thus its source is `W2=SR`
and its consecutive target is `W3=NL`.

The square

```text
6:EL -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL
     -> third east arrival on 6
```

must consequently join consecutive stable events. It skips
`WR,WL` in P1 and two such pairs in P2. Both are
impossible. P0 is not excluded by this square.

#### The same square excludes Q1

For Q0 and Q1, the first south departure on six is
`WL`; the first `ER` feeds `5:SR`. The return from
`W2` again ends at the second east arrival `EL`, but
this is now the second north departure on six.

If `W2=NL`, it would be the first north arrival on seven,
supplied by the initial `6:WR`. The eight-step first return

```text
6:first WR -> 7:W2=NL -> 4:first WL -> 3:SR -> 0:WR
           -> 1:NR -> 4:ER -> 3:first SL -> 6:first ER
```

would have two right-turn endpoints. Hence `W2=SR`,
with consecutive target `W3=NL`. The square from `6:EL`
through `W3,4:second WL,3:last SL` to the third east
arrival now skips `WR,WL` in Q1. This excludes Q1,
but not Q0.

#### The first-EL word P3 is impossible

For P3, the return from `W2` ends at the first east
arrival `EL` on six, the second north arrival on seven.
If `W2=SR`, its target is `W3=NL`, and the same square
from this `EL` to the last `ER` on six skips intervening
stable visits. Thus `W2=NL`, the first north arrival on
seven. The earlier west departure is `W1=SR`, immediately
followed by `W2=NL`.

Label the east arrivals on eight by `Ei`. Since the word
on five is A, the three-step path from `W1` through
`4:first WR,5:first NR` ends at `E2`. It cannot turn
right: its south step would return from `W1=SR` to
`SL`, rather than its immediate successor `W2=NL`.
Hence `E2=EL`, with consecutive predecessor `E1=ER`.
They are the initial two stable events on eight, because
`E1` supplies the first south arrival `W1` on seven.

The four west arrivals on six in P3 are `WR,WR,WL,WL`.
Let their matching west departures on nine be `D1,...,D4`.
The third has the twelve-step first return

```text
D3 -> 6:first WL -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR -> 6:second ER
   -> 5:first SL -> 8:E1=ER -> 7:W1=SR -> 4:first WR
   -> 5:first NR -> 8:E2=EL -> first north arrival on 9.
```

Its source cannot be `NL` before the first north arrival.
Thus `D3=SR`, with consecutive target `D4=NL`. The
square

```text
8:E2=EL -> 9:D4=NL -> 6:second WL -> 5:second SL -> 8:E4
```

skips `E3` in east-arrival order. This excludes P3.

#### The final EL excludes Q2

The left-return obstruction in
`odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_reduction_2026-09-14.md`
applies to word B whenever the last east arrival on six
is `EL`, regardless of the number of `ER` ports. It
forces `W3=NL` and an incoming `WR` immediately before
that last `EL` on six.

For completeness, an `SR` at `W3` would return to
`W4=NL`. The path from `W4` to `E3` then forces
`E3=EL`, with `E2=ER` supplying `W3`. The resulting
eight-step first return

```text
5:SL1 -> 8:E2=ER -> 7:W3=SR -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL
      -> 6:last EL -> 7:W4=NL -> 4:second WR -> 5:NL
```

has two left-turn endpoints. Hence `W3=NL`; the square
through `4:second WL,3:last SL` forces the claimed
immediately preceding `WR` on six.

In Q2 the last `EL` is instead immediately preceded by
`ER`, so Q2 is impossible. Q3 has the required `WR`
predecessor and is not excluded by this argument.

Only P0, Q0, and Q3 remain. These are the forms P, Q,
and R in the statement. Their higher continuations still
require separate arguments.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-u-eighth-words-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_u_eighth_words_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_u_eighth_words_2026-09-14.md`.

### Five offset-eight words and a left turn at offset twelve

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Continue with the upper U branch of family A. The preceding
return arguments fix offsets five through seven and the final
events through offset eleven. This note reduces offset eight
to five explicit words and forces the last east arrival on
offset twelve to turn left. The U branch remains open.

#### The second east arrival on eight turns left

Use the labels from
`odd_skew_upper_a_u_forced_tail_2026-09-14.md`. There
are six east arrivals `E1,...,E6` and five south departures
on eight. The following are already forced:

```text
E1=ER at south ordinal 2;
E3=EL;
J=WL at south ordinal 4;
E5=ER at south ordinal 5;
the consecutive final block J,E5,E6=EL.
```

If `E2=ER`, alternation between the consecutive east arrivals
`E1=ER,E2=ER` would require an intervening `WL`. This
would put `E2` at south ordinal at least four. But `E2`
precedes `J`, which is already the fourth south departure.
Thus `E2=EL`.

The first south departure is `WL`, immediately before the
first east arrival `E1=ER`. Since no east arrival precedes
it, the word begins exactly `WR,WL,ER`.

#### The five words

Only the turn at `E4` remains to be chosen among the east
arrivals. If `E4=ER`, it is the third south departure.
Alternation fixes the complete ten-visit word

```text
A: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,EL,ER,WL,ER,EL.
```

If `E4=EL`, the third south departure is another `WL`,
denoted `I`. Between `E1` and the final `J` there are
exactly the four left turns `E2,E3,E4,I`, with `E2,E3,E4`
in their fixed order. Every intervening right turn is `WR`.
The four positions for `I` therefore give exactly

```text
B0: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL;
B1: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,WL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL;
B2: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL;
B3: WR,WL,ER,EL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,WR,WL,ER,EL.
```

These fourteen-visit words and A are necessary alternatives,
not realized traces. They use only the stable turn order and
the already forced square through the last west departure
on seven. The subsequent return-order argument in
`odd_skew_upper_a_u_unique_eighth_word_2026-09-14.md`
excludes A and B1 through B3, leaving only B0.

There is one useful distinction at offset nine. In the four
B words, `E4=EL`; the eighth-step first return from `D5`
through `6:WL2,5:SR,2:WR,3:NR,6:ER,5:SL2,E4` cannot
have an `SR` source, because its consecutive target would
be `D6=NL`, whereas `D6=SR` is already forced. Hence
`D5=NL` in B0 through B3. Its incoming predecessor on
eight is the `WR` immediately before `E4`.

#### Comparing the two upper clocks

The endings from the preceding note are

```text
9:  ...,D6=SR,D7=NL,D8=SR,SL;
10: ...,F6=ER,K=WL,X=WR,F7=EL;
11: ...,P_J=NL,NR,SL.
```

The final `NR` on eleven is supplied by `F7`. Let `H`
be the last east arrival on twelve; it is supplied by the
last `SL` on nine, whose incoming predecessor is `10:K`.

Write `tau` for stable lifted time in the upper inward
orientation. A south step followed by an east step has
voltage minus one, so its time increment is `2+N`, not
the lower-cap value `2-N`. The relevant identities are

```text
tau(F7) = tau(E5)+2+N,
tau(H)  = tau(K)+2+N.
```

The three consecutive square gaps on offset six give
`tau(6:EL2)=tau(6:WL3)+8`. Since `E6` and `F6` are
supplied by `6:WL3` and `6:EL2`, each by a two-step
path with the same voltage, this yields

```text
tau(F6)=tau(E6)+8.
```

Now `F6<K` and `E5<E6`. Consequently

```text
tau(H)-tau(F7) = tau(K)-tau(E5)
               > tau(E6)+8-tau(E5)
               > 8.                                (1)
```

No bound on a black lifetime is used here.

#### The last east arrival on twelve is EL

If `H=ER`, its south step would reach eleven after its
last north arrival, which has time `tau(F7)+1`. By (1)
the arrival would be strictly later. The only event on
eleven after that north arrival is its final `SL`.
Thus `H` would be the last south departure on twelve
as well as its last east arrival.

An alternating word cannot have a left turn after such
an `ER`: an `EL` would be another east arrival, and a
`WL` another south departure. The word would end in
`ER`, making offset twelve wake. But the U branch has
`h=1` in family A, so every offset from seven through
fifteen is non-wake. This contradiction proves `H=EL`.

There is at most one south departure on twelve after `H`,
since it too would have to feed the unique final `SL`
on eleven. Because `H` is the last east arrival and twelve
is non-wake, the word on twelve therefore ends either
at `H=EL`, or with the block

```text
H=EL,WR,WL.
```

These two endings and the remaining offset-eight word B0
still require higher return arguments. They do not exclude
U, the upper two-`ER` branches of A, or family B.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-u-forced-tail-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_u_forced_tail_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_u_forced_tail_2026-09-14.md`.

### Forced returns above the remaining upper U word

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

Consider the remaining single-`ER` word at the upper cap of
family A, as reduced in
`odd_skew_upper_a_single_er_reduction_2026-09-14.md`.
Offsets seven through eleven are non-wake. This note proves:

* The fifth and sixth east arrivals on offset eight are the
  consecutive final pair `E5=ER,E6=EL`.
* The final four events on offset nine are `SR,NL,SR,SL`.
* The final four events on offset ten are `ER,WL,WR,EL`.
* The final three events on offset eleven are `NL,NR,SL`.
* The final four offset-six visits `WL,WR,EL,WR` have
  successive gaps of exactly four steps.

These are necessary restrictions. The remaining U word is
not excluded here. No search or longitudinal time inequality
is used in deriving the forced returns.

#### Fixed rows and matching labels

Use inward offsets and rotated headings throughout. The fixed
rows are

```text
5: NR1,SL1,SR,NL,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3
6: WR1,WL1,WR2,EL1,WR3,WL2,ER,WL3,WR4,EL2,WR5
7: SR,SL,NR,NL,NR,SL,NR,NL,NR,NL,SR,SL.
```

Label the west departures on seven by `W1,...,W5` and its
north arrivals by `N1,...,N7`. In particular,

```text
W3=NL=N5,       N6=NR,       W4=NL=N7,       W5=SR.
```

There are six east arrivals `Ei` on eight. They are supplied
by `5:NR1,SL1,NR2,SL2,NR3,SL3`, in that order. The preceding
reduction gives `E1=ER,E3=EL`. There are five south departures
on eight; `W5` is the fourth south arrival on seven, followed
by its last `SL`.

The eight west departures `Di` on nine feed

```text
6:WR1,WL1,WR2,WR3,WL2,WL3,WR4,WR5.
```

Let `F1,...,F7` be the east arrivals on ten. We will use
that `F5` is supplied by `7:N4=NR`, from `6:WR3`; `F6`
by `7:N6=NR`, from `6:EL2`; and `F7` by the final `SL`
on seven.

#### E5 cannot turn left

Suppose `E5=EL`. The square through `W5` forces the
consecutive pair `E4=ER,E5=EL`, with `E4` the fourth south
departure on eight. If `E6=ER`, it would be both the last
east arrival and the last south departure. No left turn could
follow it, so offset eight would be wake. Thus `E6=EL`.

There is a twelve-step first return

```text
D5 -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR -> 6:ER
   -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=ER -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR
   -> 5:NR3 -> 8:E5=EL -> north arrival on 9.
```

If `D5=SR`, its consecutive target is `D6=NL`, supplied
by `8:E5=EL`. The square

```text
8:E5=EL -> 9:D6=NL -> 6:WL3 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL
```

would have two left-turn endpoints. Hence `D5=NL`.

Its incoming predecessor `X` on eight is the north departure
immediately preceding `E5`, and therefore precedes `E4`.
The first part of the long return gives the eight-step first
return

```text
8:X -> 9:D5=NL -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SR -> 2:WR -> 3:NR
    -> 6:ER -> 5:SL2 -> 8:E4=ER.
```

Its source must be `X=E3=EL`. But then

```text
5:NR2 -> 8:E3=EL -> 9:D5=NL -> 6:WL2 -> 5:SR
```

is a square with two right-turn endpoints. This excludes
`E5=EL`.

Consequently `E5=ER` supplies the fifth and last south arrival
on seven. The only later east arrival is `E6=EL`, immediately
after `E5`. No event can follow `E6`: the only possible right
turn would be `WR`, and no left turn would remain to make
offset eight non-wake. Thus `E5,E6` are its final two events.

#### The last three west departures on nine

The square from `D6` through `6:WL3,5:SL3,E6=EL` cannot
have an `NL` source. It would require an incoming `WR`
immediately before `E6`, where the predecessor is `E5=ER`.
Therefore `D6=SR`, with consecutive target `D7=NL`.

The next forced path is

```text
D7 -> 6:WR4 -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL
   -> 6:EL2 -> 7:N6=NR -> 10:F6.
```

If `F6=EL`, the south predecessor of `D6` on ten would
have a twelve-step first return to `F6`. It would be
`F5=ER`. The square

```text
6:WR3 -> 7:N4=NR -> 10:F5=ER -> 9:D6=SR -> 6:WL3
```

would then skip `WL2,ER` in the offset-six word. Hence
`F6=ER`. Its south step completes the eight-step first
return from `D7=NL` to `D8=SR`.

The north arrival `D7` is supplied by the final event `E6`
on eight. There are no later north arrivals on nine and no
west departures after `D8`. Since nine is non-wake, it
therefore ends exactly

```text
D6=SR,D7=NL,D8=SR,SL.                               (1)
```

#### The final east arrival on ten

The south departure `F6=ER` feeds `D8`, the penultimate
south arrival in (1). Only one further south departure is
available. If `F7=ER`, alternation between the consecutive
east arrivals `F6,F7` would require a `WL` between them.
That would consume two further south departures. Thus
`F7=EL`.

Let `J` be the south departure on eight feeding `W5`.
The square through `4:last WR,5:NR3` forces `J=WL`,
immediately before `E5=ER`. It is the last west arrival
on eight, since that word ends `J,E5,E6`.

Let `P_J` be its matching west departure on eleven. The
eight-step first return

```text
P_J -> 8:J=WL -> 7:W5=SR -> 4:last WR -> 5:NR3
    -> 8:E5=ER -> 7:last SL -> 10:F7=EL
    -> north arrival on 11
```

cannot have an `SR` source: its `NL` target would be a
further west departure after the last one. Hence `P_J=NL`.
Its incoming predecessor `X` on ten is the north departure
immediately preceding `F7`. Starting one step earlier gives
an eight-step first return from `X` to `F7=EL`, so `X=WR`
immediately precedes `F7`.

Between `F6=ER` and this `WR` there must be a left turn.
The only available one is the final south departure `K=WL`.
Alternation therefore fixes the ending on ten as

```text
F6=ER,K=WL,X=WR,F7=EL.                              (2)
```

There are no events after `F7`: every east arrival and south
departure has occurred, and ten is non-wake. The last north
arrival on eleven is consequently the `NR` supplied by
`F7`, immediately following `P_J=NL`. Since eleven is
non-wake and has no further west departures, its final
three events are `P_J=NL,NR,SL`.

#### Three consecutive square gaps on six

The forced matches give three first square returns:

```text
6:WL3 -> 5:SL3 -> 8:E6=EL -> 9:D7=NL -> 6:WR4;
6:WR4 -> 7:W3=NL -> 4:second WL -> 3:last SL -> 6:EL2;
6:EL2 -> 7:N6=NR -> 10:F6=ER -> 9:D8=SR -> 6:WR5.
```

These are the final four stable visits on six. If the first
has time `T`, their times are exactly `T,T+4,T+8,T+12`.
There is also the square

```text
7:N6=NR -> 10:F6=ER -> 9:D8=SR -> 6:WR5 -> 7:W4=NL.
```

All these returns have zero displacement and remain valid
with rotated inward headings. They further restrict the U
continuation but do not yet contradict it.

<a id="source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-a-u-unique-eighth-word-2026-09-14-md"></a>

## odd_skew_upper_a_u_unique_eighth_word_2026-09-14

Source: `research-notes/odd_skew_upper_a_u_unique_eighth_word_2026-09-14.md`.

### The upper U branch has one possible offset-eight word

Later result (21 September 2026): [the upper fat-cap exclusion](05_Negative_skew.md#source-research-notes-odd-skew-upper-fat122-exclusion-2026-09-21-md) closes family A, including the upper-cap branch studied here. The derivation and intermediate status below record the earlier reduction.

The five words in
`odd_skew_upper_a_u_eighth_words_2026-09-14.md` reduce
to B0 alone:

```text
8: WR,WL,ER,WL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,EL,WR,WL,ER,EL.
```

In addition, the first two west departures on offset nine
must be `D1=SR,D2=NL`. The U branch itself remains open;
this note does not exclude family A's upper two-`ER`
branches, family B, or the other cap profiles.

#### The third south departure precedes E3

Keep the fixed U words and labels from the preceding notes.
Let `I` be the third south departure on offset eight. It
feeds the `SL` between `7:N3=NR` and `7:N4=NR`. Those
north arrivals are supplied by `6:EL1` and `6:WR3`.
North and south steps have zero voltage, so their order gives

